Update this file at the end of every development session.

the repository as it exists today.

## Architecture Decisions

Each ADR is marked **holds**, **amended** or **superseded**. Read the amended
ones before acting on the original wording.

### ADR-001 — Technology Stack — holds
**Decision:** Next.js + Supabase + Vercel  
**Reason:** Free tier covers the product, one language (TypeScript) throughout, Supabase handles auth + database + storage.  
**Alternatives considered:** React + Node + PostgreSQL (more complex), Django + React (two languages)  
**Note:** the stack has not changed since September; only the Next major version moved (15 → 16).

### ADR-002 — Multi-tenancy Model — holds
**Decision:** Shared database, application-level isolation via `organization_id`.  
**Reason:** Simpler to implement; every query filters by `organization_id`.  
**Future:** database-level enforcement (Phase 14).

### ADR-003 — RLS Disabled / Hand-Managed During Development — amended
**Decision:** Row Level Security is off or partial on all tables.  
**Reason:** @supabase/ssr 0.12.x + the App Router session cookie produced 403s on INSERT; policies were correct but the session never reached the database.  
**Amended (Oct 2026):** policies are edited by hand in the Supabase dashboard, never in this repo. App-level `organization_id` filtering is the active isolation mechanism. This is the reason new features ship client-side (ADR-013).  
**Affected tables:** all business tables — organizations, org_members, gst_registrations, profiles, customers, vendors, products, invoices, invoice_items, purchase_bills, purchase_bill_items, expenses, payments, stock_movements, invoice_sequences, gst_return_periods, warehouses, plus everything added later (see "Data model" below).

### ADR-004 — No Direct GST Filing — holds
**Decision:** The app prepares return data; it does not file.  
**Reason:** Direct GSTN integration requires a GSP (GST Suvidha Provider) contract.  
**Amended:** this is now enforced in code by ADR-016 and ADR-017 rather than left as a future concern.

### ADR-005 — Client-Side GST Calculation — holds
**Location:** `src/lib/gst-engine.ts` — the same engine drives invoices, GSTR-1, GSTR-3B, GSTR-9, registers, the ledger and the financial statements.

### ADR-006 — Invoice Data Immutability — holds
`invoice_items` stores `item_name`, `hsn_sac`, `rate`, `gst_rate` as copies taken at creation time. Quotation, credit-note and debit-note line items follow the same rule.

### ADR-007 — Invoice Number Format — amended
**Decision:** `INV-{FY}-{0000}` (e.g. `INV-2026-27-0001`), one row per org per FY in `invoice_sequences`.  
**Amended:** quotations, credit notes and debit notes each got their own sequence table in September, and the prefix is user-configurable in Settings. Offline drafts get a temporary `OFF-` number and are renumbered on replay (ADR-015).

### ADR-008 — Financial Year (India) — amended
April → March. `getFinancialYear()` lives in `gst-engine.ts:175`.  
**Amended (Oct 2026):** `src/lib/targets.ts` is now the shared FY calendar (`fyLabel`, `fyMonths`, `monthOf`, `SEASONAL_WEIGHTS`) and every period-aware feature imports it. Trap: `iso(year, month, day)` takes a **1-based** month while `fyMonth()` returns a **0-based** index into the FY — mixing them silently shifts everything one month.

### ADR-009 — No proxy/middleware — holds
`proxy.ts` was deleted (the cookie session was rewritten server-side and broke inserts). Auth is read client-side through `@supabase/ssr`.

### ADR-010 — URL-Based ID Resolution — holds
`src/app/(app)/invoices/[id]/page.tsx:28` still reads `window.location.pathname.split('/')`.  
**Amended:** the same file now also needs the `XPageInner` + `<Suspense>` split whenever it uses `useSearchParams`, otherwise prerendering fails.

### ADR-011 — Draft Invoice Cannot Be Marked Paid — holds.

### ADR-012 — Expense GST Calculation (Tax-Inclusive) — holds
`gst_amount = total − (total / (1 + rate/100))`.

### ADR-013 — Client-Side First, No Migrations — holds (new, Oct 2026)
**Decision:** A new feature must ride on existing tables plus local storage. No `supabase/migrations`, no new columns requested from the dashboard unless the feature is impossible without one.  
**Reason:** the schema is hand-managed in the Supabase dashboard (ADR-003); a migration file in the repo would be a second source of truth that nothing applies.  
**Consequence:** features that would normally need a table (reorder plans, compliance calendars, e-way bill logs, GSTR-9 declarations) store their state per organisation in `localStorage` instead. This is what let nine competitive features ship in three days.

### ADR-014 — Two-Layer State — holds (new)
Postgres holds the financial record (documents, parties, products, payments, stock). `localStorage` holds settings, declarations, plans, logs and queues. The split is deliberate: nothing in local storage is a figure a court, a CA or the GST portal would rely on. Anything a user would grieve losing is reconstructible from documents or already offered as an export (CSV, `.ics`, JSON).  
**Rule:** every key is namespaced `taxflow:<feature>:<orgId>` and carries `{ version, updatedAt, orgId }` (see the storage table below).

### ADR-015 — One Write Path Per Document — holds (new)
`src/lib/create-invoice.ts` and `src/lib/create-bill.ts` are the only ways an invoice or bill is written. The online form, the POS counter, the offline outbox replay and the scanned-bill draft all call them, so an offline invoice cannot diverge from an online one and there is no second implementation to keep in sync. Stock moves only through the `record_stock_movement` RPC (Rule 18).

### ADR-016 — GSP Behind an Adapter, Mock Provider — holds (new)
**Decision:** `src/lib/gsp/` defines a provider interface (`types.ts`, `index.ts`) with a mock implementation (`mock.ts`) and one route (`/api/gsp/ewaybill`).  
**Reason:** there is no free government API; a real connection needs a contracted GSP.  
**Consequence:** the UI, validation and payloads are real and only the transport is simulated. Swapping in a provider touches `gsp/`, nothing else. Do not describe the submit button as live filing.

### ADR-017 — E-Invoice as Build → Download → Paste — holds (new)
The app produces NIC v1.10 JSON (`src/lib/einvoice.ts`) for the user to upload at the IRN portal, then accepts the ACK (IRN + signed QR) back. No IRP call, no fake IRN. Same shape for e-way bill: payload + a register log in local storage.

### ADR-018 — AI That Never Leaves the Browser — holds (new)
**Decision:** OCR and text extraction run in the tab — tesseract.js for images, pdf.js for PDF text. Report answering is a deterministic intent engine (`src/lib/ask.ts`, 17 intents), not a model.  
**Reason:** no server-side LLM key, no per-seat cost, and a purchase bill is the most sensitive document a small business owns.  
**Consequence:** extraction quality is bounded by the engine and is surfaced as per-field confidence. The only server-side piece is `/api/pdf-worker`, which serves the `pdf.worker.min.mjs` asset out of `node_modules` so the worker version stays locked to the bundle instead of drifting from a CDN. No document bytes are ever uploaded.

### ADR-019 — Navigation and Search Are Data in One Place — holds (new)
`src/lib/nav.ts` is the only sidebar/mobile-nav definition; `CommandPalette` and the GST hub read it. `ENTITY_SPECS` in the palette defines live record search and each entry's `?q=` deep link into a list page.  
**Traps:** a new page not in `nav.ts` is invisible; a `?q=` column list that diverges from what that page actually filters on produces a hit that lands on an empty list; icon keys must exist in `Icons.tsx` or the item renders the fallback glyph.

### ADR-020 — One Styling Kit — holds (new)
New pages use `:root` tokens + `.tf-*` utilities (`globals.css`) and the primitives in `src/components/ui.tsx` / `Icons.tsx`. Counted honestly (R2-12, complete): **32** pages inside the shell plus the three auth pages (`login`, `signup`, `setup`, on the shared `src/components/AuthShell.tsx`) are on the kit, and **4** (`bills`, `credit-notes`, `debit-notes`, `quotations`) import only `SearchInput` and still hand-code their header/buttons/badges. No shell page is pre-kit anymore: the pass closed with the two document pages. `4-Design-System.md` §16 is the ledger and the migration order; the pre-R2-12 "~16 pages on the kit" claim counted a `SearchInput` import as a migration.

"Fully on the kit" is about the chrome, not about a clean hex audit — `ledger` (8), `reports/targets` (9), `reports/registers` (6), `pos` (6), `ai/scan-bill` (6), `gst/calendar` (3), `gst/gstr9` (3), `inventory/reorder` (3) and `dashboard` (2) all still hold one-off tints (row hover, skeleton gradient, border next to a `--*-soft` fill, the document-type colour map). **Two of those counts were stale in this doc and are corrected at the R3-7 pass:** `ledger` was listed at 7 and `gst/calendar` at 4, while the files hold 8 and 3 — the calendar's fourth literal went to `--brand-line` at R3-6, and R3-7's deposit card added no new hex because it is built from `--mono`, `--amber` and `--brand-line`. `reports`, `settings`, the auth trio (three pages plus `AuthShell`) and `invoices/new` migrated to absolute zero hex; the brand gradient those pages needed now lives in one `--brand-grad` token, shared with the sidebar mark. **The one deliberate exception is the printable document inside `invoices/[id]`:** its chrome is at zero but the classic document keeps 63 hex literals (cell tints, `#dbeafe` fills, zebra rows, red/green summary text) because that markup is a legal artefact and changing its palette is changing the artefact — do not "fix" it without a reason that survives a print test.

### ADR-021 — Documents Are Signed Rows — holds (new)
Credit notes carry a negative taxable value and negative tax; they are summed, never `Math.abs()`-ed. This single decision is why a credit note dated in a month correctly reduces that month in GSTR-1, the registers, targets and the annual workpaper at once.

### ADR-022 — Whole-Rupee Display in Dense Grids — holds (new)
Statutory tables (GSTR-9, registers, state-wise) format with `Intl.NumberFormat('en-IN', { maximumFractionDigits: 0 })`. Paise stay in the data; only display rounds. Rounding is applied per row and totals are computed from unrounded values, so a column still adds up.

### ADR-023 — Batched Line-Item Reads — holds (new)
Fetching items for many documents goes through 500-id chunks. A single giant `in=(...)` list returned a 400 from Supabase and blanked the registers page (commit `0aba36f`).

### ADR-024 — Invoice PDFs Are Rasterised, Not Vectorised — holds (new)
`html2canvas` at `scale: 2` placed into jsPDF as `image/jpeg` at 0.94 quality. PNG capture produced 36 MB invoices; the JPEG switch is why a 20-line invoice is now a few hundred KB. Captured node is `#invoice-capture`.

### ADR-025 — Recurring Schedules Accrue; Due Dates Are Derived — holds (new)
**Decision:** a schedule stores its *rule* (template lines, cadence + interval, anchor date, end mode, payment terms) and its billed trail — never the dates it owes. `buildCalendar` expands the rule over a horizon and the page intersects it with today, so "what is due" is a computation, not a row.  
**Reason:** there is no server worker (ADR-009 — no cron, no background job) and the store is per-browser (ADR-013/014). A stored due date would go stale the moment the app stayed closed; a derived one cannot.  
**Consequence:** opening `/recurring` *is* the trigger — every missed period is still there, and raising it dates the invoice on the day it belonged to, which — because `createInvoice` reads the FY off `invoice_date` — numbers it in that year's series and files it in that year's return. The UI must warn when that lands in an already-filed period. Occurrences are addressed by **date**, never by click order (`latestOccurrence`, and `recordOccurrence` refuses duplicates, off-cadence dates and dates past the end rule), and all month arithmetic is anchor-relative, clamped (Jan 31 → Feb 28) and UTC-based, so no drift and no timezone can move a bill into another financial year. Do not "simplify" this by caching due dates.

### ADR-026 — Multi-Currency Is a Ledger Beside the Books, Not a Column in Them — holds (new)
**Decision:** `invoices` keeps its rupees. Currency, booked rate, the foreign value and the remittances against a foreign bill live in `taxflow:fx:<orgId>` (ADR-013) and join to the books **by invoice number**. A rate is a dated row with a source stamp (`manual` | `feed`); `rateFor()` returns `null` when nothing has been recorded for that currency on or before that date.  
**Reason:** there is no currency column and the 29 statutory `invoices` columns are frozen (ADR-013 — no migrations, the schema is hand-managed). Rule 45 of the CGST Rules asks for the value in INR at the rate notified for the day, which is a *dated* fact about a bill, not a live conversion factor — so the honest storage is a rate ledger, not a multiplier.  
**Consequence:** three rupee numbers must stay separate and are never averaged: the rate used, the rate the books imply (`grand_total ÷ foreign_value`) and today's rate. The disagreement between them is the finding the page shows, not a rounding error to hide. Revaluation is `null` for a currency with no recorded rate — reporting ₹0 would read as settled. Recording a remittance on `/fx` does **not** receipt the invoice; money still arrives through `/payments`, the single writer (the `/bank` precedent). Two guards are load-bearing and should not be relaxed: `validateRate` refuses an **inverted quote** by the `max/min > 2` ratio against the previous row (a swing check cannot see it — 0.012 against ₹83 is 99.98% below, not "more than a doubling"), and the ECB reference feed is fetched **one currency per request** because inverting its 5-significant-figure INR-base quote loses precision (0.01038 USD/INR → ₹96.34 against the published ₹96.78), with AED/SAR/KWD/QAR/OMR reported `missing` rather than invented.

### ADR-027 — Job Work Is a Clock Beside the Challan, and the Calendar Reads the Register — holds (new)
**Decision:** a job-work movement lives in `taxflow:jobwork:<orgId>` (ADR-013) as one lot per rule 55 challan line, with an append-only disposition trail against it. The lot carries a *stipulated* period but `periodMonths()` takes the **shorter** of that and the statutory cap (inputs 1 year, capital goods 3 years, moulds/dies/tools/templates **no return period at all** → `deadline = null`). A deemed supply is dated the day the goods **went out**, never the day the period ran out, and there is deliberately **no "written off" disposition** — leaving units outstanding is the statement that they never came back. The ITC-04 grids and the HSN summary are **drawn per period from the register** (`itc04Statement(views, period)`), and the compliance calendar's ITC-04 rows are **derived from that same register** (`jobWorkEvents` → `loadJob` + `itc04Periods`/`itc04Cadence`: movements recorded ⇒ statements owed, turnover decides half-yearly vs annual) rather than from a filing-profile tick-box.  
**Reason:** the statutory paper already exists and carries no "must return by" field — a challan records movement, not an obligation to come home, so the clock has to live somewhere else; and a tick-box would be a second source of truth that can disagree with the workpaper it points at, while half-yearly vs annual is a fact about turnover, not a preference.  
**Consequence:** the register cannot double-count the paper: the challan stays on `/challans` (the editor only *copies* a job-work challan's number/date/EWB/party into the lot), a delivery from the job worker's own place is a supply by the principal and belongs on `/invoices`, and money is still only receipted on `/payments`. A late return must stay visible — `returned_after_qty` counts units that came home after the deadline, because rule 45 had already deemed them supplied on the send day — so "settled" and "clean" are different states and the sort puts `returned_late` after `overdue`/`approaching`, not with the closed lots. `indicative_tax` is the goods' own rate applied to the outstanding value and is **labelled indicative wherever it renders**: whether tax falls on the value of the goods or on the job-work service is the officer's and the CA's call, so the page never presents it as a challan amount. Adding job work to the calendar must not go through `Profile` — a new `generateEvents` flag would let the calendar claim a statement the register says is nil.

### ADR-028 — A Cost Centre Stores a Share, Never a Rupee — holds (new)
**Decision:** `taxflow:ccentres:<orgId>` (ADR-013) holds the centre list and, per document, one allocation of `{ centre_id → share }` where **share is a percentage**. No rupee is ever stored: `bookDocs()` reads the five document tables and `sheets()` multiplies each share against the document's *current* taxable / tax / total / ITC. Parts come from the formula, the unassigned remainder comes from **subtraction**, so `Σ(parts) + unallocated = the document` to the paisa. **A cost centre is not a legal person** — no figure on `/cost-centres` reaches a return, and the statutory questions a split raises are flagged rather than answered: a branch in another state with no GSTIN of its own becomes rule 41A territory, worded from the registered home state (`settings.home_state`, stamped on load from `gst_registrations` where `is_primary` — cross-state names both states, same-state raises nothing, unknown says "if"), always with a link to `/gst/gstr9` instead of an invented tax figure. A tag whose document has since been deleted survives as an **orphan** row that says so and contributes no rupees.  
**Reason:** there is no cost-centre column anywhere in the schema and ADR-013 keeps it that way. A stored rupee amount would be a second copy of a number the books already own — amend or cancel the bill and every split saved against it silently rots, and nothing on the register would reveal that it had. A percentage cannot rot: the next load re-reads the document and the centre's contribution changes by itself. Shares are percentages rather than a forced 100 because a document legitimately belongs to nobody in particular; making the form close at 100 would push users to invent splits to dismiss it, and the remainder would be lost rather than printed.  
**Consequence:** the library owns the arithmetic and the page owns no math of its own — the split modal's "what gets written" preview is literally `sheets()` re-run on the draft, so the preview and the register cannot disagree. A credit note's share is negative because ADR-021 still holds (documents are signed rows), so tagging a reversal *reduces* a centre rather than adding to it. Σ shares ≤ 100 is enforced by `validateAllocation`, not by the input widget. Orphans are kept rather than re-pointed: a tag names the paper it belonged to, and deleting a document must not silently move rupees onto a different one. The page reads `invoices`, `credit_notes`, `debit_notes`, `purchase_bills` and `expenses` **read-only** (the `/fx` and `/bank` one-writer precedent — money still arrives only through `/payments`, and the books are written only by their own pages), so nothing here can change a statutory figure, and the boundary is printed on the page in those words.

### ADR-029 — A Tally File Is Evidence, Not Instruction — holds (new)
**Decision:** `/tally` (`src/lib/tally.ts`, R2-22 `f67f56f`) reads a Tally export **in the browser** and turns it into a *plan* before anything is written: one `PlanItem` per ledger, stock item and voucher carrying `target` (customer / vendor / product / invoice / purchase_bill / credit_note / debit_note — the last two since R2-27 — / other), `status` (`ready` / `blocked` / `skipped` / `already`), the rupees it would write, `reasons` for whatever it cannot write, `derived` for every figure that had to be computed from other figures, and `flags` for a disagreement inside the file. Only ticked rows are written; `expandSelection` pulls in the masters a ticked document needs (and, since R2-27, the document a ticked note names), `writeOrder` puts masters before documents and notes after them, and each row goes through `createInvoice` / `createPurchaseBill` / `createCreditNote` / `createDebitNote` — never a direct insert. **Our numbering stays ours:** `invoice_number` comes from `invoice_sequences` and the Tally reference travels in `notes` (`Tally Sales #V-101 · ref PO-118`, read back by `tallyReference()`), while a purchase bill's `bill_number` remains the *vendor's own* reference. **Money is never written**: an entry whose ledger is a cash/bank/round-off account is typed `money`, reported, and the document lands unpaid for `/payments`. Only the log of an import is stored — `taxflow:tally:<orgId>` (ADR-013).  
**Reason:** there is no mapping table in the schema and ADR-013 keeps it that way, but the deeper reason is trust. A competitor's bridge uploads the file, runs a mapping on someone's server, and the ledger simply changes; bookkeeping imported on a guess is worse than bookkeeping not imported, because it looks like the owner's own record. So a document whose inputs cannot be resolved **blocks rather than guesses** — no party invented, no product created implicitly by a line, no tax rate spread across lines the file cannot attribute it to — and where the file disagrees with itself (ledger lines that do not sum to the printed total, a voucher that does not balance to zero) the disagreement is *reported* and the file's own lines win, because those lines are what the shop billed. The numbering rule is the same argument pointing the other way: `bill_number` is what GSTR-2B matches, so claiming a vendor's number for a Tally voucher would corrupt a reconciliation nobody can undo.  
**Consequence:** the reader must stay a **scanner, not a validating parser** (Node has no `DOMParser`, and the browser's refuses the half-closed files real exports turn out to be), so it keeps accepting broken input while saying plainly what it could not read. The library stays free of React and Supabase — `window` appears only inside the guarded log section — so the Node strip-types harness can exercise all 670 assertions and the page owns every line of DOM code, including the file download. Three lines from the v1 boundary must not be blurred without reopening this ADR: a receipt, payment, journal or delivery challan (objects whose write paths live on other pages) is **listed as skipped with the reason**, not coerced into the nearest document type — *credit and debit notes left this list in R2-27 (`0e5b4f3`), which reopened exactly this clause and closed it again the honest way: `noteItem()` plans the note against the document it names through the note pages' own creators (`creditnote.ts`), and where the original cannot be resolved the row blocks with the missing number printed*; a service voucher whose value exists only as ledger entries **blocks** rather than inventing a line; and a note is planned only against an original that exists — on the register (by number, or by the `#<Tally ref>` in an imported document's notes) or ready in the same file — never inferred from the party's name. The plan is re-run against the register after every write, which is what makes the UI idempotent (written rows flip to `already` instead of being importable twice), and a master that fails to create leaves the documents that needed it unwritten with the reason — a partial import must never produce an invoice pointing at a party that does not exist. The file's Tally company state is *not* read: the organisation's primary `gst_registrations` row supplies the state code, because two sources disagreeing about where the business is registered is a worse failure than asking the user for nothing.

### ADR-030 — A Project Register Measures Work and Refuses to Own the Money — holds (new; amended R2-36, a named hour is costed beside contribution, never inside it)
**Decision:** `/projects` (`src/lib/projects.ts`, R2-23 `dc41578`, doctrine set in `a134408`) stores **measurements** in `taxflow:projects:<orgId>` (ADR-013): projects, hour rows, and whole documents attached **by id**. It stores no rupee and re-reads every one on load through `bookDocs()` — the reader `costcentres.ts` already owned, imported rather than rewritten, so a cancelled row carries nothing and a credit note stays negative in both features. Three rules carry it. **Labour is priced, not costed:** an hour carries `rate` = what you would *bill* it at, and there is no payroll in the register, so `contribution = billed revenue − attached spend` with `labour_value` sitting beside it and deliberately **not** subtracted. **"Billed" is a reference stamp:** `markBilled(ref)` records the invoice number you raised elsewhere; raising the invoice remains `createInvoice`'s job (the only path that allocates `INV-<FY>-NNNN` and moves stock) and receiving money remains `/payments'`. **The window is a cited constant, not a verdict:** `SERVICE_INVOICE_DAYS = 30` comes from CBIC's *Handbook on Invoicing under GST* (17-06-2025 §2.1.2, rule 47 read with section 31(2)); `internal` work has no recipient so no supply and **no window to miss**, an unknown billing mode falls to the loud side, and a late row prints a date, a rule and a link to the GSTR-3B workpaper — never a tax figure.  
**Reason:** there is no project table, and the deeper reason is the same one behind ADR-028: a rupee saved here is a second copy of a number the books already own, so amending or cancelling a bill would silently rot every job saved against it. Costing labour instead of pricing it would need payroll — a different customer segment (gap #6) — and a margin that subtracted your own billing rate from itself would be double counting. And the 30-day invoice rule for services is a rule about *paperwork timing* whose exceptions are set by notification, so the register can name the document owed and the date it was owed by without pretending to know the tax.  
**Consequence:** the engine owns all arithmetic and the page owns none — the four watch states (`no_window` / `open` / `due_soon` / `overdue`), the per-project-month grouping, `unpriced_hours` (which yields **no rupee figure rather than a ₹0 one**, and `watchSentence()` says so in the state's own tone), `days_over` counted as well as reported, and the finding list including `unmatched_refs` (a stamp naming an invoice the books do not have is accepted, because the paper may exist outside TaxFlow, and reported, because it cannot be verified). Orphans behave like cost centres': a tag survives its document, says it is an orphan, contributes nothing. `deleteProject` refuses a project that still carries hours or tags — archive instead (the serial-register precedent). The page shares no state with `/cost-centres` beyond the reader: **a centre splits one document by percentage, a project collects whole documents and adds hours**, and because `costcentres.KIND_META.project` is literally labelled "Project / job", the collision is resolved in printed copy on both pages rather than left to the reader. A related-party (internal) job whose hours carry a rate is *not* blocked — this register cannot know whether the charge is arm's length, so it prints hours, rate and rupees and points at `/gst/gstr9`.
**Amended R2-36 (`9ec2387` engine, `6ab4212` page):** a timesheet row may now **name whose hours they were** — an optional `employee_id` pointing at a payroll person, additive at `PROJECTS_VERSION = 1` (an old envelope reads typed names, which is what it recorded), with `''` meaning the name was typed and is never costed. The join is built as **rows of data, never as an import**: the *page* reads payroll's figures through `costPerHourFor()`, rebuilds each as a **`HourCostRow`** (`{employee_id, name, month, cost_per_hour: number | null, basis, why}` — the structural subset of payroll's own `HourCost`, a refusal carrying its reason), and hands them to `projects.ts`'s new **`costHours()`**, so the library still imports no payroll code (the `BookInput` / `docsFor` precedent: the page fetches, the library computes, and a test can build cost rows by hand). Four rules carry the amendment. **The month is the entry's own** (`periodOf(entry.date)`): September's hour is priced at September's figure, so a register corrected later cannot silently re-price last quarter's work. **A refusal is a row, not a zero** — an hour whose person the register cannot price for its own month keeps a `null` cost and the register's own `why` (the page synthesizes the row, so the amber finding's sentence is the register's, not the page's), a typed-only hour is a grey finding, and the two kinds are counted apart: `uncosted_hours` (linked but unpriceable) and `unlinked_hours` (no link at all), neither standing in for a rupee. **The reading sits beside the number it may not change:** `after_labour = contribution − labour_cost` prints as *after named hours* — the stat sub reads ` · after named hours ₹…, partial` while any hour is unpriced — `contribution` is byte-identical whether a row names a person or not, and the contribution card carries the sentence *"Read them together, do not add them."* **The pick stays a pick:** a native `select` (the lot-select precedent) offers *"Typed name only — never costed"* first and keeps a stale option for a person no longer on the register, the logger previews the row's own cost as it is typed (*"October 2026: this person's hour cost ₹146.21 — this row would read ₹877.26 at cost."*), and the projects CSV carries the priced column. Two facts stay open, printed rather than hidden: an hour nobody names is priced only, and **nothing yet connects the day book's daily marks to a job** — R2-34 keeps which days a person worked and R2-36 lets a row name the person, but which hours on which day those marks paid for is still stated nowhere.

### ADR-031 — A Recipe Stores Quantities, a Build Records What Was Drawn — holds (new; amended R2-28, minutes and dated shop rates; amended R2-29, the build names its lots; amended R2-30, the shortage becomes paper)
**Decision:** `/inventory/recipes` (`src/lib/bom.ts`, R2-24 engine `2c9eed7`, page `fd9126d`) keeps **relationships and quantities** in `taxflow:bom:<orgId>` (ADR-013): a recipe is the finished good, how many units one run produces, and the quantity of each component drawn for that run with the waste percent the shop actually gets; a build is the units made, the date, and **the lines that were drawn**, under a job number (`JOB-<FY>-NNNN`) allocated by this register and never by an invoice sequence. Four rules carry it. **A recipe stores no rupee:** `rollUpCost()` multiplies each component's quantity against `products.purchase_price` — the same field `/accounting` values stock at — on every load, so a price corrected on the master re-values every recipe that uses it without anything being re-typed, and a component with no cost yields **no rupee figure rather than a ₹0 one**. **A build stores what was drawn rather than re-deriving it from the recipe,** because the drum said 1.3 kg and the recipe hoped 1.2; `planBuild()` prints that difference as a *variance* instead of smoothing it away. **Stock moves only through `record_stock_movement`,** components out and finished units in on the two `adjustment_*` types that already exist, with the job reference carried in `notes`. **A build is not a supply:** making something inside your own registered place moves no GST, the tax happens when the finished good is sold at `/invoices/new` at *its own* HSN/SAC and rate, and materials handed to somebody else are job work, which lives on `/gst/job-work` with its section 143 clock. **Amended R2-28 (`bcdd305`):** a recipe also stores `labour_minutes` and `overhead_minutes` per run, and the register holds **dated shop-rate rows** (`from_date`, `labour_per_hour`, `overhead_per_hour`, a note) — the latest row at or before a date prices the work, one row per `from_date`, and a rate of ₹0 is refused rather than clamped, because absence prints a dash and zero prints a claim. The legs are priced **readings, not postings**: material re-reads the catalogue at today's prices, the time legs price at the rate in force on *the run's own day*, and the two bases never add — `summarise` keeps them in separate columns. The minutes shipped additively under `BOM_VERSION = 1`: an old envelope reads zero minutes and no rates, which is what the shop had recorded when the field did not exist; a version bump would read every existing register as empty.  
**Amended R2-29 (`7de09ee` engine, `11adb45` page):** a build line also records **which lot each drawn unit came off** — `lots: [{batch_id, batch_no, qty, expiry_date}]` on the line, and `output_batch_id` on the build for the lot the finished units landed in. Three rules decide the shape. **FEFO is a proposal the shop may overrule:** `allocateLots()` offers the earliest-expiring lot with stock left (within the run's own godown when it names one), the editor lets a user switch the lot or split one component across several, and naming *no* lot stays legal — the run leaves the shelf and the batch register says nothing, rather than the page inventing a lot. **The pick is a copy of a decision, not a balance:** the quantities are re-checked against `taxflow:batches:` + `taxflow:batchmoves:` at the moment of writing (`validateLotIssues()` + the page's per-lot clash check), so a sale made in another tab since the draft was opened blocks the write instead of being talked around. **The mirror reads as paper:** each draw becomes one `adjust` move with the job in `doc_ref` ("Build JOB-… — Chocolate 1 issued from lot CH-2026-JUL") and the finished units become a lot whose `batch_no` *is* the job number, so `/inventory/batches` needs no new column to show who made what; `reverseLotWritesFor()` turns the verb round ("Cancel JOB-… — Chocolate 1 went back to lot CH-2026-JUL") and drawn-plus-returned sums to zero per lot. `BOM_VERSION` again stays **1** — the fields are additive, and an old envelope simply reads "no lots named", which is exactly what was recorded before. 
**Amended R2-30 (`2b72431` engine, `d49c506` page):** the materials board now carries its shortage into the register that buys — but it *writes* nothing of its own. `mergedShortages(views)` folds every recipe plan into one row per component (`roundQty` on the running sum, `for_recipes` deduped, short-desc then name) and is now the only fold in the file: `summarise()`'s `shortage_rows` / `components_short` and the orders a board raises both read it, which is what makes the number on the screen and the quantity on the paper the same number. `shoppingPlan(views, products, supply, parties)` then arranges that list by **the vendor each component last came from** — `latestSupplier()` takes the newest bill line that names one, ties to the row read last, unnamed vendors skipped, the rule `/inventory/reorder` already used — and prices it at that bill's own rate, falling back to `products.purchase_price` only when no bill names one, every line labelled `last bill` or `product master` so the reader can see which basis it stands on. Three rules carry the honesty. **A line is routed only when the order could actually be saved:** no bill ever named a supplier, the supplier has left the vendor list, and the vendor card carries no state are each refused with its sentence printed (`… has no state on the vendor card, so there is no place of supply to write`), because `validateDoc` on the register's own page would reject that draft anyway — declining first beats writing a document addressed to nobody. **One unpriced line and the order has no value:** `value = lines.some(l => l.rate === null) ? null : round2(…)`; a sum of just the priced lines would read as *the* value of an order with a hole in it, which is the two-bases rule of R2-28 speaking in the buy direction, and the same refusal is repeated in `orderNote` and left **blank** in the CSV rather than typed as 0. **The number is taken, never typed:** the page hands each grouped order to `raiseDrafts()` → `appendDraft()` → `takeNumber()` in `src/lib/orderflow.ts`, so a draft born on the recipes board is numbered by the purchase-order register's own per-FY high-water mark, lands in `taxflow:porder:<orgId>`, and is indistinguishable from one written at `/purchase-orders`; `validateDoc` runs *before* the write and a grouped order that would be refused is skipped and named rather than forced. Nothing is read until the shop asks — `ensureSupply()` fetches live vendors and non-cancelled bills on the click and pulls items in 500-id batches narrowed to the short product ids. Still **no new key and `BOM_VERSION` still 1**: the plan is a reading, the drafts belong to another register, and the vault spec for `taxflow:bom:` already covers it.
**Reason:** there is no BOM, recipe or work-order table in the schema, and ADR-013 keeps it that way. The deeper reason is the one behind ADR-028 and ADR-030: a rupee saved here is a second copy of a number the catalogue already owns, and the second copy is the one that goes stale — every register that stored a cost at write time eventually disagrees with its own product master. Inventing a `build` movement type would be a schema change *and* would put a type in `stock_movements` that every other reader of that table has never seen, so the movement log shows a manual adjustment **named after the job**, which is the truth, instead of a fiction with a tidier label. There is no cost-layer engine behind this (no weighted average, no WIP account), so what a build *costs* is a reading with its source printed beside it, not an accountant's figure. And automatic back-flushing — consuming materials whenever an invoice is raised — was refused on purpose: a finished unit may equally have come from opening stock or from a purchase, and guessing which is exactly how stock books get cooked. R2-30 refused the *inverse* automation for the same reason: the board could have raised and confirmed orders by itself, and instead it stops at a draft, because a purchase order is a promise to a vendor and that promise is the register's own act.
**Consequence:** the page cannot read the library's validators as a stock gate. `validateBuild()` polices the *record* (an active recipe, a real date and not one that has not happened yet, a plausible run, lines that point at catalogue products) and **never asks whether the components are on the shelf**; `planBuild()` knows each row's shortfall but a plan is not a refusal — so `/inventory/recipes` builds its own over-draw check from `current_stock` and blocks the write itself. Likewise `cancelBuild()` flips the status and **leaves `moved: true`**, which would tell the next reader that a cancelled run still stands in the stock ledger, so the page writes `reverseMovementsFor()` first and stores `moved: false` in the same upsert, keeping both rows on the log as the doctrine requires. Movement writes are all-or-nothing *at the screen*: a failure reverses whatever already applied and leaves the run a plan rather than a fact. Sub-assembly explosion stops at `MAX_EXPLODE_DEPTH = 12` with `findCycle()` refusing the loop **before** the stack does; quantities round at four decimals because a recipe speaks in fractions of a unit while money rounds at two; and the ceilings (`MAX_UNITS_PER_BUILD`, `MAX_WASTE_PCT`, `MAX_QTY_PER_COMPONENT`, `MAX_COMPONENTS`) exist to catch a unit error — grams typed where kg was meant — as a refusal rather than as a plausible-looking number. **Boundary that must not be blurred without reopening this ADR:** nothing here reaches a return or moves a rupee of tax; there is still **no work-in-progress account** — R2-28 prices the labour and overhead legs as *readings*, each printed with its rate and date beside it, a leg with no rate in force printing nothing (an amber finding names the reason) rather than a parts-only total in full-cost clothing, and payroll (gap #6) stays outside: the shop states its own rate row and the register never invents a wage; a build draws **quantities from named lots** (R2-29) but **not serials** — a serial is one unit and a build is a quantity of them, so unit issuance stays at the counter in `/inventory/serials`, one lot per run rather than a batch-split output, and the finished lot inherits whatever expiry and godown the run actually recorded instead of being given one it cannot justify; and a scrap or offcut *sale* is an ordinary invoice, not a build. **R2-30 added one more thing the page must not pretend to own:** the paper it raises is a **draft at one rate basis and one slab** — the last bill's rate or the catalogue price, seeded at 18% GST in every line — with no MOQ, no lead time and no price-list engine behind it, so the raised row's prices, dates and tax rate are corrected and confirmed at `/purchase-orders`, which stays the only place an order becomes a commitment; and a short component whose supplier cannot be resolved stays on the board as a named finding rather than becoming an order to a guessed vendor.

### ADR-032 — An Export Register Keeps Paper and Dates, Never a Rupee — holds (new)
**Decision:** `/gst/exports` (`src/lib/exports.ts`, R2-25 engine `3e6582d`, page + wiring `580086d`) keeps **the paperwork and the dates** in `taxflow:exports:<orgId>` (ADR-013): which shipping bill carried the consignment, out of which port, to which country, by which mode, under which Letter of Undertaking, whether the proceeds landed, and by when. It stores **no invoice value, no IGST and no balance** — those are re-read on every load from `ExportBookInvoice` rows joined **by invoice number**, the key `/fx` already uses, because the bank quotes a number and not a uuid, so amending the invoice moves the file's exposure with it. Four rules carry it. **The route to zero-rating is recorded, never inferred** — `lut` (section 16(1)(a) + Notification 44/2017-Central Tax (Rate)), `igst_paid` (16(1)(b) + rule 96), `sez` (rule 48) or `unknown`, each with its `basis` quoted, because the two routes run *different clocks*: a LUT file has no IGST to refund, so its refund state is `nothing_to_claim` rather than a deadline. **One rupee, one home** — an invoice with an FX entry is receipted on `/fx` and this register shows those rupees without adding its own; a credit typed here beside an FX deal prints `not counted`, and where both sides hold figures that disagree the view reports `sources_diverge` naming **both** amounts instead of summing them into a number no bank ever credited. **The realisation window is a dated band table** (`REALISATION_BANDS`, `ADVANCE_BANDS`) chosen by the *date of export*, each band carrying its instrument, its URL and its note, with the 13-11-2025 one-day split kept as a printed `disputed` range rather than resolved. **The missing paper is the product** — `unregisteredExports()` finds export and SEZ invoices with no file, and `paperGaps()` lists per consignment which document the register has not seen and what hangs on it.  
**Reason:** nothing in the schema describes a shipping bill, an undertaking or a realisation, and ADR-013 says the register lives on localStorage. The deeper reason is that the books already answer *how much* and cannot answer *whether it was lawful*: `supply_type` zeroes the IGST and the figure flows into GSTR-1 and GSTR-3B, while section 16(1) makes that treatment conditional on paper that exists only at ICEGATE and the AD bank. Guessing the route would put the wrong clock on the file — a refund deadline for a claim that cannot exist, or no deadline for IGST that is actually reclaimable — and a deadline is the one thing a register must not be wrong about. Summing two partial realisations would be the same error in rupees. And a statutory period that moved four times inside a year cannot be a constant: hard-coding "9 months" would silently re-date every consignment in the older bands.  
**Consequence:** `/gst/calendar` cannot restate these dates, it **calls `watchRows()`** and maps the four kinds to `ComplianceEvent`s (`lut`/`refund` as `gst`, the FEMA rows as `other`), so a calendar row carries the file's own id and href, disappears when the file does, and a non-GST default gets `penalty.note` while the ₹-per-day estimator stays silent on a contravention it has no rate for. Two pages share one clock, so `isExportDoc()` is exported and the invoice detail page's banner points at `?file=<invoice_number>` instead of printing its own dates. Validators refuse a named LUT that does not cover the export date, a shipping bill predating the invoice it was filed against, a goods export whose date of export falls before its own invoice (rule 47), a service consignment carrying a shipping bill, and a window override outside 1-60 months — but they do **not** check the shelf-like facts: a shipping bill number is verified for shape, never against ICEGATE, so `paperGaps` reports absence rather than validity. Honest v1 boundary, printed in the library header and on the page, not to be blurred without reopening this ADR: nothing is filed and no refund amount is computed (the claim is a transcribed RFD-1/RFD-11 reference; the IGST figure is the invoice's own), credit notes that reduce an export invoice are netted in `/gst/gstr9`, and a shipping bill covering several invoices is modelled as one file per invoice sharing a number — which is how the refund claim itself is filed. Whether unutilised ITC may be claimed instead is a rule 89 question for the CA, so the register names the option and takes no position.

### ADR-033 — The Vault Walks a Named List and Restores Through the Owners — holds (new)
**Decision:** `/vault` (`src/lib/vault.ts`, R2-26 engine `8aae49e`, page + wiring `ba15288`) writes every device-side register into **one file the user owns** (`kind: "taxflow.vault"`, version 1, named `taxflow-vault-<business>-<date>.json`) and puts it back. Four rules carry it. **The vault walks a named list; it never sweeps the browser** — `REGISTERS` names every `taxflow:<name>:<orgId>` key the book holds — thirty-one when this sentence was last written, **thirty-five as the R3-9 pass re-read it** (ADR-013) with what each holds and which module writes it, so a `sb-*` session key or an extension's leftover cannot ride into a backup; the list *is* the security property and is printed on the page, not hidden in code. **A restore goes through the module that owns the key** — `applyRestore()` calls each register's own `save*(orgId, value)`, never `localStorage.setItem`, so the version check, the row parsing and the org stamping run on restore exactly as when a page saves; it is then **proved by reading back** through the same `load*` the owning page uses, and the report prints `file → here` per register, naming the rows the register's own parser dropped. **A damaged page does not void the book** — every block carries an FNV-1a checksum over its own serialised content; a block that does not match is refused by name while the other thirty still restore, and unreadable JSON is kept as *text* with a note rather than silently omitted, because a vault that shrank instead of reporting damage would be lying by omission. The checksum is integrity, not authenticity — nothing proves who wrote the file. **The five flow logs travel as evidence only** — the offline outbox, the e-way bill log, the Tally import log, the palette recents and the calendar profile are collected so the file shows what the browser holds, but they have no `write`: restoring them would replay a pending sync or re-claim a number the portal owns. The vault's own log (`taxflow:vault:`) is not in the file — a backup that remembered being backed up would grow every time it was restored.  
**The plan comes before the write:** `planRestore()` puts one of nine states on every row — *Will add / Unchanged / Will replace / Will clear / Shown only / Refused / Other business / Not in file / Unknown key* — and its ladder is ordered because each rung assumes the previous passed: unknown → foreign → checksum → shape (`accepts`) → evidence → 0/0 `same` → byte-equal `same` → `new` → `replace`, with `blank` (the file empty where the browser holds rows) **selectable but never pre-ticked**, because restoring it would clear live work and a checkbox someone else ticked is not consent. `defaultSelection()` pre-ticks only what is safe to add; the page demands an explicit confirmation naming the counts when the plan would replace or clear; a file from another business is refused outright; and `readVault()` gives every wrong file its own sentence — *empty / not-json / not-a-vault / future-version / no-registers* — because a CSV, a Tally XML dump and a truncated download are three different mistakes.  
**Reason:** ADR-013's trade is offline-first at the cost of portability: the documents come back with the login and the judgement does not, so a cleared browser or a new laptop takes the LUT numbers, the batch and serial registers, the godowns, the recipes, the splits, the TDS choices, the targets and the calendar ticks with it — and none of them is reconstructible from a return. Every cloud competitor keeps this state in its own database; the honest answer to "we chose device-side" is a file the user carries, not a promise that the device will not fail. Restoring through the owners is the deeper half of the same decision: a vault that wrote storage directly would bypass every parser the app has learned to refuse shapes with, and would half-write a register while reporting success. Sweeping the browser would be a credential leak with a download button.  
**Consequence:** `taxflow:invoice-template:` moved out of `InvoiceDocument.tsx` into `src/lib/invoice-template.ts` — a preference a backup has to walk is data, and a library test cannot import a `.tsx`. Restore is deliberately **not a transaction** — a failure in one register is reported and the next is still tried, because a vault is a stack of independent documents, not one write — and `applyRestore` logs every attempt to `taxflow:vault:` including a fully-refused one, so the history card is a record of what was *attempted*, not only of what succeeded. Honest v1 boundary, not to be blurred without reopening this ADR: nothing here touches Supabase — a restore never re-inserts documents, invents a receipt or clears a debt, because the database rows are the ones the login brings back and a second way to write money is the one thing this app refuses to add; a newer build's file is refused (`future-version`) rather than guessed; and the "Registers stored here N/31" count is a state, not an error — an honest zero on a fresh browser is exactly what a new user has.

### ADR-034 — A Salary Register Stores Structure and Exceptions, and Re-walks the Year for Every Rupee — holds (new; amended R2-32, the shop's sheet reads in and is discarded; amended R2-33, the month prints as paper; amended R2-34, the days themselves are kept as a day book; amended R2-36, the hour cost is read by `/projects` as data)
**Decision:** `/payroll` (`src/lib/payroll.ts`, R2-31 engine `140d1e0`, page + wiring `9393529`) keeps, in `taxflow:payroll:<orgId>` (ADR-013), only the two things that are facts: **what a person is paid** (six monthly components — basic, dearness, HRA, conveyance, medical, special — with the PF-carrying and ESI-carrying flags on each row, an age band, a state, a joining and a leaving date, typed hours a day, a per-employee professional-tax figure) and **what a month took** (lost days, arrears, bonus, recovery). Everything else is computed on every load. Five rules carry it. **The rates are dated bands, each citing its own instrument** — `PF_WAGE_CEILINGS` moves ₹15,000 → ₹25,000 from 17-09-2026 (S.O. 5109(E), with the EY and Ministry of Labour releases beside it), so a run dated before that day is computed on the old ceiling and says so; `ESI_BANDS` stayed at ₹21,000 (₹25,000 for a person with disability) at 0.75% and 3.25%, and the two statutes disagreeing on the same payroll is printed as a finding rather than smoothed; `PF_RATES` splits the employer's 12% into 8.33% EPS and 3.67% EPF with EDLI and administrative charges at 0.5% each. **Tax is section 192's own method, not a monthly percentage** — `taxWalk()` runs April forward: each elapsed month adds its actual wages, the months after it are projected at the current month's rate, the estimate sheds the standard deduction (capped at the estimate) and the *year's* professional tax under section 16(iii) — `min(₹2,500, monthly × the months this person is actually on the rolls)`, because dividing by the months left only works if the whole year was priced first — `taxBreakdown()` computes the year's tax on what remains (bands, 87A with its marginal relief, the surcharge cliffs measured from the foot of the band, the cess), and the balance is divided by the on-roll months still to come, so a June bonus lifts July onward, a December joiner carries the year in four months and the March row absorbs whatever the estimate under-collected; nothing is stored as "TDS deducted" and the walk re-runs on every load, which is the only way the register can promise that June's row and March's row add up to the year. A financial year the tables do not hold is **refused** ("no tax figure is invented") rather than extrapolated from the nearest year. **The default regime only, and it says so** — section 115BAC, where HRA is not exempt and investments do not reduce tax, and an employee aged 60 or above is **refused** a tax figure rather than given the general schedule, because the senior bands were not in any source fetched. **Absence and zero are never the same row** — `pt: number | null` prints a dash and "not recorded" against a real `0` that prints zero and claims it. **Unsettled questions are printed as questions** — whether the *pensionable* salary for EPS moved with the September ceiling is not settled by any document fetched, so `settings.eps_pension_ceiling` is the owner's figure (₹15,000 by default) and the slip prints what it used; professional tax is a state subject whose fetched tables contradict each other, so there is **no national PT table at all**, `StatutoryLine.due` is the empty string and the dues card prints *Due on the state’s own date — no national date to print*; and the four labour codes' 50% wage floor (in force 21-11-2025) is a **flag with its citation**, not an add-back, because the EPFO office that administers your establishment settles it.  
**Reason:** there is no employee table, no salary table and no schema window to add one, and a salary is the most private record a business keeps — the owner's browser is the honest home. The deeper reason is drift: a stored gross or a stored TDS would disagree with the structure the moment anyone corrects a component, which is why the recipe book (ADR-031) and the cost centres (ADR-028) already refuse to store money. The year-walk is the same refusal in time: deducting a flat monthly percentage would leave twelve instalments that do not add up to the year's tax, and the true-up that section 192 asks for is only possible if the year is recomputed from what the books now say. And the dated bands exist because a rate table that does not know its own effective date is a rumour — the September ceiling is the second one this app has had to catch mid-year (ADR-032's FEMA bands are the first).  
**Consequence:** three other pages now read this register and none of them may restate it. `/gst/calendar` **retires its own guess**: `payrollEvents()` emits an ECR, ESIC, salary-TDS, ESI-return and Form 16 row per month the books were actually run, and the calendar filters out the tick-box's monthly PF/PT guess for any month the register files, matching on the row id — a guessed row names the *deposit* month, so `addMonths(..., -1)` steps back to the salary month before asking (`${fy}-pay-${head}-${month}`). An undated professional-tax row sits in its own *State dates* rail, prints *due on the state’s own date*, counts as neither overdue nor due soon and stays **out of the `.ics`**, because an .ics event needs a start date and inventing one is the exact error the register refuses. `/projects` (ADR-030) can now ask what an hour costs — `costPerHour()` divides the month's *employer cost* by that person's paid days and their own `hours_per_day` (a per-employee field the card says is used for nothing else), and `blendedHourCost()` weights the month rather than averaging averages — and since R2-36 a timesheet row can *state* whose hours they were: `/projects`' page builds `HourCostRow`s out of `costPerHourFor()` and hands them to that library's `costHours()`, so the figure is read **as data** (the library still imports no payroll code) and printed as *after named hours* **beside** contribution, never inside it — ADR-030's sentence now reads *priced, and costed only where the row names its person*, and an hour nobody names still carries only its billing rate (this page's own two copy lines were re-worded to say exactly that). What is left is the attendance-to-job link — nothing yet connects the day book's daily marks to a job. `/vault` gained its thirty-first spec (ADR-033's list is now thirty-one, twenty-six restorable), and the month CSV carries the ceiling the run actually used as its own column — a figure read against the wrong ceiling is a different figure, and the sheet a CA opens should say which one this is. Nothing here writes money: `/expenses` remains the writer of the rupees that leave, `/payments` the writer of money in and out, and the challans are paid on the EPFO, ESIC and income-tax portals, which no browser-side register can reach — so a `taxflow:payroll:` row that looks like a payment is a bug. Honest v1 boundary, printed in the library header and on the page, not to be blurred without reopening this ADR: **no old-regime computation** (an employee who opts out is out of scope here and the page says so), no NPS, LTA, perquisite valuation or 80CCD(2), no overtime (a 2× statutory multiplier and an establishment-level question), no gratuity or leave-encashment provision, **no device feed and no per-day wage ledger** — since R2-34 the days themselves *are* kept, as one attendance mark per person per day in the register's own day book, but that is evidence and not a payroll: no biometric or device integration, no shift timings, no overtime priced off the marks, no per-day or contract labour, no accrual or work-in-progress posting, no Form 16 Part A (it comes out of TRACES, and the annual sheet is *shaped* like Part B and labelled as not one), no challan numbers — which is why `/tds` cannot see salary TDS and the dues card links there rather than claiming the statement is complete, and no PT challan. 435 assertions in `/tmp/payroll-test.mjs`.
**R2-32 amended the attendance half of this ADR** (engine `79e985b`, page + copy fixes `2942fa9`, ⌘K keywords `27388e1`): the month run now takes the shop's own sheet as a paste and reads **one figure** out of it — `lop_days` — while the sheet itself is thrown away. The decision that keeps ADR-034 intact is that the paste enters through the same door the month form uses: `applyAttendance()` re-runs `validateInput()` inside its own `mutate`, writes only `lop_days` plus a `[attendance <month>]` sentence stamped into the row's note (re-importing a month **replaces** that segment rather than stacking a second answer, and text the owner typed after the em dash survives), and never touches arrears, bonus or recovery, because a sheet says nothing about money. **No new key, no new register, no version bump** — `PAYROLL_VERSION` stays 1 and the vault spec's `what` line was re-worded instead of the list being lengthened. Four refusals are the decision, not the plumbing: a number whose header names no direction is refused rather than defaulted (days present and days lost are opposite salaries); the sheet's own stated month length (`24/26`) **outranks** `settings.working_days`, and a row carrying both columns that disagree is refused instead of arbitrated; a person the sheet forgot is printed as a finding rather than zeroed (silence is not zero); and an identity is earned — code, then exact name, then one *unique* substring reported as `approximate`, with two people sharing a code or a name refused by name. `planAttendance()` is pure, so the preview the owner approves is the same computation the write performs, and it refuses a stale preview by comparing `plan.month` against the screen's month. Two implementation calls are worth keeping: the reader is `detectDelimiter()` / `splitRow()` **imported from `bank-statement.ts`** — the app has not written a second CSV parser for a paste — and the panel's "clear the preview when the month moves" lives in React's adjust-during-render recipe because `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` forbids the obvious effect. **What the live session caught that 208 assertions did not:** the stranger-on-a-name-only-row refusal asserted the opposite of what it meant (*"…is on the payroll register"*), `rowText()` printed a grid row's blank cells as `· · · · ·`, the receipt read *"1 month row now carry"*, and the paste pane stood as two ~135px columns at 355px until `/bank`'s 1100px break was copied — the first two are engine copy, the last two are the page, and all four are the reason Rule 26 says to drive the screen. The suite's needles now quote whole sentences, because a substring needle was a match for both the wrong wording and the right one. 212 assertions in `/tmp/attendance-test.mjs`, with the 435 payroll assertions re-run green beside them.
**R2-33 amended the paper half of this ADR** (engine `ac14df8`, page + wiring `6f68291`): the register's reading of a month now ends as an A4 payslip (`src/lib/payslip.ts`, **463 lines**, laid out by `/payroll/payslip` at **892**), and the decision that keeps ADR-034 intact is that **the slip is a reading, never a second computation** — `buildPayslip()` receives the `Payslip` that `payslipOf()` computed and re-derives no rupee, so the suite polices the *tie* rather than the layout: `earnings_total = monthly_total + arrears + bonus`, `deduction_total = lop_deduction + the statutory legs`, `statutory_total = slip.deductions`, `employer_legs_total = Σ` the legs printed under its own heading, and `employer_cost − net_pay = employer_legs_total + deduction_total − loss of pay` — counted once, because loss of pay is taken off inside gross and prints in the deduction block. Four conventions travel with every sheet: a **statement, not a payment** (*Net payable*, never paid, with `PAYSLIP_BOUNDARIES`' four standing sentences printed beside it — payable is not paid, the statutory columns are computed not recorded, **this is not Form 16**, nothing here is filed); **a dash with a named sentence** for any figure or identifier nobody recorded, the same `number | null` rule an unset professional tax has followed since R2-31, applied now to letterhead codes because a statutory-looking document carrying an invented establishment code is worse than one carrying a dash; **a refused month prints no figures at all** — `printable: false` carries the engine's `why_not`, the deduction and employer blocks are not laid out, and Print and PDF stay disabled with the engine's sentence as the title; and **the exceptions stay on the owner's side of the paper** — the register's flags and gaps print in a `.no-print` strip while `monthPack()` returns `docs` *and* `refused` with `partial`, so a month pack is never silently a subset. The **letterhead** — 15 typed lines, 4 of them the statutory identifiers (`pf_establishment`, `esi_code`, `employer_pan`, `gstin`), each carrying a sentence naming what it would be needed *for* — is asked for and never invented: a blank one is an allowed state, and the slip falls back to the workspace name and prints its four gaps beside the document. It lives **inside** `taxflow:payroll:<orgId>` (`saveLetterhead` inside the payroll store's own `mutate` is the only writer) because *a heading is not a register* (Rule 24's new paragraph) — **no new key, no slip stored, `PAYROLL_VERSION` stays 1**, and the vault's payroll spec grew only its `what` line. One person prints one A4 document (`downloadElementAsPdf`, named by the engine's own filename slug); the month prints one PDF, a sheet per person, through `downloadElementsAsPdf()`, which `src/lib/pdf-export.ts` grew in this round. **What the live session caught that 394 assertions did not:** the refused month's header subtitle is assembled out of document fields, so it printed *"net payable ₹0.00"* and left both buttons live until the page was driven in the browser — the defect was in *which fields a branch chooses to print*, not in the engine's reading, so no assertion over the reading could see it; **when a page has a no-figures branch, drive that branch** (Rule 26's lesson, now Rule 33's closing note). 394 assertions in `/tmp/payslip-test.mjs`, with the 435 payroll, 212 attendance and 95 shopping suites re-run green beside them.  
**R2-34 amended the attendance half again, in the opposite direction** (engine `4e2c6f8`, page + wiring `784a1b8`): where R2-32 read the sheet for one figure and discarded it, the days can now be **kept** — a **day book** (`src/lib/attendance.ts`, **557 lines**) living inside the *same* `taxflow:payroll:<orgId>` envelope as one `attendance` array of `{employee_id, date, mark, source: 'hand' | 'sheet', note, written_at}` marks. The doctrine is data, not copy: `dayBook()` returns the sentence *"Unwritten days are not absent days. The book counts only what the shop wrote."* and the page prints it. Three writes, three doors, and only one of them can move money. **A tap** cycles `DAY_MARK_CYCLE` (`'' · P · A · H · W · PL · SL · 0.5`) through `markDay()` — the single door for every mark, which refuses (with the window printed) a date before joining, after leaving, a future day, an unreadable mark, a person not in the register, and treats an empty mark as a *clear*, legal anywhere, because a mistake should be erasable rather than permanent; it keeps the shop's own spellings (`LL`, `NLW` canonicalise, an unknown mark is refused by name). **A pasted page** lands as marks through `marksFromPlan()` only if it was ruled with dates across the top — a summary sheet with a Present/LOP column has one figure and no days and stays the month run's business, and `attendanceTemplate(store, month, grid = true)` rules the blank page (`1..31` columns) that can be pasted back. The bridge's refusals travel by `AttendanceRefusal.kind`, never by parsing a sentence: a row that simply carries no marks (`kind: 'blank'`) is **noted and skipped** — a supervisor who marks only the absents is the common case, and a blank row is the sheet agreeing with the book's doctrine — while any other kind (a stranger, a shared code, an unreadable mark, an impossible day) **stops the whole page**, because a book with a silent gap in it looks complete; `writeSheetMarks()` refuses the batch whole on any refusal. **The pull** is the only write that can touch money: `pullMonthFromBook()` counts each person's marks, pushes every candidate row through the same `validateInput()` the month form runs (so the book cannot smuggle past joining windows, month length or the future-month rule), stamps each written row with the register's own provenance tag (*day book, October 2026: 3 of 31 day(s) marked*, replaced rather than stacked), and **leaves a person the book never wrote untouched rather than zeroed** — silence is not zero, the identical decision R2-32 made for the sheet — while an **empty book refuses to pull at all** (*"An empty book is not a month of absents — nothing is pulled, and nothing is deducted."*). **What the book finds** is printed, not stored: marks kept outside a person's roll window (named, counted nowhere), a weekly-off day marked at a loss (the count reads what was written), a day count that disagrees with the month row (the month row is what the salary uses — pull, or correct it by hand), and people with no day written named as exactly that. `clearMonthMarks()` is the per-person eraser for a page ruled for the wrong month, and `dayBookCsv()` prints the month's ruled page — the book's own marks in it — ending with the doctrine line, so the page is pasteable back a year later. Four stat cards read the footer (`people`, `cells_expected` / `cells_written`, `loss_days_total` / `people_with_loss`, `unwritten_days`). **No new key, no new vault spec, no version bump** — the vault's payroll spec grew only its `what`/`count`/`detail`/`verify` lines and `PAYROLL_VERSION` stays 1. **What was found on the way:** the bridge's first shape refused legitimate pages, because it inherited the sheet reader's refusals *including the blank rows it was built to tolerate* — the fix was to give every refusal a machine-readable `kind` and skip only `blank`, a `kind` the vault round-trip's sheet fixtures then required anyway; and the ruled month measured 1289px inside a 1130px column at 1440px until the day tracks became `minmax(20px, 28px)` with `width: 100%` cells and a `calc()` `min-width` — measured 1130/1130 with document overflow 0, scrolling inside its own `.pb-scroll` frame only below the 20px floor (Rule 15's lesson, re-learnt on a grid). 247 assertions in `/tmp/daybook-test.mjs`, with the 435 payroll, 212 attendance, 394 payslip and 95 shopping suites re-run green beside them.

### ADR-035 — An Audit Trail Is a Numbered Log, a Period Lock Is a Door a Save Path Asks, and the Register Keeps Neither a Rupee Nor a Signature — holds (new)

**Decision:** `/audit` (`src/lib/audit.ts` **611 lines** engine, `/audit/page.tsx` **716 lines**, R3-1 — the first round of the PDF review's buildable backlog) keeps two things that a bookkeeping app cannot answer from its documents alone: *who changed what* (an append-only trail) and *can a closed period still be edited* (a seal the shop puts on a GST month or a whole financial year). One new key, `taxflow:audit:<orgId>` at `AUDIT_VERSION = 1` (ADR-013), and Rule 31's same-round duty is met: the vault's `REGISTERS` list grew from **31 → 32 specs** (27 restorable) with `id: 'audit'` in the *workpapers* group, a `write:` to `saveAudit` and a `verify:` to `loadAudit` that counts both seals and events. Four rules carry it. **The trail has one writer and every row is numbered.** `append()` is the only function that touches `events`; every event gains a monotonic `seq` counting from 1 through it (`nextSeq = max(events.seq) + 1`), so an edit is not a silent overwrite but a new row. `verifyChain()` walks the log **by number rather than by order** — a run of `1,2,3,5` is a gap, `1,2,2,4` is a duplicate, and a re-sort the array does not fix is caught because the reader compares `events[i].seq === i + 1` — and names each broken number. This is **a checksum, not a signature**: it shows a hand-edit happened, it cannot say who, so the page prints *"the trail is unbroken"* and never a security claim stronger than that. **A period is sealed only once it has ended, and releasing is stamping rather than deleting.** `sealPeriod()` takes a `month` (`YYYY-MM` → its own first and last day) or a `year` (April → March of `FY 2025-26`) and refuses a range whose last day is today or later (*"A period is sealed only once it has ended — <label> runs to <range.to>, and today is <today>. The last day has not passed."*), refuses one that overlaps a seal already standing (`overlapsActiveSeal()` — a book with two opinions about the same window cannot answer a question), and refuses an unparseable shape. `releaseSeal()` **never removes the row**: it stamps `released_at` and requires a `release_reason` (*"Releasing a seal without a reason is a silent unlock — say why the period is being reopened."*), because a silent unlock is exactly what an auditor looks for, and a released seal stays visible forever as a released seal on the seals tab. `monthSealability()` gives the picker its shape — a month is offered only when its last day lies fully before today; a month in progress or in the future is not. **The seal is a door a write path asks, not a wall around the store.** `sealGuard(orgId, date)` is the one question a document save calls before it writes; it reads the register and returns the register's own sentence (*"<date> falls inside a sealed period — <label>, closed by <actor> on <dd>. Release the seal at /audit before editing a document dated there."*) so the page never has to invent one. When the answer is yes the invoice form shows a red inline banner with a *View seals* deep link, refuses the save, and calls `recordRefusal()` — the **attempt itself** lands on the trail as a `refused` event with its seq continuing, so a save that quietly did nothing still leaves evidence the shop tried, and `recordRefusal` builds its note article-aware (`an invoice`, `a bill`) so the trail reads as prose, not a template with a hole. **v1 wires the guard into only `/invoices/new`.** Bills, credit/debit notes, payments and expenses still save on their own paths. The `/audit` page names that boundary on the seals tab rather than implying a lock that quietly covers some doors and not others; widening the guard is a wiring task on each remaining write path, not a change to the engine. **The register holds no rupee.** A seal is a date range and an event is a sentence, so the money the seal protects stays in the documents it only governs; this is the fifth register under the stricter reading of Rule 31 / ADR-013 that ADR-028 (cost centres store a share), ADR-030 (projects store measurements), ADR-031 (recipes store quantities and minutes), and ADR-034 (payroll stores structure and exceptions) already follow — and the four `/audit` stat cards render their counts as `String()` values so `StatCard`'s default `formatCompactINR` cannot turn a `0` into `₹0.00` on a page that has no rupee to show.

**Reason:** the PDF review asks, of a bookkeeping app, two things the documents cannot answer on their own: an audit trail (who touched what) and a period lock (can a closed GST month still be edited). A tax file that cannot say the first is unauditable; a set of books that lets a March invoice be re-typed after the year has been filed is not a ledger, it is a draft. The register cannot solve the underlying trust problem — the file lives in the browser and no server validates a `seq` — but it can solve the honest half: a hand-edit that leaves the log with a gap or a duplicate is *visible*, and a closed period is *deliberately* reopened with a reason rather than quietly edited. The three refusals (a period still running, an overlap, an empty release reason) each exist because the alternative is worse: sealing a month you are still inside pretends at a history that is not finished; two seals over one window disagree; and a silent unlock is the exact pattern an auditor searches for. The rupee-less doctrine is the same refusal that keeps ADR-028/030/031/034 trustworthy — a stored rupee drifts from the documents the moment anyone corrects one, and the audit trail is the file a CA opens precisely when the documents are being argued about, so it must not carry an opinion of its own about money.

**Consequence:** the invoice save path now asks a question before it writes, which means an offline `outbox` invoice and a POS sale still bypass the seal (they save through `create-invoice.ts` directly), and the guard sits at `handleSave` inside `/invoices/new/page.tsx` — a real boundary, honestly printed. A future round that widens the guard wires the same two calls (`sealGuard()`, then `recordRefusal()` on refusal) into the bill, note, payment and expense save paths in the same place each runs its own validation. **R3-5 came and closed that consequence** (ADR-039): the question moved off `handleSave` and into the writers themselves — `create-invoice.ts`, `create-bill.ts` and `creditnote.ts` each call `requireOpenSeal()` before they take a sequence number — so an outbox invoice and a POS sale now inherit the seal from the writer they were already saving through, and nothing bypasses it except a write this app does not own. `handleSave` keeps its own `askSeal()` — re-counted at the R3-5 pass: **seven page files, nine call sites** (`/invoices/new`, `/bills` ×2, `/expenses`, `/payments`, `/quotations`, `/invoices/[id]` ×2, `/ai/scan-bill`) — not as the boundary but so the banner can print before the shop presses Save. **Corrected at the R3-5 pass:** the door is now the writer's, and `/audit`'s own `GUARDED` list prints where each of its twenty-one rows asks — twelve by `the writer`, one by `its own engine` (`/journal`), six by `this page`, two open on purpose — so the six page-carried rows (expense, payment, the invoice and bill paid/cancel stamps, quotation → invoice, and the scanned receipt the scan page keeps as an expense) are the ones the seal actually guards at the form, while `/invoices/new` and `/ai/scan-bill` ask both at the page and inside the writer. The `recordRefusal` note is built once inside the engine (`Refused ${article} ${target} write dated ${date} — ${guard.label} is closed. (${ref})`), so a page never paraphrases it, and the article-aware rule was caught by driving the page: the first draft read *"Refused a invoice write"*, and both the engine's own test and the page's needle were corrected together. `verifyChain` is not a signature — if the whole file is replaced, the trail restarts at 1 and the register cannot tell; this is stated on the page as *"the trail is unbroken"*, never as *"verified"*. The seal is not a *filing* lock — the shop can seal a period it has not actually filed, and the app cannot know it filed; that discipline is the owner's. The vault now walks **32 specs (27 restorable)** — the figure at this writing; it is **35 specs, 30 restorable** since R3-4's filings register — and its own `plural()` bug — the projects register's row reading *"3 time entrys"* (ADR-030's spec counted rows) — is a copy polish deliberately deferred under Remaining per the standing directive to keep small changes for later. **That deferral ended at R3-5:** the row now reads *"3 time entries"*. `AUDIT_VERSION = 1` ships with an empty register for every org, so nothing to migrate. 122 assertions in `/tmp/audit-test.mjs` — **172 since R3-5**, whose extra section reads the `GUARDED` coverage list back out of `/audit`'s own source rather than trusting a typed caption — cover every refusal, both seal kinds and their ranges, the release branch with its empty-reason block, the `sealGuard` sentence, the `refused` event that continues the seq and its article-aware wording, the `recordNote` trim branch, and every shape `verifyChain` catches — clean, gap, duplicate, reorder — with a real `parseAudit` round trip through the envelope's own version guard. Live in the browser: a September 2026 seal seen land on the owner's register, the invoice form's red banner and its *View seals* deep link, the save refused with the register's own sentence and the *Write refused* event seen continuing the trail, the reason-gated release seen keeping the seal as *Released* rather than deleting it, both CSVs exported through a Blob capture (the 8-column trail sheet, the 11-column seal sheet with its release date and reason on the row), the phone layout measured at 355/1440 with no page-level overflow, and every probe row and event removed afterwards so the register came back to the state it was in.

### ADR-036 — The Shape of a Number Is the Shop's, the Counter Belongs to the Register, and a Branch Is a Registration That Stamps Its Name Into the Notes — holds (new)
