**Decision:** `/business` (`src/lib/setup.ts` **547 lines** engine, `/business/page.tsx` **663 lines**, R3-2 — the second round of the PDF review's buildable backlog) answers the two accounting-setup questions the review lists — *multiple GST registrations, branches, financial years and configurable document series* — and adds a third the review's "proper double-entry / opening balances" line implies. One new key, `taxflow:setup:<orgId>` at `SETUP_VERSION = 1` (ADR-013), and Rule 31's same-round duty is met: the vault's `REGISTERS` grew from **32 → 33 specs** (28 restorable) with `id: 'setup'` in the *settings* group, a shape-guarded `accepts:`, a `write:` to `saveSetup` and a `verify:` counting branches + series overrides. Five rules carry it. **The register dresses a number; it does not count it.** `composeNumber(fmt, n, fy)` puts an already-counted integer into a shape (prefix, zero-pad 1–8, and whether the FY sits inside), while the invoice counter stays the Supabase `invoice_sequences` row per org per FY and each order kind keeps its own localStorage high-water mark — so a mid-year shape change re-issues nothing and papers keep the shape they were born with; proven live twice (under a `TAX` override the form previewed `TAX-2026-27-0012`, count 12 carried from Supabase, while the invoice list still read `INV-…`, and after reverting the next number wore `INV-2026-27-0012` without restarting). Because a kind can change shape, `seriesHistory()` hands the read walk every shape that kind has worn and `usedNumber()` / `usedNumberAnyYear()` parse each, so a *running* (year-less) series inherits the largest count its year-shaped family reached rather than restarting at 1. **One prefix, one series.** `validateSeries()` refuses a prefix another kind already wears (*"… two series with one prefix would issue the same number twice"*), because the counters are per kind and a shared prefix would double-issue silently. **A branch is a registration.** Each carries its own 15-character GSTIN (the same shape rule `einvoice.ts` uses; the checksum digits stay honestly unverified without a GSP) and its state code, and `validateBranch()` refuses the pair when they disagree — *"a Tamil registration cannot carry a <state> branch. Place of supply and the CGST/SGST split read this pair."* — with codes and GSTINs unique, the GSTIN pre-filling its state, and the first branch the head by construction. **The head is held, not owned, and history does not go out of business.** `setHead()` demotes every other row in one write; the head cannot be closed or deleted until handed over (`deleteBranch`/`setBranchActive` refuse it — *"the header loses the signature it prints by default"*), and a branch still stamped on any invoice can only be **deactivated**, never deleted, the `referenced` answer read from the `invoices` rows themselves (an `ilike` probe on `[branch CODE]`) and passed into `deleteBranch()` as an argument, so the refusal comes from the books and not the modal. **The stamp rides the notes, not the schema** (Rule 21 unbroken — no column added): `[branch CODE]` is written at the front of the notes, replace-never-stack, the form's *Issuing branch* picker swaps the printed seller header, and the print page reads it back with a **named fallback** under the primary registration when the register can no longer answer — while the statutory pages (GSTR-1, e-invoicing) still sign with the primary registration, because this is what the paper prints, not what the government holds. **`/business` holds no rupee**, and `ledger.ts`'s `OpeningBalances` grew additively (`bank`, `receivables`, `payables`, `as_of`) under the `openingStood = !as_of || as_of <= asOf` gate, so the balance sheet counts an opening only once the day it was struck has arrived, with the honest limit printed: a later payment grows `cash` but cannot reduce an opening receivable.

**Reason:** the review's first backlog item after the audit trail is *business setup & accounting depth*, and a shop that grows past one office needs two things the current single-registration, hard-coded-numbering app cannot give it — a document that names which of its registrations issued it, and a number that carries the shop's own prefix rather than the code's `INV-`. The shape-vs-counter split is the whole decision: the app already counts correctly in `invoice_sequences` and the order kinds' high-water marks, so a *new* counter would create a second answer that drifts (the exact failure ADR-028/030/031/034/035 all refuse), whereas dressing the printed shape is reversible and re-issues nothing. The prefix-collision refusal exists because counters are per kind — two kinds on one prefix would each happily issue `TAX-2026-27-0001` and the shop would only find out when a customer held both papers. A branch is modelled as a registration rather than a free-text location because the GSTIN and its leading state digits are what every tax split derives from; letting them disagree is how a place-of-supply error is born. Deactivate-not-delete and the head handover are the same honesty the audit seal applies to a period: a record documents a decision rather than erasing history, and a paper stamped with a branch code must keep resolving to the place it was issued from. The stamp lives in `notes` and the opening balances in the ledger because the schema is hand-managed (ADR-013) — the alternative is a migration this app's rules forbid.

**Consequence:** every numbered write path that wants the configured shape now imports `setup.ts`'s `resolveSeries()` + `composeNumber()` and threads an optional `SeriesFormat` through `create-invoice.ts`, `orderflow.ts` (`peekNumber`/`takeNumber`/`appendDraft`/`raiseDrafts`), the challans page, the shared `OrderRegister`, the quotations page (its own numbers *and* the invoice its convert path raises) and the recipes materials board — six call sites dressed, and the counters untouched at all of them. A kind whose counter already ran under an old prefix is read back through `seriesHistory()`, so changing shape mid-life never re-issues; a *running* (year-less) series deliberately reads the widest net (`usedNumberAnyYear`) so dropping the year from the numbers does not restart the count. Credit/debit notes and purchase bills stay typed by hand — the portal and the supplier number those — so they are deliberately *not* in `SERIES_KINDS`. The `taxflow:opening:` key's shape grew additively, so every legacy file still reads and the four original lines stay unconditional; the balance-sheet round trip was seen live tie out (assets 15,00,452 = liabilities 5,46,111 + retained 9,13,821 + derived capital 40,520) with the new sub-lines printed. **The stamp → read-back → delete-refusal chain is engine-tested (131 assertions), not lived on a stamped invoice** — the UI offers no invoice delete and no shop's books were dirtied with un-deletable probe paper; a later round that adds an invoice delete should drive that chain end-to-end. `SETUP_VERSION = 1` ships an empty register for every org, nothing to migrate; the vault's own `plural()` bug (a row reading *"3 time entrys"*) stays deliberately deferred under Remaining per the standing directive to keep small copy polish for later. 131 assertions in `/tmp/setup-test.mjs` cover the store parse/version guards, every `validateBranch` refusal, head handover, the close-vs-delete honesty, `validateSeries` including the collision, the `composeNumber` shapes, the worn-shape high-water reads and both CSV row builders, with the audit suite's 122 re-run green against the grown `ledger.ts` / `orderflow.ts` surfaces. Live in the browser: branch CRUD with the GSTIN→state auto-fill and auto-head seen, the series editor's live preview recomposing before Save, the `TAX` and challan overrides seen dressing new papers while old ones kept their born shapes, the collision refusal seen verbatim, the invoice form's seller swap to a Karnataka branch seen print its GSTIN/state/address with *(head)* named in the picker, the accounting opening round-trip seen tie out, the head-delete refusal seen open the editor with the engine's sentence, the invoice list regression seen clean (no banner, primary GSTIN, no probe leak), the 355/1440 width pass, and every probe reverted (branch unheaded and deleted, opening register back to zeros).

### ADR-037 — Judgement May Be Written By Hand Only Where It Balances, Is Answered Rather Than Erased, and Asks the Seal — holds (new)

**Decision:** `/journal` (`src/lib/journal.ts` **1,135 lines** engine, `/journal/page.tsx` **1,335 lines**, R3-2b — the review's "proper double-entry" line, taken literally) lets the shop state a movement no document raised: depreciation, drawings, a loan that arrived as cash rather than a bill, a provision a GST notice has started and an invoice has not. Everything else in this app is written *backwards* from a document; a journal is written *forwards*, which is exactly why it needs three gates rather than a text box. **A head declares its line** — each of the fifteen `StatementSlot`s carries one `SLOT_META` record with a `natural` side (`debit` for assets and expenses, `credit` for liabilities, income and capital), and that single declaration is the only place the convention lives, so the fold cannot get an income leg backwards and there is no second sign anywhere to drift; a head is the shop's own word for that line, six `STARTER_HEADS` are offered on a click and never seeded, and a head that has taken a rupee is **closed rather than deleted** (`deleteHead` refuses, `headUsage()` answers from the register). **Nothing reaches a statement that has not been posted, and posting is arithmetic** — `totals()` sums through `paise()` so a float tail cannot decide whether an entry balances, and `validateLegs(legs, store, posting)` refuses with the engine's own sentences: fewer than two legs, a leg with no head, a leg naming a head that is gone, a leg on a closed head (only at posting — a draft may remember a head that has since closed), a ₹0 leg, a leg that rounds to nothing, a side that is neither debit nor credit, one head on both sides of the same entry, and the balance itself with the three figures named (*"An entry is posted only when it balances — debits …, credits …, out by …"*). **A posted entry is answered, not erased** — `writeEntry` refuses a non-draft, `deleteEntry` refuses a posted row (*"A delete would leave a number with no entry"*), and `reverseEntry()` builds a mirror with **every leg re-summoned from the original and only its side flipped**, a required reason, a date that must be readable and not in the future, and one answer per entry; `foldableEntries()` filters drafts only, so a reversed pair *still folds*, nets to nothing, and stays visible on the register. And because every posting balanced on the way in, an entry **renames money without moving the derived plug** (Δplug = Σdebits − Σcredits, and a capital-named head gives the room back by name through `journalCapital`) — a claim `plugProof()` reads off the rows the sheet is holding and prints beside the figures on `/accounting` and in `/ai/ask`, per Rule 33's *beside, never merged*. Numbers wear `/business`'s sixth series (`JR-<FY>-NNNN`) over this register's own high-water `seq` map, counted nowhere else; `peekNumber()` allocates nothing, and a draft takes no number, so deleting one cannot punch a hole in a series an auditor reads. **One new key**, `taxflow:journal:<orgId>` at `JOURNAL_VERSION = 1` (ADR-013), and Rule 31's same-round duty is met: `REGISTERS` grew **33 → 34 specs (29 restorable)** with `id: 'journal'`, an `accepts:` shape guard, a `write:` to `saveJournal` and a `verify:` counting postings. This is the app's **one deliberate exception** to "money records are never stored here" — it holds rupees, but as *stated movements on named statement lines*, never as a cash figure, and it is still the shop's own discipline kept in this browser.

**Reason:** the PDF review's second backlog step is accounting depth, and a shop whose books were carried over from a file has real, lawful movements with no paper behind them. Refusing them means the statements are wrong; accepting a free-text amount means the statements become uncheckable. The three gates are what makes the difference survivable: balance-before-post means a journal cannot invent a rupee (it can only move one between named lines), reverse-not-edit means a mistake leaves two rows rather than zero evidence, and the seal question means a closed period stays closed for judgement as well as for documents — which is why `stopForSeal()` is the *second* door on this register (`/invoices/new` being R3-1's first) and asks **before** the legs are read, because a sealed window is a statement about the date, not about the row being typed, and the attempt is logged as a `refused` event so an edit that never happened is still evidence. The sign convention lives in exactly one table because double-entry's classic failure is a second copy disagreeing with the first. And the register files nothing: no return, portal or workpaper reads a journal row, so the boundary is printed as four facts — it files nothing, it settles no bill, it does not reach the bank, and there is no chart of accounts, no account codes and no trial balance here — rather than implied by a page that looks like accounting software.

**Consequence:** `ledger.ts` (**296 → 450 lines**) now takes an optional third argument in both derivations and folds the postings into profit *and* into the balance sheet, so a journal figure lands on the line its head names and nowhere else, `otherAssets` / `otherLiabilities` carry the heads' own names, `journalCapital` is subtracted by name, and a `touched` count lets the foot say *nothing moved* when the legs answered each other. That threading reached four more surfaces in the same commit: `/accounting` (500 → **592**), `/audit` (716 → **725**, its coverage sentence now generated from `GUARDED` via `sealedDoors()` so a page can never claim coverage the wiring has not earned — *New invoice* and *Journal entry* live; *New purchase bill*, *Payment received* and *GSTR-1 filing* are not), `src/lib/ask.ts` (1,227 → **1,261**) and `/ai/ask` (which now says which answers exclude hand-written judgement), plus `nav.ts`, `commands.ts` (two ⌘K actions), `Icons.tsx` (the 73rd glyph — a page with a T-account ruled on it, since none of `book`/`clipboard`/`scale`/`edit` says *a movement the shop stated by hand*), `setup.ts` (`journal` as the sixth kind, `JR`/pad 4/FY inside by default) and `business/page.tsx`, whose flash copy had to switch from `${label}s` to `SERIES_KIND_LABEL[kind].plural` or it would have printed *"Journal entrys now wear JR…"* — the same naive plural the vault still carries for the projects spec. **Every future round that touches the statements now has two sources to reconcile** — documents and judgement — which is why the fold prints beside the paper rather than inside it, and why the unbalanced count and dangling-leg count on `/journal` are *findings* rather than states: by construction both are zero, and a non-zero means a hand-edit of the JSON, the same finding a `seq` gap is on the trail. `JOURNAL_VERSION = 1` ships an empty register, nothing to migrate. Verification: `tsc` 0; `eslint` 0 new against the pre-existing `no-explicit-any` baseline in `ask.ts` and `/ai/ask/page.tsx`; `npm run build` ✓ **67 routes** with `/journal` prerendered; **393 assertions in `/tmp/journal-test.mjs`** and **250 in `/tmp/fold-test.mjs`** — the latter walking **every pair of the fifteen statement lines** so the plug invariant is proven per pair rather than eyeballed — with the audit suite's **122** and the setup suite's **131** re-run green at the docs pass (**896 across the four suites that exist in `/tmp`**). Live on the owner's org: a draft inside the standing September 2026 seal seen refused with its `refused` event on the trail, **JR-2026-27-0001** (₹40,000 Depreciation / ₹40,000 Accumulated depreciation) seen posted and answered by its mirror **0002**, and **0003** (₹2,500 Bank charges / ₹2,500 Loan received) seen standing, with `seq {"journal:2026-27": 3}` and eight open heads read back from the key. Two honest limits: **the rows are left behind on purpose** — a posted entry is answered not deleted and the trail only grows, so this is the first round whose demo data is permanent by design, not a probe that was forgotten; and the phone pass could **not be measured** (the in-app surface reported `window.innerWidth === 0`), so it was substituted by a static CSS audit — 0 hex, 145 `var()` uses over 24 names of which **23 resolve against `:root`** and one (`--mono`) is defined nowhere and rides only its fallback, 2 `rgba` both shared with other pages, 89 distinct `.jn-*` class names, breakpoints at 1100/980/768 where the modal becomes the house bottom sheet — plus an `evaluate_script` DOM drive of the golden path; a static check is not a measured pass and is not recorded as one. Follow-ups recorded rather than fixed: define `--mono` (or reuse the app's only mono stack, declared inside `.tf-cmd-kbd` at `globals.css:755`) so the register's figures share the app's recipe, and the vault's `plural()` *"3 time entrys"* stays deferred. Widening the seal to the remaining doors (bills, notes, payments, expenses) is a wiring task on each write path, not a change to either engine.

### ADR-038 — A Filing Register Transcribes What the Portal Says, Posts Nothing, and Reads the Seal Instead of Guarding a Door — holds (new)

**Decision:** `/gst/filings` (`src/lib/filings.ts` **2,279 lines / 118 exports** engine, `/gst/filings/page.tsx` **2,079 lines**, R3-4 — the third buildable line of the PDF review: *"live GSP workflows, **filing-status history, amendment tracking** and period locks"*) keeps the two halves that line names which this app had no page for. Period locks arrived as R3-1 and a GSP feed stays blocked on the schema, but *what each return stated, when it went in, and what was done about the paper that missed its window* had no home: `/gst/calendar` knows a return is **due** and carries a tick, and `/gst/gstr9` knows what a year's 3B **stated** for one workpaper — neither answers the department's question, and a tick cannot be audited. One new key, `taxflow:filings:<orgId>` at `FILINGS_VERSION = 1` (ADR-013), six forms (`gstr1 · gstr1a · gstr3b · cmp08 · gstr4 · gstr9`), three cadences (`monthly · qrmp · composition`), three statuses (`not_filed · filed · nil`), five amendment kinds and nine row states. **A row is a transcript, not a posting**: the figures are what the return *states*, they are stored rather than re-derived from this register's own taxable amounts, `ledger.ts` reads none of it, and `reference` is kept exactly as the shop writes it because nothing here asks the portal. Five conventions carry it. **A due date is snapshotted, then audited** — `due` copies the calendar's date onto the row (a filed history must not silently re-date itself when the calendar's rule text is later corrected) and `statutoryDue()` independently re-derives the day from the rule where a rule states one (the 20th under rule 61(1)(i); the **22nd or 24th** under 61(1)(ii) by the state code of the principal place of business; the 18th under 62(1)(i); 30 June under the proviso to 62(1)(ii) from FY 2024-25; 31 December for CMP-08's preceding-quarter payment and for the annual return), with `expectedRows()`/`calendarObjections()` printing **only the disagreements** as drift (Rule 29) and a QRMP filer's twelve monthly summary rows **folded into the four quarters rule 61(1)(ii) actually speaks of** — the fold printed, not hidden. **A late fee is a slab, not a headline** — section 47's ₹50/day under Notifications No. 19, 20 and 21/2021-Central Tax dated 01.06.2021 (₹2,000 up to ₹1.5 crore of the preceding year's aggregate turnover, ₹5,000 up to ₹5 crore, ₹10,000 above, ₹500 on a nil return) and GSTR-9's own table under Notification No. 07/2023-Central Tax dated 31.03.2023 (₹50/₹100/₹200 a day capped at 0.04% / 0.04% / 0.50% of the turnover *in that State or Union territory*), with the deciding notification returned in the `basis` and printed **beside** the calendar's ₹5,000 headline rather than merged into it (Rule 33); the composition ceilings are labelled *"this register's reading"* because they could not be verified from a dated source this round. **An amendment lives inside a window** — the proviso to rule 59(1) (GSTR-1A stays open *after* that period's GSTR-1 and shuts the moment its 3B goes in, so the date printed on that door is the 3B's own), Table 9A (open until GSTR-9 is filed), and section 16(4)'s credit deadline as reproduced verbatim in Circular No. 237/31/2024-GST dated 15.10.2024 (*"the thirtieth day of November following the end of financial year … or furnishing of the relevant annual return, whichever is earlier"*) — and `windowsFor()` / `amendmentBoard()` print **lapsed** when every clock has shut, because a correction nobody can file is not a to-do; paper naming a document no book holds is flagged `dangling`, a finding rather than a correction. **The bars are quoted, not invented** — rule 59(6)(a) (no GSTR-1 while the previous period's 3B is unfurnished), 59(6)(b) (the same for a quarterly filer's Invoice Furnishing Facility, with the 2024 amendment recorded where the two readings differ) and 59(6)(d) (no next GSTR-1 while a rule 88C(1) intimation stands unpaid or unanswered against a return filed under rule 59(10)) — and an **empty register legitimately prints the 59(6)(a) bar** rather than a clean sheet. **The seal is read and handed, never asked**: unlike `/journal` (Rule 40), no door stands here because a transcript moves no rupee on the statements, so the engine reads a period's seal through `sealForPeriod()` (R3-5 folded this register's second spelling, `sealOf()`, into it, so a period label has exactly one path to a seal), which shows a sealed period as closed paper with a lock badge on the row, `sealFilingsPeriod()` **hands** the close to `/audit`'s own `sealPeriod()` and keeps its rules (an ended period, no overlap — and a quarter is not a seal kind, so asking for one refuses by naming the three months it would take and points at `/audit`), and every status change appends one line to that same append-only trail (Rule 37 — another register's control is drawn, never duplicated). Joined the vault as its **35th spec (30th restorable)** in the round it was created (Rule 31) with `id: 'filings'`, an `accepts:` requiring **both** arrays by shape (because `parseFilings` keeps the first row per `(form, period)`, so a wrong-version file would otherwise restore as a shorter register and print as a success) and a `verify:` counting returns + amendments.

**Reason:** the review names filing-status history and amendment tracking as the gap between a compliance calendar and a tax department's actual work, and both are *records of what was stated* rather than calculations — the app already computes a due date (`compliance.ts`) and already derives what the books hold (the documents), so the missing object was the shop's own account of the portal. The snapshot-then-audit split is the whole decision: a filed history that re-derives its dates live would silently rewrite the past every time the calendar's rule text is corrected, whereas a copied date plus a printed disagreement keeps the record frozen and still shows the shop where the app and the statute differ. The slab-not-headline rule exists because the app's calendar card prints ₹5,000, which is the *middle* of three ceilings and double the cap on a nil return — a single headline is how a small shop is told it owes more than it does. Amendment windows are kept as three separate clocks because the three mechanisms genuinely have three shutters, and printing an unfillable correction as an open to-do is the failure mode the review's "amendment tracking" line is complaining about. And the seal is read rather than asked because the honest distinction between this register and the journal is *what a row does to the statements*: a journal posting moves a figure, so it must ask permission; a filing row records what a return said, so the only meaningful thing a seal can tell it is that the period is closed paper. Making filings a guarded door would have implied that deleting a transcript reopens a book.

**Consequence:** two things in the app now speak about the same return and must not drift. `/gst/gstr9`'s 9C table and `/gst/filings`' variance tab compare **the same two sides** — books against what a 3B stated — so they share `RECON_TOLERANCE = 1` (the ₹1 the 9C workpaper already used; both pages were checked to agree on what a rupee means) and the new tab prints the gap as *books minus stated*, compares a **nil return as nothing** rather than skipping it, skips an unfiled period, and calls an unrecorded row `unset` rather than zero. `/gst/calendar` keeps its ₹5,000 headline but now points at this register for the slab, `/audit` gained a sentence naming filings as a register that *reads* the seal and guards no door (so the coverage list cannot outrun the wiring — Rule 38), and the GST hub distinguishes its own tick from this register's transcript. `compliance.ts` was corrected rather than bypassed in two places the fold exposed: `quarterLabel()` now takes the **financial-year start** instead of the supply month's calendar year (Q4 of FY 2026-27 is January–March 2027 and was reading *"Q4 2027-28"*), and GSTR-4's annual due date moved to **30 June** under the proviso to rule 62(1)(ii) for FY 2024-25 onward while keeping 30 April for an earlier year; the calendar still emits **monthly** summary rows for a quarterly filer and still emits **no PMT-06 deposit row** (rule 61(3), due the 25th) — the fold lives in this register and teaching the emitter is the deferred half. *(Superseded by R3-6 / ADR-040: `compliance.ts`'s emitter now writes the four quarters, the eight IFF windows and the eight PMT-06 days, and `qrmpCategory()` moved here-from `compliance.ts` as the one shared table; `eventPeriod()` keeps only the period-key fold, and `filings.ts` now re-exports the emitter's `qrmpCategory`.)* Wiring: `nav.ts` one sidebar row in the GST group, `Icons.tsx` the **74th** glyph (`filing` — a return sheet with the day stamped across it, since none of `invoice` / `file-check` / `book` / `calendar` says *a transcript of what a return stated, kept by period*), `commands.ts` two ⌘K actions (`action:record-filing` → `?new=1`, `action:amendment-board` → `?tab=amendments`), three tabs (`returns · amendments · variance`) and three CSVs (27 / 17 / 10 columns), deep links `?tab= ?period= ?new=1 ?settings=1`, and a `UNTRACKED_CALENDAR_FORMS` table so a form the calendar names and this register does not keep prints with its rule instead of being dropped. **v1 says no five times over** — `NOT_TRACKED` (5 entries) names the portal/GSP feed and ARN verification, the IFF, the PMT-06 deposit, refunds and DRC notices and demands, and any effect on the statements, each with its rule; the register files nothing, pays nothing, posts nothing and reaches no bank. Verification: `tsc` 0; `npm run build` ✓ compiled with `/gst/filings` prerendered (**75** route entries listed — corrected at the R3-5 pass, where the build was measured row by row: **68** rows, the same composition as its sibling note in the R3-4 session log below; the file count is the figure that holds); **67** route files on disk — 65 `page.tsx` + 2 `api/route.ts`); `eslint` 0 new (the 10 `no-explicit-any` errors in `/gst/gstr9/page.tsx` are a HEAD-baseline pre-existing count — 10 before, 10 after, and this round's diff there is JSX and CSS only); **446 assertions in `/tmp/filings-test.mjs`**, with the four surviving suites re-run green at **393 + 250 + 122 + 131 = 1,342 across the five in `/tmp`, 0 failing**. The suite caught two real bugs the first draft shipped with: every *agreeing* due date was printing a drift sentence that said the opposite of what it meant (only disagreements should print), and the Q4 fold lost its calendar-year carry, dating a 2026-Q4 return a year early. Two honest limits: **the live drive could not be completed** — the browser session has no signed-in Supabase user, so the register is engine-verified and CSS-audited, not driven end-to-end, and that pass is still owed; and the responsive claims are read off the page's own CSS (three `@media` queries at 1180 / 1040 / 768; the surface reports `innerWidth === 0`), which is not a measured phone pass. The docs pass also caught and fixed two off-recipe style literals the page shipped with (`rgba(15, 23, 32, 0.42)` scrim → the house `rgba(15, 23, 42, 0.45)`, and a unique modal shadow → `var(--shadow-lg)`).



### ADR-039 — A Seal Is a Door on the Writer, Not on the Page; the Page That Hosts the Button Inherits It — holds (new)

**Decision:** R3-5 took the period seal R3-1 shipped and **moved the question from the screens into the code those screens already call**. `src/lib/audit.ts` (**611 → 673 lines**) now offers three forms of one question — `sealGuard()` **reads**, `askSeal()` **reads and puts the attempt on the trail** and returns the guard or `null`, `requireOpenSeal()` does both and **throws** `SealedWriteError` whose `message` *is* the register's sentence (a stable `name`, a `.sealed` flag, so a caller can tell a sealed stop from any other failure). The throwing form went in as the **first statement** of the three shared writers — `create-invoice.ts` (**118 → 132**), `create-bill.ts` (**69 → 81**), `creditnote.ts` (**116 → 132**) — above the sequence-number read, so twelve paths inherit one door (`/invoices/new`, `/pos`, `/recurring`, `/offline`, `/sales-orders`, `/purchase-orders`, `/challans`, `/tally`, `/ai/scan-bill`, `/bills`, `/credit-notes`, `/debit-notes`) and a refusal cannot burn a number. Where no writer exists, the page carries its own door through `askSeal`: `/expenses`, `/payments`, `/quotations`, `/invoices/[id]` (paid stamp and cancel), `/bills` (the same two) and the scan page's *keep as an expense* half; `/journal`'s `stopForSeal()` moved from `sealGuard` to `askSeal` inside `journal.ts` (**1,135 → 1,139**) because its refusals belong on the trail like everyone else's. `/audit`'s `GUARDED` (**730 → 772** with it) became the ledger of the whole arrangement — **21 rows labelled by which hand writes**: `the writer` ×12, `its own engine` ×1, `this page` ×6, and **2 printed `not wired` on purpose** (master records carry no date; GSTR-1 filing status is not dated money) — with every count in the printed coverage sentence computed off that array (`doorsAsking`/`doorsWriter`/`doorsEngine`/`doorsPage`) and the audit suite **re-reading the array from source** to assert each labelled row's page really mentions the seal, that every `href` resolves to a real `page.tsx`, and that at least one row stays open. One component carries the visible half: `src/components/SealNotice.tsx` (**47 lines**, the tenth in `src/components`) draws `.tf-notice` (new in `globals.css`) from `sealGuard` **only** — reading is not an attempt, and only an attempt belongs on an append-only trail — and is hung on **eight pages**. `filings.ts` (**2,279 → 2,282**) folded its dead second spelling, `sealOf()`, into `sealForPeriod()` standing on `audit.ts`'s `sealForDate()`, so a period label has exactly one path to a seal. Codified as **Rule 42**.

**Reason:** R3-1 shipped a seal with one door and R3-2b added a second, and both were placed on the screen where the rupee was typed. That is the wrong altitude for a control whose subject is *a window of dates*: a door on a form governs only the forms somebody thought of that day, so POS sales, recurring catch-ups, outbox replays, order conversions, challan conversions, Tally vouchers, scanned bills, purchase bills and both notes could all still write inside a closed month while the page that *did* ask looked protected. The writers were already shared (R2-27 extracted `createNote`; #28 extracted `createInvoice`), so widening was a wiring job with one call site each rather than a new abstraction. The three forms exist because the callers genuinely differ: a banner must not write evidence just by being seen, a page that reports a stop wants a value rather than an exception, and a shared writer has no screen to draw on and must refuse upward to whichever caller is nearest the user. The door sits **before the number** because a refusal that lands after `last_number + 1` leaves a hole in a statutory series — a gap an officer reads as a deleted invoice. The list is checked by the suite rather than written by hand because this page's recurring failure mode has been copy claiming coverage the wiring has not earned (Rule 38 caught the sentence, R3-2b caught the count, and R3-5 caught both a dead `/bills/new` href and `/journal` labelled as a page door when the engine holds it).

**Consequence:** the seal's coverage claim is now a **number that changes by itself** — nineteen of twenty-one — and `/audit` says where each door is, so a future path that skips the writer is visible as a hole rather than implied as covered. What stays outside is named on the page: **masters and filing status are open on purpose**, and the boundary sentence moved to where it is read — *the door is in this app's writers*, so a row changed through the Supabase table directly or by a client that does not consult `audit.ts` is not stopped, exactly as the trail is a checksum over `seq` and not a signature; no schema, no migration and no RLS policy was added (Rule 21 unbroken), and **R3-3's durable backend remains the place where that boundary could move**. Eight pages hang the banner and print the writer's sentence in their own error slot; a ninth door is carried without one — `/quotations`' convert path types an invoice straight into the table, so it asks `askSeal` itself, before the number, and reports the stop in its own words, `invoices/new` collapsed `sealGuard` + `recordRefusal` into one `askSeal` call and replaced its inline red box with `<SealNotice>` — its page-level ask **stays** beside the writer's, deliberately: the form should say *sealed* before the button is pressed rather than throw after — `globals.css` gained `--mono` (the token R3-2b's journal used and no `:root` answered) plus the `.tf-notice` block, and `vault.ts`'s `plural()` learned `ies` so the projects row reads *"3 time entries"*. Ledger after the round: **65 page files / 67 route files, 54 libraries, 74 glyphs, 10 component files, 35 vault specs (30 restorable)** — no new route, key, spec or glyph, because a wider door is a rule change, not a new register. Verification is `tsc` 0, `eslint` at the HEAD baseline, `npm run build` ✓ and **1,392 assertions across the five `/tmp` suites, 0 failing** (audit **122 → 172**); **the live browser drive is still owed** — no signed-in session, so no banner, refusal or `refused` event has been produced through the UI, and neither `/gst/filings` (R3-4) nor these doors (R3-5) has been walked end-to-end in a real org.

### ADR-040 — A Calendar Emits the Period the Rule Names, and Every Day, Sum and Citation on It Is Read From a Primary Text or Labelled as the Shop's Own — holds (new)

**Decision:** R3-6 taught the compliance calendar what the QRMP scheme actually asks of a shop, and then went back through everything the app had already written about that scheme and checked it against the statute itself. `src/lib/compliance.ts` (**313 → 407 lines**) now emits, for a quarterly filer, the **four** quarters rule 61(1)(ii) speaks of rather than twelve monthly summary rows, and adds the paper and money the quarter leaves behind: **eight** Invoice Furnishing Facility windows (the thirteenth, under rule 59(2)) and **eight** FORM GST PMT-06 deposits (the twenty-fifth, under rule 61(3)) per year, with `profile.filing === 'monthly'` now the only gate on the twentieth-day GSTR-3B row. A `Profile.stateCode` arrived with them — the two digits of the GSTIN that say which of rule 61(1)(ii)'s two rows a shop sits in — and `qrmpCategory()` became the **one** function that answers it: `QRMP_TWENTY_SECOND` / `QRMP_TWENTY_FOURTH` live here, keyed by state *name* against `INDIAN_STATES` (the app's two-digit codes are its own, not all the codes the portal issues), and `filings.ts` deleted its own half-read copy of that table and re-exports this one (`export { qrmpCategory, type QrmpCategory }`), so the emitter that sets the date and the register that audits it cannot disagree about which day a state is in. `src/lib/filings.ts` (**2,282 → 2,296 lines**, 117 `^export` lines) kept the month-to-quarter fold only for **keys** — `eventPeriod()` still lands a row saved against any month of a quarter inside the quarter that owns it, because a register keeps periods and a calendar prints dates — retired the two objections that existed only while the emitter was wrong, and replaced them with one that is true: the day the calendar now prints is certain while the rupee beside it is not, because rule 61(3) *"asks for the tax due under proviso to sub-section (7) of section 39"* and states **no sum** at all. `/gst/calendar` (**520 → 593 lines**) gained the control that sets the day — a state selector shown only to a QRMP filer, prefilled from the shop's own primary `gst_registrations` row, which fills a blank and never overwrites a state the owner already chose — and a second paragraph on the cost-of-slipping card saying that a deposit is not a return, so it adds nothing to that figure. Codified as **Rule 43**.

**Reason:** for two rounds the honest-looking answer was downstream: the calendar printed a date the rule could not produce and the filings register folded it and complained in print. Rule 29 says a disagreement is *found, then shown* — but that contract is between two texts, and here the app was simply stating the wrong period on the screen a shop reads before the register exists. Fixing the emitter was the only move that left the fold doing what a fold is for. The second half of the round was forced by the same discipline applied to the app's own copy: the deposit and the IFF had accumulated figures nobody had read from the rule — a **"six per cent"** deposit, a **12% interest cap under "the second proviso to rule 61(3)"** (no such proviso exists, and no 12% appears in the consolidated rules as on 01.10.2025), two notifications cited as **13/2025 and 14/2025 dated 17.02.2025** (the 2025 pair in that range is dated 17.09.2025 and covers handicrafts, bricks and rule 31A valuation), a **Circular 143 dated 23.02.2020** carrying a *"two days prior to the due date for filing of GSTR-1"* reading that appears nowhere in it, an IFF window described as **inter-State only**, an IFF date on the **eleventh**, and a claim that rule 61(4) has barred top-ups since 01.04.2025. All of it was swept after reading the primary texts — CBIC's own rule 61 and rule 59 pages, Notification No. 85/2020-Central Tax and Notification No. 83/2020-Central Tax (both dated 10.11.2020) from the GST Council's own PDFs, and Circular No. 143/13/2020-**GST** dated **10.11.2020** from its own PDF — which is also what settled the round's open questions: Chandigarh and Delhi are both in the **twenty-fourth** row, the ₹50 lakh IFF ceiling covers inter-State **and** intra-State B2B (rule 59(5)(a) names both), the fixed deposit sum is **thirty-five per cent of the tax paid in cash in the last GSTR-3B of the immediately preceding quarter** — **notified by Notification No. 85/2020-Central Tax dated 10.11.2020**, issued under section 148 read with the proviso to section 39(7), with para 6.1(a) of Circular 143 being the Board's explanatory reading rather than the source — late interest runs *"at the applicable rate"* from the twenty-fifth (para 8.1(ii)) with **no interest at all** when the system-calculated fixed sum went in on time and the quarter is cleared in its own 3B (para 8.1(i)), and the rule's own words fix the IFF window at the **thirteenth** so no circular has to be read into it. The app works interest at the **eighteen per annum of section 50(1)** — the rate the filings register already uses everywhere — and says so rather than inventing a lower one from a Council proposal. Where a date comes from a notification the register has not read, `cited: false` now travels with it and the citation refuses to name an instrument: a quarterly GSTR-1's thirteenth is printed as the app's reading of the table, not as statute.

**Consequence:** the QRMP half of this app now prints one calendar rather than a calendar and a correction. Both screens read the same function, so the drift rows `expectedRows()` produces for a quarterly filer went from twelve-fold-and-disputed to *"cannot be checked until the state is named"* — and an unnamed state still prints the **earlier** of the two days, which is never later than the rule allows and a day early at worst, with the page and the register both saying so instead of one of them silently choosing. The retired objections left two disclosures worth stating, and both are printed: a QRMP year's register keeps no row for the eight deposits and eight invoice windows the calendar now carries (neither is a return), and the twenty-fifth is printed with confidence while its amount stays the shop's, because pre-computing it needs the *cash* tax figure from the quarter's own filed rows — folding the register's rows and the cash ledger into a ready challan is **named as the next round's work rather than guessed at this one's**. The suite grew with the emitter: **511 assertions in `/tmp/filings-test.mjs`** (446 → 511) now pin the sixteen monthly rows and the election in an untracked QRMP year, the rule-quoted strings, the thirteenth, the interest figure, the 35% cited to **Notification No. 85/2020-Central Tax** rather than the Circular alone, and a guard that fails if any of the six disproved citations returns (`/13\/2025|14\/2025|23\.02\.2020/`), and the fold suite, journal suite and setup suite re-ran unchanged — **511 + 393 + 250 + 172 + 131 = 1,457 across the five `/tmp` suites, 0 failing**. Ledger after the round: **65 page files / 67 route files, 54 libraries, 74 glyphs, 10 component files, 35 vault specs (30 restorable)** — no new register, key, glyph or route, so Rule 31 stayed untriggered — and the build's route listing was re-counted row by row at **69 entries** over those 67 source files (67 + `/_not-found` + `/manifest.webmanifest`), which corrects the **"68"** carried in the R3-4 and R3-5 notes; the 52 inline monospace stacks and the calendar's remaining page-local literals stayed open. `globals.css` gained one token, `--brand-line: #bfdbfe`, and `/gst/calendar` became the page that points at it instead of typing it twice — the same `--mono` move R3-5 made, with the five literals still typed by other pages left as cleanup rather than swept mid-feature. **NOT VERIFIED LIVE**: the browser session still has no signed-in Supabase user, so the new selector, the quarter rows and the deposit rows have not been driven through the UI, and this round's live drive joins R3-4's and R3-5's as owed.

### ADR-041 — A Sum on a Calendar Is Either the Shop's Own or a Labelled Ceiling, and a Fold Names the Line It Read — holds (new)

**Decision:** R3-7 closed the one gap R3-6 left open on purpose: rule 61(3) fixes the twenty-fifth and states no amount, so the eight PMT-06 rows a QRMP year carries printed a day and an empty rupee. `src/lib/filings.ts` (**2,296 → 2,368 lines**) grows `DepositFigures` and `depositFor(store, statuses, supplyMonth)`, with the notified share as data (`DEPOSIT_PCT = 35`, the sum Notification No. 85/2020-Central Tax dated 10.11.2020 prescribes under section 148 read with the proviso to section 39(7)). A deposit answers for one month, so its base is **the quarter before the quarter that month sits in** — `prevPeriod(quarterOfMonth(...))` — which makes a May 2025 deposit read `2024-Q4` and a January 2026 one read `2025-Q3`, carrying Q4's year the way R3-4's workpaper already does. The fold has two sources and three states: `ready` — 35% of the cash tax the shop stated for that quarter; `ceiling` — 35% of what that quarter's filed GSTR-3B **stated in total**; `none` — neither exists, so no rupee prints at all. `src/lib/compliance.ts` (**407 → 435**) widens `StatusMap` with an optional `cashTax` and stores the figure on the calendar's existing `taxflow:compliance:<orgId>` envelope under `cash:<quarter>` — one entry per quarter, read by both of that quarter's deposits, **no new localStorage key**, so Rule 31 stayed untriggered and the vault's 35 specs are unchanged — with `cashTaxKey()` / `enteredCashTax()` / `withEnteredCashTax()` as the three helpers and `isCashTaxKey()` separating the envelope's two kinds of row (the vault line for this register now reads *"2 returns marked filed · 1 quarter with a stated cash tax"* rather than counting a sum as a filing). `/gst/calendar` (**593 → 675**) loads the filings store **read-only** (`loadFilings(oid)`, never saved from this page), folds every `PMT-06` row it emits through `depositByEvent` keyed off the `pmt06-<YYYY-MM>` tail of the event id, prints `ready` as ₹ with the sentence that produced it, `ceiling` as **up to ₹…** in `--amber` with the stated total named beside it, `none` as rule 61(3) and the notification with no figure, and puts a single `type="number"` box on the same card that writes the shop's own cash tax on blur (with a `clear`). Codified as **Rule 44**.

**Reason:** the register does not hold the number the statute asks for. `FilingRecord.heads` (the `Heads` shape from `gstr9.ts`) is what a return **stated** — taxable, IGST, CGST, SGST, cess, tax — while the notified fixed sum is thirty-five per cent of the tax **paid in cash** in that return, i.e. the leg that left the electronic cash ledger rather than the credit ledger. Multiplying the stated total by 35% is therefore only exact when none of the quarter's tax was met from input credit, so the app prints it as a ceiling and says which line it read, and the figure that *is* the shop's own — the cash tax — is what turns it into a challan. Rule 43's contract is that a sum on this screen comes from a primary text or is labelled as the shop's; relabelling a stated total as a cash leg would satisfy neither, and inventing a credit/cash split would be a third. The second reason is a bug this round's own test caught: the first `withEnteredCashTax()` copied the map but then `delete`d `cashTax` off the **entry object it shared with the caller**, so clearing a figure mutated the previous state and a `ready` deposit read back as a `ceiling` in the next memo — the helper now builds a new entry, and the suite pins *"clearing leaves the map it was handed alone"*.

**Consequence:** a QRMP shop opening the calendar on the 20th now sees, on each deposit row, either a rupee it can pay or a rupee labelled as the most it could be, with the quarter it was read from named on the same line; and the two sentences that used to defer this work became descriptions of what the screen does — `UNTRACKED_CALENDAR_FORMS['PMT-06']` ends *"the calendar has carried the day since R3-6, and since R3-7 its card folds the amount"*, and `calendarObjections()`' deposit card now explains the stated-versus-cash distinction and tells the shop to state the quarter's cash tax. What is still outside the app is printed rather than hidden: nothing here reads the cash ledger or the portal, so the challan on the GST site stays the record, a ceiling is a bound and not a liability, and a shop that never states its cash tax keeps the amber figure forever. Ledger after the round: **65 page files / 67 route files, 54 libraries, 10 component files, 74 glyphs, 35 vault specs (30 restorable, 5 evidence-only)** — no new route, key, glyph, component or register. Verification is `tsc` 0, `eslint` at the HEAD baseline (the calendar's one pre-existing `react-hooks/exhaustive-deps` warning on its load effect), `npm run build` ✓ 67/67, and **1,493 assertions across the five `/tmp` suites, 0 failing** — filings **511 → 547** (section 18 pins both sources, all three states, the year-carrying base quarter, the nil-quarter case that states zero and still reads as a ceiling, the non-month refusals, `DEPOSIT_PCT`, the key split and the rewritten disclosure copy), journal 393, fold 250, audit 172, setup 131. Two figures in this file were re-measured at the docs pass and corrected: the per-page one-off tint counts now read `ledger` **8** (was 7) and `gst/calendar` **3** (was 4, after R3-6 moved its two `#bfdbfe` hairlines to the token). **NOT VERIFIED LIVE**: the browser session is still signed out, so the deposit card, the ceiling, the state selector and the quarter rows have not been driven through the UI — this round's drive joins R3-4's, R3-5's and R3-6's as owed.

### ADR-042 — An Import May Raise a Draft, Never a Posting: the Bridge Borrows the Door, the Numbering and the Key of the Register It Lands In — holds (new)

**Decision:** R3-8 shrank ADR-029's skip list by its two remaining document names. A **Journal voucher** in a Tally file plans through `journalItem()` (`src/lib/tally.ts`, **3,211 → 3,513 lines**, two new `PlanTarget`s `journal_draft` / `challan_draft`) and a **Delivery Challan voucher** plans through `challanItem()`. Every resolution is one-way: a journal leg lands only on a head that **already stands on the journal register** (case-folded name match; the import never creates a head, and one that has since been closed still lands with a flag), and a challan lands only against a **party and items the books already know** — an unknown name blocks with the missing word printed, and the row becomes draftable only when the shop registers that name. The refusals carry ADR-029's money rule into the new kinds: a **cash or bank leg in a journal blocks** ("money has one writer here"), a voucher whose legs **do not balance blocks rather than being squared**, a ₹0 line is dropped with a flag, and a challan with no date blocks. What the destination row cannot carry is said, not smoothed: challan lines are **untaxed** (tax that sits in a challan voucher is reported in `flags`, the lines' rupees win, and the row stays ready), an unknown movement reason defaults to `other` with a read-before-confirm flag, the place of supply is derived exactly as an invoice's, and a negative-quantity line — a *reverse* challan — blocks, because the challan register's stock semantics have no side for it. Then the write: a ticked journal row goes through the journal register's own `writeEntry` as a **draft**, and a ticked challan row through orderflow's own `appendDraft` — so the JR number is taken at *posting* and the `DC-…` number at *raising*, from that series' high-water mark in the shop's own series shape, and the period seal is asked by `writeEntry` itself, **even for the draft** (R3-5's door inherited with no new wiring). The import log counts the two kinds as two new fields (`journal_drafts`, `challan_drafts`) **on the existing `taxflow:tally:<orgId>` key**. The page (`/tally`, **1,608 → 1,735 lines**) grows the two groups under their own glyphs, a draft detail card, the two log chips, and a header paragraph saying why a draft keeps all three of the bridge's rules at once. Codified as **Rule 45**.

**Reason:** the bridge's whole contract is *a file is evidence, not instruction*, and a journal or a challan is the sharpest test of it: a file can name a head, a party or an item that does not exist, and the previous rounds' answer — block — left the two most-used Tally voucher kinds outside the app. The alternative was to make the import stronger: create the head, create the party, post the entry. Every one of those is a posting the shop did not make. So the round moved the boundary **exactly one word**: an import may raise a *draft*, never a *posting*. A draft burns no number in a statutory series, cannot reach a return, and is refused by the seal like any other write; the human who opens `/journal` or `/challans` is the one who gives it a number and a legal shape. And because both destination registers already had a writer, a numbering scheme and a seal door, the bridge borrowed all three instead of growing a parallel path — no new key (Rule 31 untriggered), no new route, glyph, component or vault spec; the two kinds ride the registers' own `seq` slots, so a shop that changed its challan series shape at R3-2 gets its own shape, not the bridge's.

**Consequence:** the sentence the library header and `7-Competitor-Comparison.md` §8 #14 have carried since R2-22 — *receipts, payments, journals and delivery challans are listed as skipped* — is now narrower and reads: **receipts and payments stay skipped** (money has one writer and an import does not make one), **a Tally order stays on the order registers until it is billed**, and an unknown kind is still skipped with its own sentence (the generic skip, the order skip and the money skip are each exactly one sentence per row — the suite pins that). Dedupe runs across import generations the same way the note rows' does: a draft whose `Tally Journal #J-…` / `Tally Delivery Challan #DC-…` line already sits in a stored row's notes reads **already**. The sample dump gained the two vouchers it lacked — `J-51` (a rent journal that **blocks** on an untouched chart, which is the round's rule demoed on the demo file) and `DC-77` (a 5 × ₹1,200 challan that drafts **ready** at ₹6,000 into May 2025) — and the plan now reads **ready 9 / blocked 2 / skipped 2 / needs-your-eye 2** over 13 rows, spanning `2025-04-01 → 2025-05-06` because a draft's own date counts into the range like any document's. Verified: `tsc` 0; `eslint` 0 on the round's three changed files; `npm run build` ✓ with the route table unchanged at **69 build entries over 67 source files** (67 + `/_not-found` + `/manifest.webmanifest`), and **99 assertions in `/tmp/tally3-test.mjs`, 0 failing** across six sections — journal planning, challan planning, what stays skipped, the sample dump, the journal write path (draft burns no number, posting takes `JR-2025-26-…` equal to the peek, a sealed April-2025 draft is refused with `sealed: true`, exactly one posted entry survives), and the challan write path (`DC-2025-26-0001` → `0002` through `appendDraft`, equal to peek, re-plan reads `already`). **The `/tmp` harness was rebuilt this round after the 2026-10-14 wipe**: `/tmp/jr-register.mjs` is a registrar that `module.register()`s `/tmp/jr-hooks.mjs`, an ESM resolve hook mapping `@/…` into `src/…`; the **five** suites that stood at R3-7 (filings 547, journal 393, fold 250, audit 172, setup 131) went unrecoverable with the wipe and were not re-run — their counts stay as recorded, and this round's figure is stated on its own. **NOT VERIFIED LIVE**: the browser session is still signed out, so dropping a file with a journal and a challan, ticking both, and seeing the draft land on `/journal` and `/challans` has not been driven through the UI — this round's drive joins R3-4's through R3-7's as owed.

### ADR-043 — A Backup's Promise Is Measured on the Device: Which Books Moved Is Answered by Checksum, Never by Timestamp, and Never from the File — holds (new)

**Decision:** R3-9 answered the question ADR-033's file could not. A backup has always printed *when* it was taken and *how many* rows travelled; the shop standing in front of `/vault` asks something narrower — **is my newest file still the newest copy, and which books have moved out from under it?** `recordBackup(orgId, vault)` (`src/lib/vault.ts`, **1,433 → 1,597 lines**) now writes a **fingerprint per register** — `{rows, checksum}` — onto the backup event in the vault's own log (`taxflow:vault:<orgId>`, the key that is deliberately never inside a vault file), taken from the very `VaultFile` it was handed, so the memory is of the bytes that left. `registerFreshness(orgId, now)` re-reads each spec's key on the device, computes the same FNV-1a `checksumOf()` the packer used, and compares **checksum against checksum** — never `updatedAt`, never the file — returning `FreshRow`s in **seven states** (`fresh` / `changed` / `new-since` / `cleared` / `nothing` / `unrecorded` / `never`) with `FRESHNESS_META` giving each its label, tone and *why* sentence, and `atRiskRows()` naming the three states a wipe would actually take (`changed`, `new-since`, `never` — with `rows > 0`). The page (`/vault`, **1,071 → 1,226 lines**) prints the answer where the question is already loaded: a *Since your file* column on the Registers tab, a "What moved since your last backup" card with counted chips and a "What a wipe would take with it" list that links each book to the page that owns it, and the *Last backup taken* stat naming how many books are newer than any file. Codified as **Rule 46**.

**Reason:** the alternative was to read the file, and the file is not here — it is in a folder, a mail, a USB stick, and this screen cannot know what happened to it after it left. Even a timestamp comparison would have been a lie twice over: a register's `updatedAt` moves when nothing about its rupees changed, and two books edited on different days would answer the same question with the same "later than your file". A checksum answers *content*, which is what a backup is for. The fingerprints stay **device-side on purpose**: a second laptop that restores the file inherits the *books*, not this laptop's claim about them, and asks its own question from its own log. And because the log is a ring of thirty events that a person can open in devtools, `loadVaultLog()` sanitises `fingerprints` on read — anything that is not a clean `{rows, checksum}` per id is dropped whole — so a forged entry can only ever *compare*; it cannot make the page say "In your file" for a book the log never hashed.

**Consequence:** the vault is still **35 specs / 30 restorable / 5 evidence-only** — no new register, no new key, no route, glyph or component, **Rule 31 untriggered** — and the two glyphs the card needed (`compare`, `clock`) already stood in the registry, which stays at **74**. A backup taken before this round (or by a build that never wrote fingerprints) reads **`unrecorded`** on every row and the card says so rather than printing an empty table; a book the device cleared but the file carries reads **`cleared`** — *Only in your file* — and is deliberately **kept out of the at-risk list**, because there the file is the surviving copy and a wipe costs nothing. The fold is read-only and re-entrant: taking a new backup re-runs it in the same click, so the answer updates against the file that was just packed. Verification: **120 assertions in `/tmp/vault3-test.mjs`, 0 failing** (no-backup, first backup, unchanged, edited, cleared, a book the file never carried, evidence-only, the `unrecorded` degradation, the sanitiser, restore-then-re-backup, cross-org isolation, and the page's own wiring), `/tmp/tally3-test.mjs` re-run **99 / 0**, `tsc` 0, `eslint` 0 problems on the round's two changed files, `npm run build` ✓ with `/vault` in the route table (**69 rows**, re-counted row by row from this round's log — which also settles the 68/69 drift between the R3-5 and R3-6 notes: the root `page.tsx` does earn its `○ /` row). Width behaviour checked by **static CSS audit** only — the browser session is signed out and `/vault` redirects to `/login`, so the live drive joins R3-4's through R3-8's as owed. Rule **46** / ADR-**043** is where a backup stops being a date and becomes a claim the device can check.

---

## GST Compliance Decisions

### GCD-001 — CGST+SGST vs IGST Rule
Same state code (supplier vs place of supply) → CGST + SGST. Different state → IGST. Export and SEZ always IGST. Source: CGST Act, verified at implementation.

### GCD-002 — GST Rates in Database
`gst_rate` per product, never hard-coded. The list lives in `src/lib/gst-rates.ts` — 0 · 0.25 · 3 · 5 · 12 · 18 · 28 · 40 after the 56th Council removal of the 12/28 slabs and the 40% demerit slab (effective 22 Sep 2025), with 12 and 28 kept so a credit note against a pre-reform invoice can repeat its original rate; every picker imports it rather than restating it. Changing a rate must not alter historical invoices (ADR-006).

### GCD-003 — GSTIN Validation
`/^[0-9]{2}[A-Z]{5}[0-9]{4}[A-Z]{1}[1-9A-Z]{1}Z[0-9A-Z]{1}$/` — format only; no GSTN lookup.

### GCD-004 — HSN vs SAC
Single `hsn_sac` field. Reporting granularity depends on turnover — the current threshold must be verified at cbic.gov.in.

### GCD-005 — ITC Eligibility
User-controlled `itc_eligible` flag per bill/expense; Section 16 conditions are the user's call. The app advises CA verification.

### GCD-006 — Reconciliation Compares, It Does not Decide (new)
`/gst/reconciliation` classifies each 2B credit (matched, mismatched, missing in books, only in books, amount/date/rate differs). It cannot know what was actually filed — the portal statement is the user's input and the books are ours; the user picks the action.

### GCD-007 — Declared Gaps Beat Silent Ones (new)
Rule 42/43 reversals, ITCA-01 demands, Table 8 importer credit and Table 9 carry-forward are not computable from documents. Pages state this plainly instead of guessing zero (Rule 19).

### GCD-008 — GSTR-9 / 9C Is a Workpaper (new)
`/gst/gstr9` builds outward and ITC tables from documents plus **declared** 3B figures, watches the 180-day credit limit, and reconciles books vs 3B. It is a preparation tool for a CA, not a return.

### GCD-009 — Applicability Is Declared, Not Inferred (new)
E-invoice threshold, e-way bill requirement, filing profile (monthly/QRMP) and TDS obligations come from user settings, because turnover and registration state cannot be derived from documents the app has never seen.

### GCD-010 — Withholding: the section is the user's call (new)
`/tds` reads a purchase bill's amount and party but cannot know whether that spend is a contract, a commission or rent — goods-vs-service and the 194 section are chosen by the owner (per vendor, overridable per bill), and an untagged vendor stays visibly untagged. Rates and thresholds are printed for the CA to verify rather than made editable; a dateless challan is dropped rather than half-saved.
