### Session 42 — 2026-10-09 (R2-21 B1 — cost centres: a document split by share, never by rupee)
**Built:** `src/lib/costcentres.ts` (`3a29c8f`) — the other half of the §4 matrix row R2-20 came from, and the buildable half of competitor gap **#10** ("Manufacturing / BOM, cost centres"): a GST invoice has twenty-nine statutory columns and none of them is *which part of my business earned this*, so the register lives in `taxflow:ccentres:<orgId>` (ADR-013 — there is no cost-centre column anywhere in the schema and ADR-013 keeps it that way) and holds **tags, not money**. The four decisions are recorded as **ADR-028**. An allocation stores a **share (percentage) against a document's own id, never a rupee amount**: `bookDocs()` re-reads `invoices`, `credit_notes`, `debit_notes`, `purchase_bills` and `expenses` (cancelled rows skipped, credit notes kept negative per ADR-021, `itc_eligible === false` yielding `itc = 0`) and `sheets()` multiplies each share against the document's *current* taxable / tax / total / ITC — amend a bill tomorrow and every centre's contribution moves with it, because nothing here was ever entered. A document is **split, not moved**: `validateAllocation` refuses Σ > 100 (`PCT_EPSILON = 0.005`, so 100.0001% is a mistake rather than a rounding artefact), parts come from the formula, and the unallocated remainder comes from **subtraction**, so `Σ parts + remainder = the document` to the paisa and the reconciliation cannot lose a coin. **A cost centre is not a legal person**: `centreViews()` derives revenue, tax collected, cost, ITC, contribution, the share of the same whole, the largest single document, and a `problems[]` list the page prints verbatim — and its only statutory finding is a *flag with a link*, never a tax figure. `kind: 'branch'` is the one kind with `statutory: true`, and the flag's wording reads `settings.home_state`: a branch in **another** state with no GSTIN of its own names both states and says registration may be required (rule 41A), a branch in the **home** state raises nothing at all — intra-state movement is not a rule 41A question — and with no registration on file the page says "**if** stock or inputs move", because it will not accuse without evidence. All three wordings keep the strings `rule 41A` and `/gst/gstr9`, so the flag always points at the page that computes reversals. A tag whose document was deleted survives as an **orphan** sheet — it keeps the number and date it was made against, says so, and contributes no rupees. Write paths refuse what actually goes wrong: a centre needs a name (it is the label every split is read against), a 15-character GSTIN or none, a state code from `INDIAN_STATES`, no name or code clashing with a live centre; new shares cannot be tagged to an archived centre; `deleteCentre` refuses while any document still carries a share to it (the serial-register precedent — archive is the honest exit); an empty part list is a legitimate save that *unassigns* rather than an error.

**Wired:** `/cost-centres` on the kit (0 hex — only the established `rgba(15,23,42,.45)` scrim and `rgba(0,0,0,0.05)` notice-close, both shared with the pages before it). Four stat cards — rupees **placed with a centre**, **nobody's rupees** with the unassigned count, the strongest centre by contribution, and things to check (centre findings + sheet findings + orphans) — over four tabs: **Centres** (cards with revenue and spending `ProgressBar`s, stat rows for revenue / tax collected / spending / ITC / contribution, the largest document named, the findings block carrying a `GSTR-9 workpaper →` link, a shares drill-down, archive/reopen, and a delete that refuses a centre still carrying shares), **Documents** (one row per book document: date, party, taxable, a placed bar, split chips each printing *its own* rupees, the unallocated remainder in `is-hot`, and Split / Forget — or Forget alone for an orphan), **To assign** (a bulk bar that tags every selected document at one share with select-all, the total sitting unassigned, and a per-row Split), **To check** (the three piles separated). Period (`this FY / last FY / all`) and side (`outward / inward`) filter the *sheets*, never the loaded documents, so a tag outside the window can never read as an orphan. The **split modal's "what gets written" preview is `sheets()` re-run on the draft** — the same call the register renders — so the preview cannot disagree with the page, and it prints the `docKey`, "Stored as percentages only", and the Σ identity in words. Three CSV exports (register 14 columns, documents 11, one centre's shares 10). Nav "Cost Centres" under Reports & AI, ⌘K "New cost centre" → `/cost-centres?new=1`, new `layers` and `tag` glyphs, and a cross-link from `/accounting` — the statements answer how much the business made, the centres answer which part made it. The two boundary strips ("A cost centre is not a legal person" / "This page never writes to the books") are printed in their own words, and the loader stamps `settings.home_state` from `gst_registrations` where `is_primary`, so the offline register reads the rule-41A flags the same way the live page does.

**Traps recorded:**
- **A stored setting that nothing reads is dead weight.** `settings.home_state` existed in the engine from the first draft and was read by nothing; it only became real once the flag needed a home state to compare against. A field is not a feature — check what consumes it before shipping one.
- **`saveCC()` returns `void`.** `setStore(saveCC(oid, stamped))` is a TS2345; build the new store, write it, then `setStore` the value you already have (the `/fx` and `/godowns` write paths return the store, this one did not).
- **Index a preview by the row's own share, not by the engine's array position.** Labelling part *i* from `preview.sheet.parts[i]` misaligns the moment an unselected middle row is filtered out by `toAllocation` — the rupee label belongs to the draft row it sits on.
- **Curly apostrophes, not straight, inside JSX text.** `react/no-unescaped-entities` fails the build path on `document's` / `centre's` in prose; every other page in the kit already writes `’`.
- **Drafting a page against a library you have not re-read invents API.** The first write carried a fabricated import and a hand-rolled trio of allocation-write helpers duplicating `setAllocation`; both went once the export surface was read back.

**Method:** 245 assertions in `/tmp/costcentres-test.mjs` (book reading incl. cancelled/signed/ITC-ineligible cases, share maths to the paisa, the Σ ≤ 100 refusal and the remainder-by-subtraction identity, orphans, per-centre derivation and rollups, sorting with archived centres always last, the three home-state rule-41A wordings and the "never invents a tax figure" guard, GSTIN/state/name/code validation, archive and delete guards, the store round-trip with a percentage kept as a percentage, and all three CSV shapes); the other eleven suites re-ran green (1,090 assertions — jobwork 210 · recurring 183 · fx 153 · orderflow 111 · godowns 97 · serials 84 · portal 82 · batches 78 · commands 49 · registers 21 · gst-rates 22); `tsc` 0; `eslint` 0 on every touched file; `npm run build` ✓ 56 routes (54 pages + 2 api) with `○ /cost-centres`. **The interactive UI is still unverified** — the Supabase dev session is expired, `/cost-centres` redirects to `/login`, and the password is only ever typed by the user (task #84).

**Ledger:** **45 pages on the kit, 4 partial, 0 pre-kit** (+3 auth, + root redirect, + public `portal`). Counts move to **56 routes / 40 libraries**. Gap #10 is half-closed (cost centres in, BOM/manufacturing still out — a different customer segment); the rest of the ranked list is unchanged. Browser pass still blocked by the expired dev session.

### Session 43 — 2026-10-09 (R2-22 — Tally bridge: a file read into a plan, the books written back)
**Built:** `src/lib/tally.ts` (`f67f56f`, 2,843 lines) — the `·` row of `7-Competitor-Comparison.md` §6 ("Tally data import/export": Vyapar, myBillBook, Marg and ClearTax all advertise it), taken because every *ranked* gap left in §8 is blocked or schema-bound (#1 a GSP contract, #4's authenticated half and #5 the Phase 14 security window, #6 a different customer segment). **Nothing about the schema changes**: the XML is read in the browser, the rows it yields go through the real write paths, and the only thing stored locally is the *log* of an import (`taxflow:tally:<orgId>`, ADR-013). The four decisions are recorded as **ADR-029**. **A file is evidence, not instruction**: every rupee on the plan comes from a tagged value or from *labelled* arithmetic on tagged values (`derived` — "₹tax ÷ ₹taxable"), and where the file disagrees with itself (ledger lines that do not sum to the printed total, a voucher that does not balance) the disagreement lands in `flags` and the file's own lines win, because the lines are what the shop billed. **Our numbers stay ours**: `invoice_number` comes from `invoice_sequences`, the Tally voucher travels in `notes` (`Tally Sales #V-101 · ref PO-118`, parsed back by `tallyReference()`), and a purchase bill's `bill_number` remains the vendor's own reference — that is what GSTR-2B matches, so co-opting it would corrupt a reconciliation nobody can undo. **Unresolvable input blocks rather than guesses**: no party invented, no product created implicitly by a line, no tax rate spread across lines the file cannot attribute it to, and `reasons` says which name is missing. **Import touches no money and reaches no return**: an entry on a cash/bank/round-off ledger is typed `money`, reported as ₹X already collected, and the document lands unpaid for `/payments` — the one writer. Four layers: a tolerant **scanner** (`parseXml` — not a validating parser; Tally's writer, integration dumps and hand-edited requests differ in case, in attribute-vs-child-tag, and in whether the file ends where the last tag closes; Node has no `DOMParser` and the browser's refuses half-closed input), a **read layer** (`readTally` → `TallyLedger` / `TallyStockItem` / `TallyVoucher`, entries typed `party`/`tax`/`money`/`value` from the ledger name, its master group and `IsPartyLedger`, the debit side from `IsDeemPositive` or the older `BalDrCrType`, rates read as percent *or* absolute with a `CGST @9` line recognised as the 18% slab via `fullRateFor`, Tally state names ↔ GST state codes, anything not a sales/purchase voucher kept as a `SkippedObject` with its reason), a **plan layer** (`buildPlan(dump, existing, org)` → `PlanItem` with target/status/fields/reasons/derived/flags/requires/matchedId/tallyRef/rupees; `expandSelection` pulls in the masters a ticked document needs, `writeOrder` keeps masters before documents, `planCsvRows` exports ten columns), and an **export layer** (`tallyLedgerXml` / `tallyStockItemXml` / `tallyVoucherXml` inside `tallyEnvelope`, `TallyAction` only `Create`/`Alter`, tax-ledger names generated from the rates the documents carry so a voucher never names a ledger the company lacks, `exportSummary` counting the rupees before the file exists).

**Wired:** `/tally` on the kit (0 hex) with three views — **Plan** (drop a file or paste it; the paste box and the plan are deliberately separate states, so typing is not an instruction to re-plan and editing after planning cannot quietly rebuild the rows under a tick; a "what the reader could not make of the file" card; four stat cards *objects read / ready to write / blocked / needs your eye*; a `Segmented` status filter plus search; expandable rows printing rupees, reasons, derivations and flags; the ticked count on the Import button; and an outcome list where every written row links to the page that now holds it), **Export** (invoices and bills from the books, FY and search filters, line items fetched lazily in 150-id slices — a document with no lines is still marked read, or the desk would wait forever — company name + `Create`/`Alter` + "Write the masters too", then `Vouchers / Ledgers / Stock items / Rupees in the file` counted before `tally-export.xml` downloads), and **Log** (which file, when, and how many of each kind). `runImport` propagates a broken dependency: a document whose master failed to create is reported "Left unwritten — <what> could not be created" rather than half-written, and after a write the register is re-read so imported rows flip to **Already here** instead of being importable twice. Nav "Tally Bridge" under Account, ⌘K actions *Read a Tally export* / *Send my books to Tally* (`?view=export`) / *Try the Tally bridge on a sample* (`?sample=1`), a new `tally` glyph (two ledgers facing each other, arrows between), and cross-links from `/accounting` and the `/settings` Company tab.

**Traps recorded:**
- **React Compiler lint errors, not warnings.** `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` fails a synchronous `setState` in an effect *or* in the synchronous prefix of a function the effect calls; the repo's clean pattern (`/fx`, `/bank`) is an inline `async load()` whose **first statement is an `await`**, plus a `useRef` guard so the desk cannot load twice. `preserve-manual-memoization`/`immutability` fire when a `useCallback` is declared *after* the effect that calls it. And a local helper named `useText` is parsed as a hook — name it `loadText`.
- **A `Card` is not a div.** It accepts only `children/className/pad/hover/style`; drag handlers need a wrapper element, and the CSS target moves with them (`.tb-drag .tf-card`).
- **A Supabase builder is a thenable, not a Promise.** `fetchIn(ids, slice => supabase.from(…).then(r => r.data))` fails to satisfy a `Promise<T[]>` parameter; use `async slice => { const { data } = await …; return (data || []) as unknown as Row[] }` and build rows explicitly rather than spreading.
- **Derived UI state that reads the input directly hides the input.** Planning straight off the textarea meant one keystroke planned the file and replaced the load card — separate *what is in the box* from *what has been planned*.
- **`IconName` is not a checked type.** `P: Record<string, ReactNode>` collapses `keyof typeof P` to `string`, and `Icon` silently falls back to `dashboard` — audit icon names by script, not by eye (`trash` does not exist).
- **Lint baseline before claiming zero.** `/settings` carries three pre-existing `no-explicit-any` errors; adding an import line shifts them, which reads like new breakage. Diff the line numbers before chasing.

**Method:** 607 assertions in `/tmp/tally-test.mjs` (the scanner on real and broken shapes, attribute-vs-child values, case folding, entity decoding, percent-vs-absolute rates, Dr/Cr from either field, state-name mapping both ways, voucher classification and skip reasons, derivation and flag arithmetic, `buildPlan` across all six targets and four statuses, matching an existing register by name then GSTIN / HSN / document number, `expandSelection` and `writeOrder` ordering, every exported XML string for escaping and envelope shape, `exportSummary` rupee arithmetic, the log round-trip, both CSV shapes, and the sample dump end to end — ready 7 / blocked 1 / skipped 2 / already 0 / attention 1, invoices ₹28,320, bill ₹26,550, order `product, product, customer, vendor, invoice, invoice, purchase_bill`); the other twelve suites re-ran green — **1,942 assertions, 0 failing**; `tsc` 0; `eslint` 0 new problems on all seven touched files; `npm run build` ✓ 57 routes (55 pages + 2 api) with `○ /tally`. **The interactive UI is still unverified** — `/tally?sample=1` redirects to `/login` on the expired Supabase dev session and the password is only ever typed by the user (task #84).

**Ledger:** **46 pages on the kit, 4 partial, 0 pre-kit** (+3 auth, + root redirect, + public `portal`). Counts move to **57 routes / 41 libraries**. §6's Tally row becomes ✔ and §8 gains **#14**; the ranked list itself is untouched, because a Tally bridge was never in it. Browser pass still blocked by the expired dev session.

### Session 44 — 2026-10-09 (R2-23 — projects & time: hours measured, rupees re-read from the books)
**Built:** `src/lib/projects.ts` (1,144 lines, `dc41578`) and `/projects` (1,365 lines) over `taxflow:projects:<orgId>` — the `·` row of `7-Competitor-Comparison.md` §4 ("Project / time tracking"), which not one of the nine vendors fetched states it has, taken again because every *ranked* gap left is blocked or schema-bound (#1 a GSP contract, #4's authenticated half and #5 the Phase 14 security window, #6 payroll is a different customer segment, #12 attachments need Supabase Storage). **Nothing about the schema changes and no rupee is stored**: the register keeps measurements — hour rows, and whole documents attached by id — and every figure is re-read on load through `bookDocs()`, the reader `costcentres.ts` already had, imported rather than duplicated so cancelled rows and signed credit notes behave identically in both features. Recorded as **ADR-030**. The four decisions: **labour is priced, not costed** (an hour carries the rate you would *bill* it at; contribution is billed revenue less attached spend and the hour value sits beside it, never inside it, because charging yourself your own billing rate is double counting); **"billed" is a reference stamp** (`markBilled(ref)` records paper raised elsewhere — `createInvoice` remains the only allocator of `INV-<FY>-NNNN` and the only thing that moves stock, `/payments` the only writer of money); **a tag outlives its document as an orphan** that says so and contributes nothing, and an invoice number the books do not have is accepted but listed in `unmatched_refs`; **the window is a cited constant, not a verdict** — `SERVICE_INVOICE_DAYS = 30` from CBIC's *Handbook on Invoicing under GST* (17-06-2025 §2.1.2, rule 47 read with section 31(2)), printed on the page as *cited not settled* because notifications widen it for classes of supplier. The engine: `unbilledGroups()` folds unbilled hours into one row per **project-month**; `invoiceWindow()` reads it off the billing mode — `time_material` and `fixed_price` are windowed, `internal` has no recipient so no supply and **no window to miss**, and an unknown mode falls to the **loud** side because silencing a statutory clock on a parse failure is how a deadline gets missed; `WATCH_META` sorts each row `no_window` / `open` / `due_soon` (≤ 7 days) / `overdue` with `days_over` counted as well as reported; `projectView()` derives hours, `labour_value`, `unbilled_value`, `unpriced_hours`, revenue, cost, ITC, contribution, `days_since_last`, budget use and the findings; `validateProject` / `validateEntry` / `deleteProject` guard the register; `monthlySeries` / `personSeries` bucket the same rows; `invoiceLineText()` writes the sentence to paste into the invoice you are raising; three CSVs export register, timesheet and unbilled work.

**Wired:** `/projects` on the kit with four tabs — **Projects** (cards with billed/spend bars over hours, unbilled and contribution rows, findings that link out, and an `attention` strip whose every flagged name is a button that opens that card), **Timesheet** (day-grouped rows, per-month and per-person strips, bulk select), **To bill** (the project-month register: window badge, oldest supply, due date, days left or days past, and `watchSentence()` printed in the state's own tone), **Documents** (whole invoices and bills attached by id, bulk tagging, the orphan block) — four stat cards (*hours on the register / work not yet billed / past the 30-day window / contribution from the books*), `?new=1`, `?log=1` and `?view=tobill|timesheet|documents` deep links, and four boundary strips that name the limits in their own words. Nav "Projects & Time" under Reports & AI, ⌘K *New project* / *Log hours* / *Unbilled work (rule 47)*, the 66th glyph `briefcase`, and cross-links both ways with `/cost-centres` plus a footer naming the browser key the register lives in. The naming collision is resolved on screen: **a centre splits one document by percentage, a project collects whole documents and adds the hours a centre cannot express** — necessary because `costcentres.KIND_META.project` is literally labelled "Project / job".

**Traps recorded:**
- **A CSS variable that does not exist fails silently.** `var(--green-ink)` is not a build error and not a console error: the declaration is invalid at computed-value time, so the note simply renders in the inherited ink and *looks* like a design choice. `globals.css` has `--green` / `--green-soft`, never `--*-ink`. The check is mechanical — extract every `var(--x)` in a page and diff against the `:root` block plus the page's own definitions — and it now runs on every new register page. Same script found the twelve hex literals the first draft carried.
- **`require()` inside a `useMemo` in a client component** is ESM-invalid; the import goes at the top. And `BookDoc` is exported by `costcentres.ts`, not by `projects.ts` — importing the type from the wrong module reports **TS2459 (declared locally, not exported)**, which reads like a typo but is a layering answer: the book reader has one owner.
- **A narrow union is not a string.** `w.tone === 'blue'` against `WATCH_META`'s `grey | green | amber | red` is TS2367 with no overlap — fix the expectation, do not cast it away.
- **An assigned-but-unused variable is a skipped feature, not lint noise.** `attention` was computed and never rendered; the fix was the strip that jumps to each flagged project (with the sentence that none of its reasons is a tax figure), not a `delete`.
- **A grep for icon names matches comparison strings too.** The audit script flagged `red` / `amber` as missing glyphs — they were literals inside `name={…}` ternaries — and its key regex also counts a `name:` field in a type, so the registry reports 67 for 66 real glyphs. Count glyphs from the registry object, then read the page's actual `icon="…"` values.

**Method:** 228 assertions in `/tmp/projects-test.mjs` (store round-trip with the version stamp, upsert/status/delete guards incl. "archive it instead", both validators incl. the 24-hour, future-day, half-archived and duplicate-code refusals, `invoiceWindow` across the three modes and an unknown one, due-date arithmetic over month-end and FY-end, the four watch states with their day counts, grouping by project-month, priced vs unpriced hours, revenue/cost/ITC/contribution against a hand-check, cancelled documents skipped, orphans, `unmatched_refs`, findings triage and the sort orders, both series, `invoiceLineText`, and all three CSV shapes); the other thirteen suites re-ran green — **2,170 assertions across 14 suites, 0 failing**; `tsc` 0; `eslint` 0 problems on all six touched files; `npm run build` ✓ 58 routes (56 pages + 2 api) with `○ /projects`. **The interactive UI is still unverified** — `/projects` redirects to `/login` on the expired Supabase dev session and the password is only ever typed by the user (task #84).

**Ledger:** **47 pages on the kit, 4 partial, 0 pre-kit** (+3 auth, + root redirect, + public `portal`). Counts move to **58 routes / 42 libraries / 66 glyphs**. §4's "Project / time tracking" row becomes ✔ and §8 gains **#15**; the ranked list itself is untouched, because a time register was never in it. ### Session 45 — 2026-10-09 (R2-24 — manufacturing: what a thing is made of, and what making it did to the stock)
**Built:** `src/lib/bom.ts` (1,294 lines, `2c9eed7`) and `src/app/(app)/inventory/recipes/page.tsx` (1,921 lines, `fd9126d`) over `taxflow:bom:<orgId>` — the **BOM half of competitor gap #10** ("Manufacturing / BOM"), the half `/cost-centres` (R2-21, ADR-028) deliberately did not take, and the reason it is buildable now is the same one as every round since R2-14: **no schema change** (ADR-013). Recorded as **ADR-031**, whose four rules are the design: **a recipe stores quantities, never a rupee** — `rollUpCost()` reads each component's `products.purchase_price` on every load, the field `/accounting` values stock at, so a corrected master price re-values every recipe and nothing here can hold a stale amount, and a component with no cost yields **no rupee figure, not a ₹0 one** (the rule an unpriced hour already follows at `/projects`); **a build stores what was drawn, not what the recipe hoped** — `Build.lines` is kept because the drum said 1.3 kg, and `planBuild()` prints the difference as a variance rather than smoothing it, since that difference is wastage and wastage is what recipes lie about; **stock moves only through `record_stock_movement`** on the `adjustment_out` / `adjustment_in` types that already exist, with the job reference in `notes`, because a `build` movement type would be a schema change *and* a type no other reader of `stock_movements` has ever seen; and **a build is not a supply** — nothing here reaches a return, the tax is at `/invoices/new` on the finished good's own HSN/SAC, and materials sent out to somebody else belong on `/gst/job-work` with the section 143 clock. The engine: `perUnitNeed()` / `requirement()` scale a recipe to a run at four decimals (a recipe speaks in fractions of a unit; money rounds at two); `planBuild()` walks the explosion and returns per-row need / on-hand / short with `can_run` and the `limited_by` answer; `findCycle()` refuses a sub-assembly that loops into itself **before** `MAX_EXPLODE_DEPTH = 12` is exhausted; `validateRecipe` refuses an unnamed recipe or output, a product made out of itself, a doubled component line, waste above `MAX_WASTE_PCT`, a quantity above `MAX_QTY_PER_COMPONENT` (grams typed where kg was meant), more than `MAX_COMPONENTS` parts, and a run above `MAX_UNITS_PER_BUILD`; `allocateRef` / `peekRef` keep `JOB-<FY>-NNNN` per-FY, high-water, never reused — this register allocates its own numbers and never borrows an invoice sequence; `whereUsed` answers the reverse question, `consumedOf` measures what the log has drawn against a component, `demandAgainstRecipes` reads active sales orders as demand and reports what is *still* to make (over-building is not a negative plan); `movementsFor` / `reverseMovementsFor` describe exactly what a run wrote and what cancelling it must write back; `recipeViews` / `buildViews` / `sortRecipeViews` / `summarise` derive everything the tabs print, and three of the four CSV exports come out of the library so the screen and the file cannot disagree.

**Wired:** `/inventory/recipes` on the kit with four tabs — **Recipe book** (cards: cost per unit and margin rolled up from the master, *Can make now* with the component that limits it named under it, runs and variance on the log, a `ProgressBar` of this shelf against the biggest buildable run, findings that link out, and an expandable parts table showing per-unit / drawn / on-shelf side by side), **Build log** (planned → built → cancelled, expandable variance and line detail, an over-draw refused on the screen, a cancel that writes the reversal *first* and then marks the run un-moved so both rows stay on the log, and no delete for a run that moved stock — the reversal is the record), **Materials board** (open sales orders demanded against recipes, per-recipe plans that indent the explosion by level, and shortages merged across every live recipe into one shopping list that links to `/inventory/reorder`), **Where used** (a component rail plus a panel: which recipes draw it, what the log has consumed against it in the window, whether the shelf could still cover them) — four stat cards (*recipes on the register / what the shelf can run, as N / M / units built this FY / runs on the log*), four CSV exports, `?view` `?open` `?component` `?new=1` `?build=1` deep links handled inside the async load (the godowns idiom — a prerendered page cannot read `useSearchParams` from the top level), and boundary strips that name the limits in their own words: a rupee is read here and never typed, a build moves quantity not value, and materials going out of the business are job work. Nav "Recipes & Builds" under Masters, ⌘K *New recipe* → `/inventory/recipes?new=1` and *New build* → `/inventory/recipes?build=1`, the **67th glyph** `recipe` (a measure jar with a fill line and a graduation rather than a coin, because what a recipe stores is quantities), a footer cross-link from `/inventory/reorder`, and links out to `/inventory`, `/products` and `/gst/job-work`. The movement write is all-or-nothing *at the screen*: `writeAll()` reverses whatever already applied and leaves the run a plan rather than half a fact.

**Traps recorded:**
- **A validator is not a stock gate.** `validateBuild()` checks the *record* — active recipe, real date, plausible run, lines that exist in the catalogue — and never reads `current_stock`; `planBuild()` knows each row's `short` and still hands back a plan, because a plan screen is allowed to show a shortfall. So the page built its own over-draw list from the index and blocks "Record build & move stock" itself. Read the function body before trusting a name.
- **`cancelBuild()` flips `status` only** and leaves `moved: true` on the row. Calling it and stopping there would tell the next reader that a cancelled run still stands in the stock ledger. The page writes `reverseMovementsFor()` first, then upserts `status: 'cancelled', moved: false` in one write, and hides *delete* for a run that moved stock — the reversal is the record.
- **A number that ignores a filter must not be labelled by it.** `summarise()` always counts the *current* FY while the build tab filters by `this_fy | prev_fy | all`, so the card read "Built in FY 2025-26" over a 2026-27 figure. The label now derives from the same clock the number does.
- **Matching by name where an id exists** breaks the moment two products are called the same thing. The merged shortage board linked *Where used* by product **name**; it carries `product_id` now and keys by it.
- **A modal opened without its record builds the wrong product.** A card's *Build* called `openBuildEditor()` with no argument and fell through to the first active recipe, so every card built the same thing; the signature takes the build and an optional recipe override, and `?build=1` now prefills against the first live recipe with `requirement()` applied rather than opening a blank draft.
- **`info` is not a glyph** (again). The registry has `shield`, and a wrong name renders the dashboard fallback silently because `P` is typed `Record<string, ReactNode>` — the icon audit script is the only thing that catches this class.
- **Unused imports are a skipped feature or dead weight, not noise.** `BUILD_STATUSES`, `costPerUnit` and `FindingTone` arrived unused from the kit; two went, and `MAX_UNITS_PER_BUILD` was *kept by wiring it* as the `max` on both units inputs — which is what the library's ceiling is for.

**Method:** 409 assertions in `/tmp/bom-test.mjs` (store round-trip with the version stamp and a foreign version read as empty, every ceiling and the cycle refusal, `requirement` scaling and four-decimal rounding, the roll-up against a hand-check incl. the no-cost-yields-no-rupee rule, `planBuild` shortage maths and `limited_by`, the untracked component that is *unknown* rather than short, the JOB sequence across an FY boundary with no number reused, where-used and demand, movement/reversal symmetry, status guards, sort orders and the three library exports (the fourth CSV, *where used*, is assembled on the page and so is audited there, not in the suite); the other fourteen suites re-ran green — **2,579 assertions across 15 suites, 0 failing**; `tsc` 0; `eslint` 0 problems on the new page and the four files touched (`/inventory/reorder` carries four pre-existing `no-explicit-any` errors that predate this round and were left where they were — verified against `HEAD` before judging them); the token audits that R2-23 was written off now run first time: **0 hex**, the two shared `rgba` recipes only, and 21 `var()`s every one resolving against `:root`; `node /tmp/icon-audit.mjs` → 0 missing; `npm run build` ✓ 59 routes (57 pages + 2 api) with `○ /inventory/recipes` prerendered static. **The interactive UI is still unverified** — `/inventory/recipes` redirects to `/login` on the expired Supabase dev session and the password is only ever typed by the user (task #84).

**Ledger:** **48 pages on the kit, 4 partial, 0 pre-kit** (+3 auth, + root redirect, + public `portal`) — the arithmetic is 48 + 4 = **52 pages inside the shell**, 57 in `src/app`. Counts move to **59 routes / 43 libraries / 67 glyphs / 2,579 assertions across 15 suites**. `7-Competitor-Comparison.md` §8 gap **#10** closes on its manufacturing half (recipes, builds, materials planning, where-used); what is left of it is costing depth that waits on payroll and a cost-layer engine. `2-System-Architecture.md` §4's folder tree was re-counted from the code for the first time since R2-11 — it had never been told about the order pages, the four inventory registers, `/recurring`, `/fx`, `/cost-centres`, `/tally`, `/projects`, `/portal`, or the twelve libraries written since. Browser pass still blocked by the expired dev session.

**Commit:** `2c9eed7` (engine), `fd9126d` (page + wiring), docs in this commit.

### Session 46 — 2026-10-09 (R2-25 — exports: the undertaking clock and the FEMA clock on one consignment)
**Built:** `src/lib/exports.ts` (1,856 lines, `3e6582d`) and `src/app/(app)/gst/exports/page.tsx` (1,574 lines, `580086d`) over `taxflow:exports:<orgId>`, recorded as **ADR-032** — the `·` half of `7-Competitor-Comparison.md` §5 after gap #15 closed, and the first row added to §8 (#16) with **every vendor cell left at `·`**: one search for the competitors' export pages returned content-marketing pages rather than feature pages, so the matrix says what it did not check instead of upgrading cells on a hunch.
**Wired:** four tabs — **Export register** (file cards whose left border *is* the FEMA clock, the paper gaps printed on the face of each card, six sort orders, five triaged filters), **Letters of undertaking** (scoped per registration per year, archive rather than delete, the next FY's due day named), **Watch list** and **The rule** (the band table with its instruments and the disputed day) — four stat cards, five CSVs, `?new=1` / `?lut=1` / `?file=<invoice_number>` deep links, nav "Exports & LUT" + ⌘K *Record export file* + a GST-hub ghost button, an **export banner on `/invoices/[id]`** that points at the register rather than repeating its dates, a cross-link from `/fx`, and `/gst/calendar` calling `watchRows()` so the two pages cannot disagree about a deadline.
**Traps recorded:**
- **An import nothing uses is a feature that was skipped, not noise.** The calendar's export wiring had been planned and never landed — only the import line survived. `eslint`'s `no-unused-vars` on the touched file is what proved it; a round is not wired until the page that consumes it calls the function.
- **A glyph count depends on the regex, and the wrong one reads low.** Registry keys can be quoted (`'doc-check':`), so `/^  [a-z-]+: /` misses every dashed key. The counting regex is `/^  '?([a-z0-9-]+)'?:\s/gm`, and one of its 70 hits is the interface's `name: string` line — the registry is **69**.
- **A `new_string` that stops early deletes what came next.** An edit to `commands.ts` ended mid-keywords and swallowed the following action's `id` line, which failed the build for a reason the diff did not show. Whole blocks in, whole blocks out; re-read the file after.
- **A non-GST default gets words, not a rate.** FEMA rows land in the calendar as `category: 'other'` with `penalty.note`; giving them `lateFeePerDay` would have the estimator print ₹-per-day on a contravention that has no statutory late fee — Rule 27's invented figure in calendar clothing.
- **A one-day disagreement between sources stays a question.** The November-2025 amendment is dated 13-11-2025 and read as effective that day or the next; the band carries `disputed { from, to, instead_months, why }` and the page prints both answers. Averaging or silently picking one would look tidier and be a guess the user cannot see.
- **The row shape of a markdown table is part of the claim.** §8 is `# | Gap | Who has it | Cost to close`: a stray `| ✔ |` makes five cells and the honesty note has to live inside the Gap cell, not in a column of its own.
**Method:** 309 assertions in `/tmp/exports-test.mjs` (store round-trip with the version stamp, every band edge including the disputed day on both sides of it, window maths across FY boundaries and month-ends, route/kind/claim metadata, LUT coverage and the next window, file and realisation validation, the `/fx` join including `sources_diverge`, `unregisteredExports`, paper gaps, refund states, watch rows and their hrefs, sort orders, `summarise()`, all five CSV shapes); the other fifteen suites re-ran green — **2,888 assertions, 0 failing across 16 suites**; `tsc` 0 errors; `eslint` 0 new problems on all eight touched files (`/invoices/[id]` keeps six pre-existing `no-explicit-any` errors plus its `set-state-in-effect` and `<img>` warnings, untouched); `npm run build` ✓ 60 routes (58 pages + 2 api) with `/(app)/gst/exports/page` in the route manifest; icon and `var()` audit clean across the eight files (23 tokens, all resolving against `:root`; 0 hex on the new page, only the shared scrim and notice-close `rgba`s). **The interactive UI is still unverified** — the Supabase dev session is expired (task #84), so `/gst/exports` redirects to `/login`.
**Ledger:** **49 pages on the kit, 4 partial, 0 pre-kit** (+3 auth, + root redirect, + public `portal`) — the arithmetic is 49 + 4 = **53 pages inside the shell**, 58 in `src/app`. Counts move to **60 routes / 44 libraries / 69 glyphs / 16 suites / 2,888 assertions**. §5's export row leaves the TaxFlow side stated from this codebase; every vendor cell is still `·`, and the next round should fetch real vendor pages before claiming anyone lacks the feature.

### Session 47 — 2026-10-09 (R2-26 — the books vault: every register into one file the user owns)
**Built:** `src/lib/vault.ts` (1,296 lines, `8aae49e`) and `src/app/(app)/vault/page.tsx` (1,071 lines, `ba15288`) over `taxflow:vault:<orgId>` (the vault's own log, deliberately not in the file), recorded as **ADR-033** — the round taken because every ranked client-side gap was closed and the twenty-five ADR-013 registers existed on one device only, with no ownable, readable file; no cloud competitor offers a portable workpaper file, and the honest answer to offline-first is a backup, not a promise about hardware. `taxflow:invoice-template:` was extracted from `InvoiceDocument.tsx` into `src/lib/invoice-template.ts` (36 lines) — a preference a backup has to walk is data, and a library test cannot import a `.tsx`.
**Wired:** four tabs — **Backup** (the four properties as decision cards, the live snapshot with the file name it would write and its three heaviest registers, the by-group inventory, the vault history log), **Restore** (the file picker, the nine-state plan tiles, the per-row table with `File → here` counts, the danger button gated by an explicit confirmation when the plan would replace or clear, the `from → seen` report), **Registers** (searchable, group-filtered inventory of all thirty keys with the module that writes each) and **Not in this file** (the four deliberate exclusions, the five evidence logs with their printed reasons, the ADR-013 closing card); plus the sidebar Account entry "Books Vault", two ⌘K actions (*Back up my books* / *Restore from a vault file*), a card on `/settings`, and a note on `/offline` explaining why the queue is shown but never restored.
**Traps recorded:**
- **A loader that adds a virtual row breaks a count-based proof.** `loadGodowns` deliberately hands back the virtual `Main` beside the stored rows, so the vault's first godown `verify` (a length count) read one higher than the file held. Verify by **identity, not length** — the rows that count are the stored ids that survived the parse and are still on the registry.
- **Measure the vault, not the seed.** The first test browser was seeded with ten rows the owning modules' parsers would silently drop (wrong `newEvent` argument order, `ends` where the LUT wants `to_date`, `code`/`foreign` where FX wants `currency`/`foreign_value`/`booked_rate`, `bill_rate` where the hour row wants `rate`, a build with 0 units, a recipe with no output product, a disposition with qty 0, a challan without its deposit date). Every "restore" would then have tested empty registers. The suite now has a D2 section pushing each seeded row back through its own parser before any vault assertion runs.
- **The plan's arithmetic has to be read from the code, not from the hope.** `counts.same` is 25, not 28, because the five logs are `evidence`; after a restore `replace` is non-zero because the stamping modules re-write `updatedAt`, so the byte-for-byte check holds only for the 25 − `replace` — the honest assertion is `same + replace = 25` plus `replace > 0`.
- **A refused restore still writes the log.** `applyRestore` calls `remember()` unconditionally, so "nothing was touched" is false at the `taxflow:vault:` key — the M-section assertion had to exclude the vault log, and the history card is a record of attempts, not only successes.
**Method:** 240 assertions in `/tmp/vault-test.mjs` (the inventory of thirty named keys and its uniqueness, the checksum's stability and sensitivity including non-ASCII, the empty browser, the seeded round-trip through all thirty registers with every read-back proved through the owning loader, the five fault sentences, the nine-state plan on four different browsers, the tampered block refused while its neighbours restore, the always-written log, the state metadata and the group distribution); the other sixteen suites re-ran green — **3,128 assertions, 0 failing across 17 suites**; `tsc` 0 errors; `eslint` 0 new problems on all seven touched files (`/settings` keeps its three pre-existing `no-explicit-any` errors and `<img>` warning, untouched); `npm run build` ✓ **61 routes (59 pages + 2 api)** with `○ /vault` in the manifest; 0 hex on the new page, 90 `var()`s over 18 tokens every one resolving against `:root`, one shared `rgba` recipe. **The interactive UI is still unverified** — the Supabase dev session is expired, so `/vault` redirects to `/login`.
**Ledger:** **50 pages on the kit, 4 partial, 0 pre-kit** — 54 pages inside the shell, **59** in `src/app`. Counts move to **61 routes / 46 libraries / 69 glyphs / 17 suites / 3,128 assertions**. The two new libraries are `vault.ts` and `invoice-template.ts`; the extraction changed no stored shape (`classic` still reads as the default skin; the bare-string encoding is documented in both the library and the vault spec).
**Commit:** `8aae49e` (engine + template extraction), `ba15288` (page + wiring), docs in this commit.

## Session — R2-27: the Tally bridge reads its own skip list again (2026-10-09)

**What was built:** the two most consequential names came off ADR-029's printed skip list. **B1** (`0e5b4f3`, first half) extracted `createCreditNote` / `createDebitNote` from the two note pages into `src/lib/creditnote.ts` — one private `createNote` over a `NoteSpec` (sequence table, document table, items table, prefix, the field names each side calls the same ideas) — so the forms and the Tally bridge write identical rows, numbered `CN-<FY>-NNNN` / `DN-<FY>-NNNN` in the financial year **the note is dated in**, the way `createInvoice` numbers an invoice. **B2** (same commit, second half) added `noteItem()` to `tally.ts`: a Credit Note / Debit Note voucher becomes a note row against the document it reverses, wired through the page (register read gains both note tables, the write dispatch gains the note branch resolving party/original/product ids from `made`, the import record and its log chips count `credit_notes` / `debit_notes`, the plan groups carry the two targets under their own glyphs).

**Why it took the shape it took:**
- **The original is never invented.** A note that named no document would be the one import that books money without a source. So the planner requires the invoice or bill it reverses to exist — on the register (by number, or by the `#<Tally ref>` an earlier import left in its notes) or as a ready row planned from the same file. The same-file case forced `buildPlan` into two passes: documents first (collecting a map keyed by the folded Tally voucher number), then the notes whose `requires` carry that document's plan key — `expandSelection` pulls the original, and `runImport` fills the real id from the ids already written.
- **The `#` anchor does not survive folding.** The first `findOriginalDocument` matched `#<ref>` inside `normTagName(notes)` — but the fold strips `#` and `-`, so the match could never fire. The test caught it (`[7,2]` blocked instead of `[7,1]`); the fix matches the raw, case-folded notes. Lesson restated: *a marker you search for must be searched for in the text that still carries it.*
- **A note rides the side its page writes.** The credit row's fields were mirrored from the credit-notes `handleSave` (including `place_of_supply_name`, `supply_type`, `cess_amount`), the debit row's from the debit-notes page (none of those three). The item payload was stripped to the 15 columns the note item tables actually have — no `discount_percent`, no `cess_rate`; the discount travels inside `taxable_value` and says so in `derived`.
- **What the row cannot carry, the planner refuses.** A debit note whose voucher charged cess blocks (no cess column on the debit row); a credit note whose cess names no rate anywhere blocks ("the note would drop it"); quantities arrive positive because the minus sign *is* the reversal.
- **The sample had to balance too.** CN-41 was first written with the tax ledgers negative and the party positive — off by ₹4,800 by the reader's Σ rule. A return debits the sales value and credits the party: entries +216 +216 −2832, line +2400, zero. The suite asserts every sample voucher balances, and now the notes do as well.
- **A latent page bug fell out of wiring the register read:** `asDoc` emitted `party` where the library's `ExistingDocument` declares `party_name`, so the lookalike warning could never fire from live page data. Fixed in the same commit.

**Method:** 670 assertions in `/tmp/tally-test.mjs` — a new "notes" section over sign-balanced synthetic files (same-file credit note with its write order `product → customer → invoice → credit_note`; same-file debit note with its bill requirement; a note against a register bill resolving `bill_id` by number; the DN-cess block; the unknown-reference block; the cess-without-rate block; the already-match by Tally reference), plus the sample section extended to six vouchers (ready 8 / blocked 1 / skipped 2 / attention 1, the note's field-by-field row, the second-read note finding its party and original through the notes' `#V-101`) and the log fixture carrying the two new counters; the other sixteen suites re-ran green — **3,191 assertions, 0 failing across 17 suites**; `tsc` 0; `eslint` 0 new problems (`creditnote.ts` carries the repo's `supabase: any` baseline); `npm run build` ✓ **62 routes (59 pages + 2 api — the manifest now lists the PWA `manifest.webmanifest` metadata route, which is where the extra one comes from; no page was added)**. **The interactive UI is still unverified** — the Supabase dev session is expired (task #84), so `/tally` redirects to `/login`.

**Ledger:** pages unchanged at **59** (the kit count unchanged); counts move to **62 routes / 47 libraries / 69 glyphs / 17 suites / 3,191 assertions**. The new library is `creditnote.ts`; ADR-029's boundary clause was reopened and closed in the same round — notes are planned, receipts and payments are not, and both sentences now say so.

## Session — R2-28: the recipe costs time as well as parts (2026-10-09)

**What was built:** one commit (`bcdd305`, engine and page together) closed the boundary ADR-031 had printed since R2-24 — "no labour hour is priced". A recipe now stores `labour_minutes` and `overhead_minutes` per run, and the same key holds **dated shop-rate rows** (`taxflow:bom:<orgId>.rates` — no new key, so the vault's obligation needed a spec update, not a new register): `from_date`, `labour_per_hour`, `overhead_per_hour`, a note; the latest row at or before a date prices the work, one row per `from_date`.

**Why it took the shape it took:**
- **Two bases that never add.** Material is re-read at today's catalogue prices; the time legs price at the rate in force on *the run's own day* — a September run is not repriced by an October rate. `summarise` keeps `value_built_fy`, `labour_fy` and `overhead_fy` in separate columns, and the page keeps them apart in the same sentence rather than summing the bases.
- **Absence is a dash, zero is a claim.** A rate of ₹0 is refused, not clamped, and a leg with no rate in force prints `null` with an amber finding naming the reason — never a parts-only total dressed as a full cost. A recipe that carries no minutes at all is an honest zero (grey finding: the cost is parts alone), and its margin reports the *material* basis even though the full cost is priced, because its full cost *is* the parts.
- **Additive fields under `BOM_VERSION = 1`.** An old envelope reads zero minutes and no rates — what the shop had recorded when the field did not exist; a version bump would read every existing register as empty, the one outcome the stamp exists to prevent.
- **A real engine bug fell out of the tests.** `fullCostOfRun` rolled materials for one unit (`requirement(recipe, 1)`) while adding per-run minutes, so a no-minutes recipe printed a full cost per unit of material ÷ output and the margins were wrong by the run size. Now it rolls for the run (`requirement(recipe, recipe.output_qty)`) and a zero-leg recipe agrees exactly with the material basis. The margin-basis flag had the same disease (a no-minutes recipe reported 'full'); fixed with a `has_minutes` guard.
- **A row with neither figure is not a rate.** `validateRate` gained the both-null refusal after the docstring and the code disagreed — a row saved with neither figure would have silently vanished on the next parse.

**Method:** 530 assertions in `/tmp/bom-test.mjs` (a new section 20: dated rate selection, one row per `from_date` with last-wins on parse, run-scaled minutes, a September run priced against the rate then in force while its overhead prints nothing, the FY summary keeping the three bases apart, the full cost per unit at 36.67, the margin-basis flags, the both-null refusal, the CSV's 18 columns); the other sixteen suites re-ran green — **3,312 assertions, 0 failing across 17 suites**; `tsc` 0; `eslint` 0 new problems; `npm run build` ✓ **62 routes unchanged**. **The interactive UI was unverified at build time** — the Supabase dev session was expired (task #84), so `/inventory/recipes` redirected to `/login`; it was driven live later the same day, see the verification session below.

**Ledger:** pages unchanged at **59**; counts stay at **62 routes / 47 libraries / 69 glyphs / 17 suites** and move to **3,312 assertions**. No new library and no new key — the rate rows live inside `taxflow:bom:<orgId>`, and the vault's spec for the register grew them into its label ("Recipes, builds and shop rates") and its count.

## Session — verification: the dev session came back (2026-10-09)

**What was checked:** the standing "browser pass still blocked" sentence that has been re-printed at the end of every round since session 25 was tested rather than trusted — it *was* true (the session was expired), and after the user signed in at `localhost:3000` the pages that had never been seen in a browser were walked in order. Everything below is what the live DOM printed, not what the code says it should print.

- **`/inventory/recipes` (R2-28, write path included).** Header: *"Parts are priced from your catalogue on every load, labour and overhead from your dated rate rows — this register stores quantities, minutes and dated figures, never a guessed price"*; five tabs; the minutes inputs carry their own helper sentences (*"paid time one run takes — priced at the labour rate in force when a run happens, never typed in rupees"*, *"machine, power and rent time the run absorbs — same rule, its own rate"*). A probe recipe + a probe rate row (`2026-10-01`, labour ₹600/hr, overhead ₹400/hr) landed **only** in `taxflow:bom:<orgId>` at `version: 1` with `labour_minutes: 45`, `overhead_minutes: 30`, `output_qty: 10`, `status: 'active'` — the Supabase side untouched. The honesty states all rendered: `NO FULL VALUE YET — SEE THE GAPS BELOW`, *"labour minutes recorded, but no shop rate in force on 2026-10-09 — add a rate row on the Shop rates tab"*, *"no cost recorded on Consulting — the card prints a dash, not ₹0"*, and the card reading *Cost / unit, full → — (1 gap)*. Typing ₹0 for a rate was refused and the register still held one row afterwards. With the row in force, the page printed *"In force today (09 Oct 26): labour ₹600/hr · overhead ₹400/hr from 01 Oct 26"*. The probe was deleted and the eight pre-existing keys came back unchanged.
- **`/cost-centres`** (R2-21) — *"the rupees are read back from the books every time this page opens, so nothing here can drift away from them"*, the unassigned pile printed as money (`₹16,95,479`, 12 documents), the boundary sentence (*"A cost centre is not a legal person. Nothing on this page reaches a return, and no tax figure is invented here"*) and the rule 41A / partial-use flags that stand in for an invented figure.
- **`/gst/exports`** (R2-25) — both clocks named in the sub-header (*"Two clocks run here: FEMA on the money, section 54(1) on the tax"*), the empty register saying what a file *is* rather than showing a blank grid, and the footer's *"A workpaper, not a filing. Proceeds are counted from /fx when it holds the deal, balances are receipted on /payments"*.
- **`/gst/gstr4`** (R2-13, the page from task #84's debt) — live figures all derived: aggregate ₹13.04L over 7 documents, tax charged ₹1.6L with the implied 12.25% beside it, deemed debit ₹9.77K on ₹3,90,979 of purchases, ₹1.7L payable *"no credit ledger to offset it"*, annual due date printed as `2027-04-30 (202 days)`, and the correct warning for a non-composition GSTIN (*"This GSTIN is recorded as regular, not composition. The workpaper still computes…"*).
- **`/gst/reconciliation`** (batch 6) — the month picker, the template/import controls, and the count read from the books rather than assumed (`0 ITC-eligible purchase bills in Oct 2026`); the parsing promise and the portal path are printed in the drop zone, and no file left the browser.
- **`/gst/einvoice`** (batch 6, functional) — `27AABCU9603R1ZX · 5 of 5 issued invoices can be signed as they are`, the 30-day window sentence, and stats Ready 5 / To fix 0 / Drafts 1 / All 6. Clicking **IRN JSON** built the real payload in the page: `Ctx 1.10`, `InvoiceType RGC`, `DocumentType INV`, `InvoiceNumber INV-2026-27-0007`, `InvoiceDate 28-Sep-2026`, buyer GSTIN and address filled.
- **Also re-walked with a live session:** `/vault` (8 of thirty registers populated, the bom row labelled *"Recipes, builds and shop rates"*), `/tally` (R2-27 — the sample file's credit note appears in the plan as `Credit Note #CN-41 · 2025-04-25 · 1 line`), `/projects` (h1 *Projects & time*, *"this register stores measurements, never amounts"*).
- **Console** on every page above: the React DevTools info line and `[HMR] connected`. No errors, no warnings, no error boundary.

**What this corrects in the record:** the Tally sample's plan reads **Ready 7 / Blocked 1 / Already 1 / Needs your eye 1** live, where the round's notes said ready 8. The one-row difference is the dedupe doing its job — this org's catalogue already holds a product the sample names, so that row arrives pre-matched. Nothing was wrong in either place; the docs quoted a synthetic browser, the app had a real one.

**The width pass (Reminder 12) ran too, without a visible panel.** Screenshots and pointer clicks stay blocked (`viewport 0×0` on the in-app Browser surface), but layout does not need them: a same-origin iframe pinned at the measured width loads the real route with the real session, and `documentElement.scrollWidth` answers the question pixels would have. Results — **zero page-level horizontal overflow at both 355px and 1440px on every page measured**: `/inventory/recipes`, `/cost-centres`, `/gst/exports`, `/gst/gstr4`, `/gst/reconciliation`, `/gst/einvoice`, `/vault`, `/tally`, `/projects` (at 355 the shell reads `clientWidth 344` — the 11px is the scrollbar the phone layout does not grow, and `scrollWidth` equals it; at 1440 it is 1429 or 1440, again equal). `/fx` and `/gst/job-work` were measured at 355 as well, same result. The only elements wider than the phone viewport sit **inside** a `overflow-x: auto` scroller — the `.tab-bar` strips (`/inventory/recipes`: *Where used* / *Shop rates* / *Archived* spill to 498px inside a 312px bar; `/gst/exports` 5, `/cost-centres` 1, `/fx` 1, `/gst/job-work` 5) — which is the designed treatment, reachable by swipe, not a page that runs off the screen. Every iframe was removed after its reading.

**Ledger:** no code changed, no counts moved. Task #84 is closed, and the standing "browser pass still blocked by the expired dev session" sentence stops being written at the end of new rounds — the session is live; the remaining browser gap is *visual* only (screenshots and click-through still need the in-app panel opened).

## Session — R2-29: a build says which lot it emptied (2026-10-10)

**What was built:** two commits — `7de09ee` (the engine half in `src/lib/bom.ts`) and `11adb45` (the page half in `/inventory/recipes`) — closed the *other* sentence ADR-031 had printed since R2-24: "a build draws quantities, not lots". A build line now carries the picks it drew from, the run mirrors them into `taxflow:batchmoves:` as `adjust` moves under the job number, and the finished units land as a lot of their own whose `batch_no` is the job reference.

**Why it took the shape it took:**
- **FEFO proposes; the shop decides.** `allocateLots()` offers the earliest-expiring lot with stock left, honouring the run's godown when it names one, and the editor lets the user switch that lot, split one component across several, or name none. Naming none is a *warning*, not a refusal — a shop that keeps no batch register must still be able to record a run, and the alternative (blocking the write) would push it to a spreadsheet.
- **The pick is a decision, not a balance.** `taxflow:bom:` stores the quantities the shop chose, and `validateLotIssues()` plus the page's per-lot clash check re-read the *live* batch register at the moment of writing. A sale made in another tab after the draft was opened therefore refuses the run ("CH-2026-JUL: 5 NOS asked, 3 NOS in it") instead of being talked around by a stale preview.
- **The register's move kinds were not widened.** `taxflow:batchmoves:` knows `sale | adjust | transfer` only, so a build issue is an `adjust` carrying the job in `doc_ref` and spelling it out again in the note. Same paper logic as the shelf RPC's `adjustment_out` in R2-24: the honest label beats a invented type.
- **A produced lot prints its gaps rather than filling them.** No cost in the run → the note says *no cost recorded* and the reason, because a ₹0 valuation would quietly value the shop's finished stock at nothing. No expiry, no godown → the note says the run recorded neither and the lot stands unplaced (Main) until the shop edits it.
- **A run that has been sold on is history.** `drawnElsewhereFrom()` looks at the produced lot's negative moves excluding this job's, and a non-empty list turns the Cancel button into a refusal that points at `/inventory/batches`. Reversing a sale by cancelling a build would rewrite the past twice over.
- **The reversal keeps the paper's shape and turns only the verb.** `reverseLotWritesFor()` was going to print "Cancel JOB-… — Chocolate 1 issued from lot …", which reads as a *second issue*; it now rewrites the middle too — *"went back to lot"* — with a test asserting no reversal note still contains the word "issued".
- **Two things the live page taught that the code did not.** A clean run (no variance, no notes) had no way to open its detail panel, so the lot picks and the produced-lot line were unreachable — the job sub-line now carries an `N lots drawn — see which` toggle in the middle `·` segment; and that toggle's leading separator landed *inside* the button, printing "movements written2 lots drawn" until it was hoisted out.

**Method:** 631 assertions in `/tmp/bom-test.mjs` (section 21 covers the lot engine: FEFO order across expiries, a godown-limited draw, a split pick, negative writes with the job in `doc_ref`, the note reading as paper, a draw with no lots writing nothing, the reversal's symmetry — same lots, same order, opposite signs, drawn-plus-returned summing to zero — and `lotTrail`'s one-line-per-component reading *"CH-2026-JUL 1 + CH-2026-AUG 0.44"*); the other sixteen suites re-ran green — **3,413 assertions, 0 failing across 17 suites**; `tsc` 0; `eslint` 0 (two dead imports and one `exhaustive-deps` memo dropped/reasoned along the way); `npm run build` was not re-run for the two R2-29 commits themselves — no route, no library, no glyph and no key was added, the fields ship inside `taxflow:bom:` at `BOM_VERSION = 1`, so Rule 31's vault duty is met by the existing spec. It was re-run once the vault sentence changed (`e84461f`): **exit 0**, 59 `page.tsx` + 2 `route.ts` + `manifest.ts` = **62 routes** on disk, unchanged.

**Verified live, in the dev session:** the editor offered the July lot first and let the August lot be chosen by hand; an overdraw disabled *Record* with the is-hot reason beside it; a draft with no lot named printed *"no lot named — 3 NOS will leave the shelf and the lot register will not know where from"* and still booked its finished lot; recording wrote the two `adjust` moves and the produced lot, and `/inventory/batches` read the draws (TF-TEST-AUG: 10 in, 5 out, 3 returned → 8 left), the sold-out July lot and the finished lot at *"—"*. Cancelling JOB-2026-27-0001 wrote *"Cancel JOB-2026-27-0001 — Consulting 4 went back to lot TF-TEST-JUL"* (+4) and its August pair (+2), and the shelf came back with it — `/products` re-read **Consulting 64** and **Jeans 50** after the reversal. The sold-from refusal was provoked on purpose: a test sale against a finished lot turned Cancel into *"…has already given units to TEST-SALE-1 … a run that has been sold on is history."*. Width pass re-run on `/inventory/recipes` and `/inventory/batches` at 355 and 1440 through the pinned same-origin iframe — page `scrollWidth == clientWidth` at both (344 at 355, the 11px scrollbar), and the two expanded lot panels measured 312px wide with zero overflowing element; the only elements reporting excess are the `tf-pagehead` decorative `::after` (a radial at `right: -60px`, `overflow: hidden` clips it — content sits inside at 277/312) and `.tab-bar`, which scrolls by documented design. The vault row was re-read after its description grew with the picks (`e84461f`): `/vault?tab=registers` prints *"…what each build actually drew and from which lots…"* beside the bom register's *1 recipe · 2 builds · 0 rate rows · 1 job counters* — the register list is the app describing itself, and the sentence now matches the storage.

**Cleanup, and what the app refused:** the test lots and every move went through the batches page's own Delete actions, and the shelf restored itself as above. The probe *recipe* could not be deleted — `removeRecipe` refuses any recipe with runs on the log, and the page says so: *"2 builds on the log were made from this recipe — archive it instead of deleting it."* Two cancelled runs and the recipe stay in this browser as history; that is the doctrine working, not residue.

**Ledger:** pages unchanged at **59**; counts stay at **62 routes / 47 libraries / 69 glyphs / 17 suites**, moving to **3,413 assertions**. `src/lib/bom.ts` grew to 1,964 lines and the recipes page to 2,505; the build-log CSV gained its 12th column, *Lots drawn*. No new key and no new register spec — the picks ride inside `taxflow:bom:` — but the vault's sentence for that register grew with them ("…what each build actually drew **and from which lots**…"), because the vault list is the product's own description of what it stores and R2-26's ADR makes an out-of-date sentence a defect, not a cosmetic lapse.

## Session — R2-30: the materials board writes the order (2026-10-10)

**What was built:** two commits — `2b72431` (the engine half in `src/lib/bom.ts`: `mergedShortages`, `SupplyReading` / `SupplierParty` / `BuyLine` / `BuyOrder` / `ShoppingPlan`, `shoppingPlan`, `orderNote`, `SHOPPING_CSV_HEADERS` + `shoppingCsvRows`, and `appendDraft` / `raiseDrafts` in `src/lib/orderflow.ts`) and `d49c506` (the page half: the "Turn the shortage into paper" section on the materials board of `/inventory/recipes`). The third deepening of the same page after R2-28 and R2-29, taken again from the `·` manufacturing row of `7-Competitor-Comparison.md` §4 — every ranked gap is still closed or blocked. No route, no library, no glyph, no storage key: the paper lands in `taxflow:porder:<orgId>`, which the purchase-order register already owns.

**Why it took the shape it took:**
- **One fold, one number.** The page had grown its own copy of the shortage arithmetic; `mergedShortages()` replaced it and `summarise()` was rewritten on top of the same call, so the stat card, the board grid and the quantity that reaches a vendor are one sum. Rounding happens once, in that fold (`roundQty`), and `for_recipes` dedupes so a recipe that is short of the same part twice does not appear twice on the line.
- **A supplier is whoever the last bill says.** `latestSupplier()` keeps the newest line that names one — ties to the row read last, unnamed vendors skipped — which is the rule `/inventory/reorder` has used since R2-7, imported rather than restated. The rate comes from that bill's own line and falls back to `products.purchase_price` only when no bill names one, and the basis is *labelled* on the line (`last bill` / `product master` / `no price recorded`), because a number whose source the reader cannot see is a claim.
- **Refusing before the register does.** Three cases send a line to `unrouted` with its sentence printed — no bill ever named a supplier, the supplier has left the vendor list, and the vendor card has no state (`… so there is no place of supply to write`). The third is the interesting one: `validateDoc` in the order register requires a place of supply, so a board that raised that draft would be handing the shop a document its own register refuses. Declining first, and saying which card to go and fill, is the same rule the batches page uses against a stale lot balance.
- **One unpriced line and the order has no value.** `value = lines.some(l => l.rate === null) ? null : round2(…)`. A sum of just the priced lines reads as *the* value of the order; the card prints "— no value yet", `orderNote` says "the draft carries 0 until you set it", and the CSV leaves the rate and the est. value **blank** rather than 0. It is R2-28's two-bases rule pointed at the buy side, and it is the same house rule as an unpriced hour at `/projects` and a missing shop rate on a cost leg.
- **The number is taken, never typed.** `raiseDrafts()` walks `appendDraft()` → `takeNumber()`, the register's per-FY high-water mark, and the whole board's drafts go out in one `saveStore`. A draft born on the recipes page is indistinguishable from one typed at `/purchase-orders` — same numbering, same status field, same cancel path — which is what ADR-013's "write through the owner" means when a second page wants to use your register.
- **Nothing is read until the shop asks.** `ensureSupply()` runs only on the *Build the shopping list* click: active vendors, non-cancelled bills, then items in 500-id batches narrowed to the short product ids, so a year of bills cannot flood the board. A failure puts the state back to `idle` with a warning instead of rendering an empty plan as a finding.
- **And it stops at a draft.** The button says *Raise N order drafts* and the flash says *open one on the register to confirm it*, because a purchase order is a promise to a vendor. Auto-confirming would have been the more impressive demo and the less honest one.

**Found and fixed on the way:** the first page cut swallowed the `{orders.length > 0 && demand.length === 0 && (` guard while inserting the new section — TS1381 "Unexpected token" at the orphaned `)}`; a `React.ReactNode[]` type reference had no default `React` import behind it, so the raised-links strip became a keyed `.map`; and `type BuyOrder` was imported into the page and left unused. All three caught before the commit, not after.

**Method:** 95 assertions in `/tmp/shopping-test.mjs` — the fold across recipes with its rounding, dedupe and short-desc order; `latestSupplier` on ties, blank dates and unnamed vendors; the rate basis and its label; all three `unrouted` refusals verbatim; the null-value rule with `unpriced` naming the lines; line / order / unrouted sort orders; `orderNote` with and without a hole; both CSV shapes with blanks kept blank. `tsc` 0; `eslint` 0 on both touched files; `npm run build` ✓ 62 routes (59 pages + 2 api) — nothing was added, so the count did not move. **The older seventeen suites could not be re-run:** `/tmp` was wiped on 2026-10-10 and they went with it, so this round records its own number and does not carry the 3,413 total forward (see gotcha 4).

**Verified live, in the dev session:** the org had no shortage at all when the pass began, so the section was correctly absent — the board said *Nothing is short* and there was nothing to raise. A throwaway recipe was built through the app's own editor (`?new=1`, native setters plus change/input events) to give the board one, and the chain then read end to end: *Consulting* short **86 NOS**, routed to **Mohd Faizan** at **₹25,000** with the *last bill* label, `Raise 1 order draft` writing **`PO-2026-27-0001`** through the register's numbering, `/purchase-orders/<id>` rendering the lines and the "Raised from the recipe board on…" note, and the draft cancelled through the register's own path. The probe recipe was deleted afterwards; the one cancelled `PO-2026-27-0001` stays on the register as the evidence that the pass ran. Console clean. Width pass on `/inventory/recipes` at 355px through the pinned same-origin iframe: `scrollWidth == clientWidth`, no page-level overflow, every iframe removed after its reading.

**Ledger:** pages unchanged at **59**; **62 routes / 47 libraries / 69 glyphs / no new key**. `src/lib/bom.ts` grew from 1,964 to **2,202** lines and the recipes page from 2,505 to **2,778**; `src/lib/orderflow.ts` is 489. The shopping CSV is nine columns. No vault spec changed — `taxflow:bom:` still holds the recipes, builds, rate rows and lot picks, and the orders this board raises are the purchase-order register's rows, already in the vault's own list.

## Session — R2-31: the salary register, walked to the paisa (2026-10-10)

**What was built:** two commits — `140d1e0` (the engine half: `src/lib/payroll.ts`, **1,947 lines**, plus its vault spec, +22 lines in `src/lib/vault.ts`) and `9393529` (the page half: `src/app/(app)/payroll/page.tsx`, **1,381 lines**, 96 added / 13 removed in `/gst/calendar`, four ⌘K actions in `src/lib/commands.ts`, the sidebar entry in `src/lib/nav.ts`, and the one-declaration `.tab-bar` fix in `globals.css` (`max-width: 100%`, its comment rewritten to say why — the `flex-shrink: 0` on the children was already there since the kit was built)), over the new `taxflow:payroll:<orgId>` (ADR-013, `PAYROLL_VERSION = 1`), recorded as **ADR-034**. The round was taken from `7-Competitor-Comparison.md` §4's **Payroll** row — the last `·` in the matrix that a competitor advertises (Tally sells it at ₹750 + GST a month, Zoho at its Premium tier, ClearTax states it) and the last one this app can build entirely in the owner's browser. **B0** was a research pass, not a commit: every figure in the library's rule tables was fetched with its own instrument and effective date before a line of TypeScript was written.

**Why it took the shape it took:**
- **The register stores structure and exceptions, and nothing else.** Six monthly components with their PF/ESI flags per person, and four numbers per month (lost days, arrears, bonus, recovery). Gross, every statutory leg, net pay, the employer's cost and the tax are recomputed on every load — the recipe book (ADR-031) and the cost centres (ADR-028) already refuse to store a rupee, and a stored gross would disagree with the structure the moment anyone corrected a component.
- **Section 192 is a method, not a percentage.** `taxWalk()` runs April forward: elapsed months add their actual wages, later months are projected at the current month's rate, the estimate sheds the standard deduction capped at itself and the *year's* professional tax under section 16(iii) (`min(₹2,500, monthly × the months actually on the rolls)` — which only divides cleanly because the whole year was priced first), and the residue is spread over the months still to come, so a June bonus lifts July onward and the March row absorbs whatever the estimate under-collected. Nothing is stored as "TDS deducted": twelve instalments that add up to the year's tax is a property of the walk, not of the data.
- **A rate table that does not know its own date is a rumour.** `PF_WAGE_CEILINGS` moves ₹15,000 → ₹25,000 from **17-09-2026** (S.O. 5109(E)) and a September run against an August ceiling is a different payroll; `ESI_BANDS` stayed at ₹21,000, so the two statutes disagree on the same slip and the disagreement is printed. The month CSV carries *PF ceiling used* as its own column for exactly that reason.
- **Four refusals, each with its sentence.** A financial year the tables do not hold returns no tax figure; an employee aged 60 or above is refused one rather than given the general schedule, because the senior bands were in no source fetched; a lost-day count above the month's own length is refused rather than clipped (it would produce a negative salary); and a person with months recorded is **not deletable** — they go off the rolls with a leaving date, because deleting them would leave their PF and challan computed on a structure that no longer exists.
- **Absence and zero never share a row.** `pt: number | null` — unset prints a dash plus "not recorded", a real `0` prints zero and claims it, and the CSV leaves the cell blank for the first and writes `0` for the second.
- **The unsettled questions stay questions.** Whether the *pensionable* salary for EPS moved with the September ceiling is settled by no document fetched, so `settings.eps_pension_ceiling` is the owner's figure and every slip prints the one it used; professional tax has no national table (the fetched state tables contradict each other), so `StatutoryLine.due` is the empty string and the dues card prints *Due on the state's own date — no national date to print*; and the four labour codes' 50% wage floor (in force 21-11-2025) is a flag with its citation, not an add-back.
- **The calendar reads the books instead of guessing.** `payrollEvents()` emits an ECR / ESIC / salary-TDS row per month actually run, one ESI return per contribution period it touched, and one Form 16 row for the year; the calendar drops the profile tick-box's monthly PF/PT guess for any month the register files, matching on the row id — and because a guessed row names the *deposit* month, `addMonths(..., -1)` steps back to the salary month before asking. Undated rows go to a *State dates* rail, count as neither overdue nor due soon, and stay out of the `.ics` because an event needs a start date.
- **One new figure, and it is not folded in anywhere.** `costPerHour()` / `blendedHourCost()` turn a month's employer cost into the rupee an hour, weighted by the month rather than averaged, and `/payroll`'s own Cost tab prints both — per person with the division written out, blended with the paid hours behind it. `/projects` does **not** import `payroll.ts`: no costed hour enters its contribution, and its "hours are priced, not costed" sentence is still exactly true. Re-read that page's copy before claiming otherwise — the build session left one line here saying the projects register "needs" the hours-a-day field, which was a statement about a future join, not a present one; the field's helper text now says what is actually built.

**Found and fixed on the way:** the 355px overflow was first blamed on the `PageHeader`, whose `scrollWidth` read 372 on a 344px viewport — a **false positive**: `.tf-pagehead` is `overflow: hidden` and its own decorative `::after { right: -60px }` is what widened it, so the excess was clipped and never reached the document. The real offender was the five-tab `div.tab-bar` (350 wide, content 504, right edge at 366): `Segmented` renders it `inline-flex`, so it sizes to its labels and pushes the page sideways. `max-width: 100%` in `globals.css` pulls it back and lets its own `overflow-x: auto` do the work; the desktop bar is unchanged at 504. The audit method is now **ancestor-aware** — recorded as trap 30.

**Method:** 435 assertions in `/tmp/payroll-test.mjs` — the dated bands and their mid-September switch, the ESI band and its disability ceiling, the PF split and the ceiling modes, the lost-day divisor under both conventions, the year-walk across joiners, leavers, mid-year bonuses and the March true-up, the standard-deduction cap and the ₹2,500 professional-tax cap, 87A with its marginal relief and the surcharge cliffs measured from the foot of the band, all four refusals with their sentences, the null-versus-zero PT rule through the CSV, `statutoryDues` / `missingHeads`, `payrollEvents` and its ids, and the hour cost. `tsc` 0; `eslint` 0 errors on the six touched files (`/gst/calendar` keeps its pre-existing `react-hooks/exhaustive-deps` warning at line 195); `npm run build` ✓ **63 routes (60 pages + 2 api)** with `○ /payroll` prerendered. **Live in the dev session:** the five tabs were driven through the logged-in app against a seeded register — the month run unfolding one slip's walk, the dues card naming its heads, the annual sheet, the hour costs — and the calendar was seen retiring its guessed PF/PT rows for the months the books filed and putting the undated PT row in its own rail. The seeded key was then removed, so the calendar fell back to its guessed rows and `/payroll` to its empty state; the register on disk is untouched. Console clean. Width pass at 355px through the pinned same-origin iframe over all five payroll tabs plus `/projects` and `/tally`: `docScrollWidth 344`, zero unclipped offenders, every iframe removed after its reading.

**Ledger:** **63 routes / 48 libraries / 69 glyphs / one new key** — the new library is `payroll.ts`, the new page is `/payroll`, and `nav.ts` reuses the `users` glyph, so the icon set did not move. Vault specs are now **31, 26 restorable** (the other five are flow logs that travel as evidence). The month CSV is 27 columns, the dues CSV seven and the annual sheet CSV nineteen — three exports, one per tab that holds a table. Components unchanged at **9**.

## Session — R2-32: the shop's sheet becomes lost days, and the sheet is thrown away (2026-10-10)
