**What was built:** three commits — `79e985b` (the engine half: the attendance section of `src/lib/payroll.ts`, lines **932–1538** of the file's now **2,564** lines, and the payroll vault spec's re-worded `what` line), `2942fa9` (the page half: the month tab's sheet panel in `src/app/(app)/payroll/page.tsx`, now **1,731** lines, plus two engine copy fixes and the `.pa-*` CSS) and `27388e1` (⌘K keywords on `action:payroll-month`, so the sheet is findable by the words a supervisor types) — recorded as an **amendment to ADR-034**, not a new ADR: the register's doctrine ("structure and exceptions in, every rupee walked out", ADR-034) is what made the round safe, because a lost-day figure was already one of the four stored exceptions. **No schema change, no new localStorage key, no new route, library, glyph or version bump** — `PAYROLL_VERSION` stays 1. The round was taken from `7-Competitor-Comparison.md` §4's Payroll row again, because every ranked client-side gap is still closed or blocked, and the row's remaining competitor feature was "import the attendance you already keep".

**Why it took the shape it took:**
- **A plan, a click, one door.** `planAttendance(store, month, text)` is pure and returns the delimiter, every column with its role, the basis with its sentence, the matches, the refusals, the findings and the sheet-level `blockers`; `applyAttendance(orgId, month, plan)` is the only writer and refuses a preview whose `plan.month` is not the screen's month. The page the owner approves and the write that follows are the same function's output, which is the only reason "here is what will change" can be trusted.
- **A number has to say which way it points.** `LOP_HEADERS` and `PRESENT_HEADERS` decide the direction a column carries; `AMBIGUOUS_HEADERS` (`days`, `total`, `count`) name a figure without a direction and `SERIAL_HEADERS` (`sl`, `no`, `#`) are excluded from being an identity, because reading a serial number as a payroll code would match strangers. A bare column of numbers stops the sheet: *"26" could be days present or days lost and the two give opposite salaries*.
- **The sheet outranks the setting, and a contradiction stops the row.** `attendanceBasis()` answers from `settings.working_days` (calendar or the 26-day convention), but a `24/26` cell becomes `kind: 'sheet'` because the paper states its own month length; when a row carries both a present and a lost column they must agree to the day, and a row that does not is refused rather than arbitrated. Blank means *the sheet does not say*, never zero: a person on the rolls the sheet forgot keeps their existing lost days and appears as a finding.
- **An identity is earned.** Code, then exact name, then one *unique* substring reported as `matched_on: 'approximate'`; two people sharing a code, two sharing a name, or a fragment that could be three are each refused with the line printed as the sheet shows it. `MAX_ATTENDANCE_ROWS = 1500` refuses a database as *"a month of people, not a database — split it"*.
- **Nothing the register would refuse gets in.** Every candidate goes back through `validateInput()` *inside* `mutate`, because the People tab can move a joining date between the preview and the click; `written = matches − skipped`, `unchanged` is counted separately, and arrears, bonus and recovery are never touched — a sheet says nothing about money.
- **The note is replaced, not stacked.** `stampAttendanceNote()` drops the previous `[attendance <month>]` segment and keeps whatever the owner typed after the em dash, so a month never carries two answers to the same question, and the only bytes that reach storage are one figure and one sentence.
- **The app prints the sheet it wants back.** `attendanceTemplate(store, month, grid)` rules the month's roster with **the register's own codes**, on screen or as `attendance-<month>.csv`, so a return paste can fail on a missing line but not on a spelling; and the reader is `detectDelimiter()` / `splitRow()` **imported from `bank-statement.ts`** — the app has not written a second CSV parser for a paste.

**Found and fixed on the way — four defects the suite could not see, all caught by driving the page:** the stranger-on-a-name-only-row refusal asserted the opposite of what it meant (*"…is on the payroll register"* — now *"Neither the code “E-99” nor the name “Nobody Atall” is on the payroll register"* when a code also fails, *"'Nobody Atall' is not on the payroll register"* when there is no code); `rowText()` printed a grid row's empty cells as `· · · · ·`, so a refused line could not be found on the sheet; the receipt read *"1 month row now carry"*; and `.pa-grid` stood as two ~135px columns at 355px until `/bank`'s 1100px media query was copied. **The deeper lesson is about the suite, not the page:** the 208 assertions running at the time could not have caught the inverted sentence, because the needle was `'is on the payroll register'` — a substring of both the wrong wording and the right one. Refusal needles now quote whole sentences. React 19 / eslint 9's `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` also refused the obvious `useEffect` reset for "a preview belongs to the month it was read against"; the page uses React's adjust-during-render recipe (previous-value state + conditional `setState` while rendering), which is now Rule 32's closing note and the repo's precedent for the pattern.

**Method:** 212 assertions in `/tmp/attendance-test.mjs` (delimiter and preamble handling, the header-accept rule `named >= 1 || days >= 3`, present-vs-LOP column reading under both bases and the `sheet` basis outranking the setting, the agreement/contradiction pair, the day grid's mark table and half-days, a `32` column in a 31-day month stopping the sheet, the identity ladder with `approximate` and its three ambiguity refusals, the row cap, the findings-not-zeroing rule, overflow flooring at zero, the stamped note replaced and hand text surviving, `written/unchanged/skipped` arithmetic through a moved joining date, and both template shapes); the 435 payroll assertions re-ran green beside them — the older suites are gone with `/tmp` by design, so no combined total is claimed. `tsc` 0; `eslint` 0 on the touched files; `npm run build` ✓ **63 routes (60 pages + 2 api)** — no route added. **Live in the dev session:** five real sheet shapes went through the panel (a *Present* column, a stated `24/26` basis, an *LOP* column, a day grid with half-days, and the app's own pre-ruled template), plus the blocker branch, the findings branch, *Do not apply* writing nothing, a re-import replacing the note while bonus and arrears survived, and a slip re-computed to **₹27,870.97 = 32,000 × 27/31** on the written figure. The org had no payroll data, so a register was seeded at `taxflow:payroll:<orgId>` in the exact `newEmployee()` shape and **deleted again** after the pass — the browser is back where it was. Console clean; width pass in the pinned same-origin iframe (one column, nothing clipped at 270px; page-level `scrollWidth == clientWidth` at 355px across all five payroll tabs).

**Ledger:** **63 routes / 48 libraries / 69 glyphs / no new key** — the counts did not move, which is the point of the round: a feature that reads a paste and stores one figure into a row that already existed adds no register, no spec and no glyph. The registry was re-counted rather than recalled: 70 lines matching `/^  '?([a-z0-9-]+)'?:\s/gm`, one of them the component's own `name: string` prop → **69**, and every `icon="…"` used by `/payroll` resolves in it. Vault specs stay **31, 26 restorable**, with the payroll spec's `what` line now naming the sheet. The payroll page's token audit was re-run too: **0 hex**, **133 `var()`s over 18 tokens** all resolving against `:root`, one shared `rgba` (the modal scrim) — the sheet panel added 54 of those `var()`s, and its chips take their borders from `color-mix(in srgb, var(--green) …)` rather than a literal.

## Session — R2-33: the register becomes paper — the payslip engine and its A4 page (2026-10-10)

**What was built:** two commits — `ac14df8` (the engine half: `src/lib/payslip.ts` new at **460** lines at commit, plus the letterhead surface in `src/lib/payroll.ts` — +123 lines: `Letterhead`, `EMPTY_LETTERHEAD`, 15 `LETTERHEAD_FIELDS` with hints, 4 `LETTERHEAD_IDENTIFIERS` each with its own `why` sentence, `letterheadIsEmpty` / `letterheadGaps` / `saveLetterhead` — and one re-worded line in `src/lib/vault.ts`: the payroll spec's `what`) and `6f68291` (the page half: `src/app/(app)/payroll/payslip/page.tsx` at **892** lines, plus `src/lib/pdf-export.ts` grown from 50 to **88** lines with `downloadElementsAsPdf()`, `employer_legs_total` (+3 lines) in `payslip.ts`, the *Payslips of the month* button and per-row Payslip links in `src/app/(app)/payroll/page.tsx`, three ⌘K actions in `src/lib/commands.ts` (`action:payslip`, `action:payslip-month`, `action:payslip-letterhead` through `?letterhead=1`), and one entry in `src/lib/nav.ts`) — recorded as an **amendment to ADR-034**, not a new ADR, and as the round that added the app's **sixty-first page** and **forty-ninth library**. **No schema change and no new localStorage key**: the letterhead lives *inside* `taxflow:payroll:<orgId>` because a document's heading belongs to the book that computes its figures (Rule 24's paragraph — *a heading is not a register*), so `PAYROLL_VERSION` stays 1 and the vault stays at **31 specs / 26 restorable** with only the payroll spec's `what` line grown. The round was taken from `7-Competitor-Comparison.md` §4's Payroll row again — this time its paper half: every payroll product in India ships the sheet an employee keeps, and this register had the arithmetic and not the paper.

**Why it took the shape it took:**
- **The paper is a reading, never a second computation.** `buildPayslip(store, slip, workspaceName, today)` receives the `Payslip` that `payslipOf()` already computed and lays its lines out; no rupee is re-derived, and the suite polices the *tie* rather than the layout — `earnings_total = monthly_total + arrears + bonus`, `deduction_total = lop_deduction + statutory_total`, `statutory_total = slip.deductions`, `employer_legs_total = Σ` the employer legs, and `employer_cost − net_pay = employer_legs_total + deduction_total − lop`. A slip can therefore never disagree with the register it came from.
- **A slip states, it does not pay.** The line reads *Net payable*, the figure repeats in words through the invoice's own `amountInWords()`, and four standing sentences (`PAYSLIP_BOUNDARIES`) print beside every sheet: payable is not paid, the statutory columns are computed not recorded, **this is not Form 16** (Part A is issued from TRACES), and nothing here is filed.
- **Absence is a dash with a sentence; zero is a claim.** `SlipLine.amount: number | null` plus `dash` — the same rule `pt: number | null` has carried since R2-31 — and the rule travels to the identifiers: a blank letterhead code prints a dash beside the line naming what it would be needed *for*, because a statutory-looking document carrying an invented establishment code is worse than one carrying a dash. The four identifiers asked for are `pf_establishment`, `esi_code`, `employer_pan`, `gstin`; a wholly blank letterhead is an **allowed state** — the slip falls back to the workspace name and prints its gaps beside the document.
- **A refused month prints no figures at all.** `printable: false` carries the engine's `why_not`, the deduction and employer blocks are not laid out, the header states the refusal instead of a ₹0.00 net, and Print and PDF stay disabled with the engine's sentence as the title — *"The register cannot compute this month, so there are no figures to sign."*
- **The exceptions stay on the owner's side of the paper.** The register's flags, the letterhead gaps and a missing UAN / ESIC number / PAN print in a `.no-print` brief strip, so the employee's copy carries only its own facts; `monthPack()` returns `docs` **and** `refused` with a `partial` flag — a printed pack is never silently a subset, and an off-roster person gets the engine's own sentence plus the note that the month row typed for them sits outside every total. The employer's own cost prints **beside** the salary, never inside it.
- **One document per print.** `#payslip-capture` is padded so the padding *is* the A4 margin under `@page { size: A4 portrait; margin: 15mm }`; one slip goes out through `downloadElementAsPdf` named by `payslipFilename(doc)`, the month through the new `downloadElementsAsPdf(sheetIds, …)` — one A4 PDF, a page per region, a region missing from the page skipped rather than the pack failing.

**Found and fixed on the way:** the suite's own first employer-legs assertion failed on an *assumption about the layout*, not the engine — `earnings_total` is not the engine's `gross` because loss of pay prints inside the deduction block, so `employer_cost − earnings_total` is not the legs (got 2,417, wanted −14,583); the fix was to derive the right identity (`employer_cost − net_pay = employer_legs_total + deduction_total − lop`) and `employer_legs_total` landed with it, so the employer block's own heading totals exactly the legs under it. The **suite-blind** one came from driving the page: a refused month's header subtitle was assembled out of the document fields, so it printed *"Gopal Iyer · E-21 · October 2026 · net payable ₹0.00"* with both buttons live — fixed with the `doc.printable` subtitle branch and `canPrint` / `printWhy`, then re-verified on the same screen with the buttons seen disabled and titled. 394 assertions could not see a defect in *which fields a branch chooses to print*: **when a page has a no-figures branch, drive that branch** (Rule 26's lesson, now Rule 33's closing note). Tooling note for the next browser pass: `history.pushState` + `popstate` does **not** drive Next.js navigation in the live page — use `navigate_page`.

**Method:** 394 assertions in `/tmp/payslip-test.mjs` (383 at the engine commit, +11 for the employer-legs heading and its identity) — every arithmetic tie above, the dash-versus-zero rule through `SlipLine` and the identifier lines, the four boundaries quoted by whole sentence, refusals carrying `why_not` with no blocks laid out, `payslipFor` / `offRosterReason`, `monthPack`'s `partial` with its refused list, `payslipFilename`, `periodWindow`, and the letterhead helpers; **the 435 payroll, 212 attendance and 95 shopping suites re-ran green beside them**. `tsc` 0; `eslint` 0 on all **eight** files the round touched (the union of both commits — `payslip.ts`, `payroll.ts`, `vault.ts`, `commands.ts`, `nav.ts`, `pdf-export.ts` and the two payroll pages); `npm run build` ✓ **64 routes (61 pages + 2 api)** with `○ /payroll/payslip` prerendered (the route table prints 65 rows because Next adds `_not-found`). **Live in the dev session:** one person's slip, the month view with its totals row and refused list, a dash slip, the refused-month gate seen disabled with its title, the letterhead editor opened and closed with *Do not save* (nothing written to the owner's register), the month PDF reporting *"4 slips written into one A4 PDF, a sheet to a person."*, the palette returning all three new actions for "payslip", console clean, and no page-level overflow at 375px.

**Ledger:** **64 routes / 49 libraries / 69 glyphs / no new key** — the new page is `/payroll/payslip` and the new library is `payslip.ts`; the nav entry reuses the **existing `printer` glyph**, so the registry stayed at 69 (re-counted: 70 lines with one of them the component's own `name: string` prop) and all seven `icon=` values the page uses resolve in it. Vault specs stay **31, 26 restorable**, the payroll spec's `what` line now reading *"the salary structure of each employee, the letterhead a payslip prints under, and the month rows behind a slip…"*. `payroll.ts` grew 2,564 → **2,685** and the payroll page 1,731 → **1,736**; `pdf-export.ts` 50 → **88**; `payslip.ts` stands at **463** and its page at **892**. The payslip page's token audit: **0 hex**, **91 `var()`s over 17 tokens** all resolving against `:root`, one shared `rgba` (the modal scrim); the sheet is a fixed artefact inside a fluid page — a 794px `#payslip-capture` inside an `.ps-scroll` scroller, its money columns collapsing at a 720px break rather than the house 768 because the sheet, not the viewport, is what runs out of room.

## Session — R2-34: the day book is kept — one mark per person per day (2026-10-10)

**What was built:** two commits — `4e2c6f8` (the engine half: `src/lib/attendance.ts` **new at 547 lines at commit, standing at 557**, plus 160 lines into `src/lib/payroll.ts` — the `attendance` array on the store, `DayMark` / `parseDayMarks` / `PAYROLL_VERSION`-safe parsing, `canonicalMark` / `markMeaning` / `DAY_BOOK_MARKS` / `DAY_MARK_CYCLE`, and the `attendanceTemplate(store, month, grid = false)` variant that rules a date-headed page) and `784a1b8` (the page half: the **Day book** tab in `src/app/(app)/payroll/page.tsx`, 1,736 → **2,270** lines, the `.pb-*` CSS, the `kind`-partition refinement in `attendance.ts`, one ⌘K action (`action:payroll-daybook` → `/payroll?tab=daybook`, wearing the existing `book` glyph) in `src/lib/commands.ts`, and the vault's payroll spec grown in `src/lib/vault.ts`) — recorded as an **amendment to ADR-034**, not a new ADR: the register's doctrine is that it stores *facts*, and a mark is a fact where a wage is a computation. The round was taken from `7-Competitor-Comparison.md` §4's Payroll row again — its last unread feature: every competitor keeps attendance as a *record*; this app read a paste for one figure and kept nothing, so the days themselves now have somewhere to live. **No new key, no new route, no new spec, no version bump** — the day book lives inside `taxflow:payroll:<orgId>`, the vault stays at **31 specs / 26 restorable** with only the payroll spec's `what` / `count` / `detail` / `verify` lines grown, and `PAYROLL_VERSION` stays 1.

**Why it took the shape it took:**
- **Marks are evidence; one deliberate pull is the only money.** The tab's doors split cleanly: a tap (`markDay()`), a pasted page (`marksFromPlan()` → `writeSheetMarks()`), an eraser (`clearMonthMarks()`), and the three read-only exits (the ruled template, the day-book CSV) around the pull (`pullMonthFromBook()`). Only the pull touches `lop_days`, it re-runs the same `validateInput()` the month form runs, and every other write stores a mark that carries no rupee. The doctrine is returned as data and printed: *"Unwritten days are not absent days. The book counts only what the shop wrote."*
- **A refusal is data too — carry the `kind`, never parse the sentence.** `AttendanceRefusal.kind: 'identity' | 'scale' | 'mark' | 'blank' | 'figure'` exists because the bridge must split the reader's refusals by *what they are*: a row with no marks (`blank`) is noted and the person left exactly as they stand, and anything else — a stranger, a shared code, an unreadable mark, a day that never happened — stops the whole page, because a book with a silent gap in it looks complete. This was found the hard way: the bridge's first shape inherited the reader's blank rows *as refusals* and refused legitimate pages, and the vault round-trip's sheet fixtures then required the `kind` anyway.
- **Untouched, not zeroed.** A person the book has no days for keeps their month row exactly as it stands — counting absence from silence is the exact misreading R2-32 refused for the sheet, and the pull's receipt says it in words (*"left alone rather than zeroed"*). An **empty book refuses to pull at all**: *"An empty book is not a month of absents — nothing is pulled, and nothing is deducted."*
- **What the book finds is printed, never inferred into the count.** Marks kept outside a person's roll window (named, counted nowhere), a weekly-off day marked at a loss (the count reads what was written), a day count that disagrees with the month row (the month row is what the salary uses — *pull the book, or correct it by hand*), and people with no day written named as exactly that, with the book leaving their rows as they stand.
- **The write is to-date, and pasted marks say where they came from.** `source: 'hand' | 'sheet'` letters a sheet-written cell dotted on the grid so it reads differently from a tapped one; a mark re-written replaces the earlier one; an empty mark is a *clear* — legal anywhere, because a mistake should be erasable rather than permanent — and `clearMonthMarks()` erases a whole person-month for a page ruled for the wrong month.

**Found and fixed on the way:** the bridge's blank-row refusal (above) was the engine defect — caught by the vault round-trip and the 212-assertion attendance suite's own needles; and the ruled month measured **1289px inside a 1130px column** at 1440px until the day tracks became `minmax(20px, 28px)` with `width: 100%` cells and a `calc()` `min-width` — re-measured **1130/1130 with document overflow 0**, scrolling inside its own `.pb-scroll` frame only below the 20px floor, where a month of days genuinely cannot fit (a cell at 20px is still a 20×27px tap target). One live-session hazard was recorded rather than fixed: a pull stamps a provenance note onto the owner's real month row, so the verification pass restored the pre-click raw snapshot and used the app's own eraser to return `attendance` to zero — **drive the pull, then put the register back**.

**Method:** 247 assertions in `/tmp/daybook-test.mjs` (the mark table and `canonicalMark`'s spellings, `markDay`'s eight refusals with their sentences, the clear-anywhere rule, `dayBook`'s columns/rows/findings/footer over roll windows, weekly-offs and future days, the `kind` partition through `marksFromPlan` with a blank row noted and a stranger stopping the page, `writeSheetMarks` refusing whole on any refusal, the pull's written/unchanged/untouched/refused arithmetic through `validateInput`, the empty-book refusal, the provenance note replaced rather than stacked, `attendanceTemplate`'s grid shape, `dayBookCsv`, `clearMonthMarks`, and `dayMarksFromEnvelope`) — the 435 payroll, 212 attendance, 394 payslip and 95 shopping suites re-ran green beside them. `tsc` 0; `eslint` 0 on all five files the round touched; `npm run build` ✓ **64 routes (61 pages + 2 api)** with `○ /payroll` prerendered — no route added. **Live in the dev session:** the tab was driven end to end — a tap cycled through the whole `DAY_MARK_CYCLE` (unwritten → P → A → H → W → PL → SL → 0.5 → unwritten), a pasted date-ruled page previewed and written, the pull counted the marks and refused the empty book, the eraser cleared a person-month, the ruled template downloaded, and the weekly-off setting shaded the right columns; console clean; width pass at **355 / 1024 / 1440px** with the grid measured 1130/1130 inside the page column at 1440.

**Ledger:** **64 routes / 50 libraries / 69 glyphs / no new key** — the new library is `attendance.ts` (the app's **fiftieth**), the tab and the ⌘K entry reuse the existing `book` glyph so the registry stands at **69** (re-counted: 70 lines, one the component's own `name: string` prop), and no route moved. Vault specs stay **31, 26 restorable**, the payroll spec's `what` / `count` / `detail` / `verify` lines now naming the day book. `attendance.ts` stands at **557** lines, `payroll.ts` 2,685 → **2,820**, and the payroll page 1,736 → **2,270**. The payroll page's token audit was re-run: **0 hex**, **177 `var()`s over the same 18 tokens** all resolving against `:root`, one shared `rgba` (the modal scrim) — the day-book grid added the last 44 since R2-32, and its written-day cells take their edges from `color-mix(in srgb, var(--green) 26%, white)` and a red twin rather than literals.

## Session — R2-35: a draw names its numbered units — serials issued into a run (2026-10-10)

**What was built:** two commits — `b62a0e1` (the engine half: `src/lib/bom.ts` 2,202 → **2,453** lines — `SerialPick` and `BuildLine.serials`, `allocateSerials()`, `validateSerialIssues()`, `serialWritesFor()` / `reverseSerialWritesFor()`, `serialMovedOn()`, `serialTrail()`, and the *Units drawn* column in `BUILD_CSV_HEADERS` — plus `src/lib/serials.ts` 496 → **534**: `issued` as an event kind, a status, a label (*In a build*, amber), a state-machine node (`in_stock → issued → restocked | scrapped`) and a sort tier that keeps issued units after the shelf and before history, and `shelfUnitsOf()`) and `4baf1be` (the page half: the build modal in `src/app/(app)/inventory/recipes/page.tsx`, 2,778 → **3,027** lines — the `.rc-units-pick` chip strip, the `rc-warn` take line and the `.rc-note.is-hot` refusal that holds Save — and `src/app/(app)/inventory/serials/page.tsx`, 925 → **955** — the fifth stat card, the `issued` chip and view, the `In build JOB-…` where-line (*"cancelling the run puts it back"*) and the hand-take action; plus `SerialView.last_ref` and the serialevents spec's `what` line in `src/lib/vault.ts`) — the **fourth deepening of the recipes page** after R2-28–R2-30, taken again from `7-Competitor-Comparison.md` §4's manufacturing row rather than the ranked list (every ranked gap is closed or blocked). **No schema change, no new key, no new route, no new library and no new glyph**: the events ride in `taxflow:serialevents:`, the vault stays at **31 specs / 26 restorable**, and `BOM_VERSION` stays 1.

**Why it took the shape it took:**
- **The lot rule, in unit terms.** A lot answers *how many*; a serial answers *which one*. `unitIds` absent follows the shelf's oldest-first proposal (`allocateSerials()`), `[]` names none *deliberately*, a list names exactly those units — and the library, not the UI, has the last word: `validateSerialIssues()` refuses a set with a unit not on the register, a unit named twice, another part's unit, a unit off the shelf (*In a build* included), or a count that fails to cover the drawn quantity to the unit: *"The units on … cover 2 of the 3 drawn — the rest names no unit. Issue the whole draw or none of it."* An unnamed draw is legal and never blocks — only a line that *picks* is checked — and a fractional draw is told it cannot name whole units.
- **The picks are a copy of a decision, not a balance.** The modal's stamped set faces `validateSerialIssues()` against `taxflow:serials:` + `taxflow:serialevents:` read fresh that moment, and a plan recorded off the run list re-proposes both its lots and its units off registers read now — never trusting a promise made on the day the plan was written. ADR-031's discipline, applied in identity terms.
- **The run writes through the register's own door, and a cancel turns the verb round.** `serialWritesFor()` emits one `issued` event per unit, the job riding as `doc_ref` (*"Build JOB-… — Chocolate TCB-001 issued into the run"*), appended through `serials.ts`'s `appendEvents()` — the append-only log, never a status edited from the recipes page. `reverseSerialWritesFor()` writes the `restocked` pair on cancel, and `serialMovedOn()` refuses the *whole* reversal, **by unit name**, when a unit left `issued` since — restocking a written-off unit would resurrect it, and restocking a hand-restocked one would claim the cancel did what a hand did.
- **The boundary prints where it always was.** The units a run *makes* are still numbered at the counter on `/inventory/serials` — a build knows a quantity and the register knows an identity — so the PRD's old *"no serial-number issuance into a build"* sentence was corrected rather than carried, and the register side grew a state rather than a page: a fifth card, a chip, a view, a where-line and a hand-take whose confirm names the tidy way back as cancelling the run.

**Found and fixed on the way:** a **latent save bug** — `saveBuild` rebuilt the lines it wrote from the candidate on *every* save, so editing the note of an already-moved run ran `validateSerialIssues()` / the lot re-stamp against it and stripped the run's stored lot draws; the fix pins the write to `asBuilt ? check.linesStamped : draftExisting?.moved ? draftExisting.lines : candidate.lines`. Driving note: the modal's chips, like the login form, only move React state through prototype-setter `evaluate_script` clicks — the coordinate `click` tool no-ops on them.

**Method:** 73 assertions in `/tmp/serialbuild-test.mjs` — the new vocabulary (`In a build` label and amber tone, `statusOf`, `canMove` through the state machine, the sort tiers), `shelfUnitsOf()` reading oldest-first and skipping taken units, `allocateSerials()`'s proposal / shortfall / fractional / quiet-untracked cases, `serialWritesFor` / `reverseSerialWritesFor` note text and verb-turn with the job as `doc_ref`, `serialTrail()`, every `validateSerialIssues()` refusal by whole sentence (unknown, wrong part, already in a build, sold, named twice, partial, over-covered, fractional), `serialMovedOn()`'s scrapped and hand-restocked guards, the view's quiet / named / noisy findings, the stored envelope (a repeated unit dropped rather than kept, order kept, a pre-R2-35 run still reads, an unknown version read as empty rather than guessed), and the 13-column build CSV with *Units drawn* at index 9 — with the **435 payroll, 212 attendance, 394 payslip, 247 daybook and 95 shopping** suites re-run green beside them. `tsc` 0; `eslint` 0 on the round's four touched files; `npm run build` ✓ **64 routes (61 pages + 2 api)**, no route moved. **Live in the dev session:** three units received (TCB-001/002/003), the modal proposing all three oldest-first, a chip toggled off producing the live red refusal with Save held, the reset to *"Oldest first — 3 units"*, the record flash *"JOB-2026-27-0003 recorded — 2 movements written to the stock ledger, 3 numbered units issued into the run."*, the log row's *"3 units issued — see which"* opening `issued TCB-001, TCB-002, TCB-003`, the serials page at *In a build 3* with the where-line and the hand-take action, the cancel flash *"…3 numbered units stand on the shelf again."*, and the register closed back at *In stock 3 / In a build 0* — with all six events (three `issued`, three `restocked`) read out of `taxflow:serialevents:` in localStorage, each carrying its job number and its exact sentence.

**Ledger:** **64 routes / 50 libraries / 69 glyphs / no new key** — the round added no route, no library and no glyph (re-counted: 70 lines matching the registry regex, one of them the component's own `name: string` prop → **69**; every `icon=` value the round uses — `bag` `box` `factory` `shield` `undo` `alert` — audited against `Icons.tsx` by script). Vault specs stay **31, 26 restorable**, the serialevents spec's `what` now reading *"…sold, issued into a build, returned, restocked, scrapped"*. `bom.ts` stands at **2,453**, `serials.ts` at **534**, the recipes page at **3,027** and the serials page at **955**. The diff's token audit: **0 hex**, **9 `var()`s over 6 tokens** (`--brand` `--brand-soft` `--ink` `--ink-2` `--line` `--surface`) — the chip strip is the recipes page's own scoped CSS, and the serials page's full audit was re-run too: **0 hex, 40 `var()`s over 17 tokens**, the two shared `rgba` recipes only.

## Session — R2-36: the costed hour inside a project — a timesheet row names whose hours they were (2026-10-10)

**What was built:** two commits — `9ec2387` (the engine half: `src/lib/projects.ts` 1,144 → **1,310** lines — `TimeEntry.employee_id`, the `HourCostRow` interface, `costHours()` joining entries to the register's rows by (person, month of the entry), `EntryCost` per entry, and the view's `labour_cost` / `costed_hours` / `uncosted_hours` / `unlinked_hours` / `after_labour` fold with the projects CSV's priced column) and `6ab4212` (the page half: `src/app/(app)/projects/page.tsx`, 1,365 → **1,458** lines — the person `select` in the logger with its market-rate preview, the *Cost* column, the amber and grey findings, the stat sub's ` · after named hours ₹…, partial` segment and the contribution card's after-labour row — plus two copy edits in `payroll/page.tsx` saying where the figure is read, and the vault's projects spec `what` line re-worded to name the link) — taken from the recorded remainder of ADR-030 ("what is left is the attendance-to-job link that would let a job name whose hours those were"), not from the ranked list (every ranked gap is closed or blocked). **No schema change, no new localStorage key, no new route, library or glyph** — `employee_id` is additive at `PROJECTS_VERSION = 1` (an old envelope reads typed names, which is what it recorded), the registry stays at **69** (70 regex lines, one of them the component's own `name: string` prop; the twelve `icon=` values the page uses — `download` `plus` `clock` `scale` `clipboard` `edit` `tag` `x` `invoice` `undo` `check` `arrow-right` — re-checked against `Icons.tsx` by script), and the vault stays at **31 specs / 26 restorable**.

**Why it took the shape it took:**
- **Rows cross the seam, code does not.** The library still imports no payroll code: the *page* calls `costPerHourFor(store, month)` — payroll's own reading, carrying its own `basis` and `why` — rebuilds each as a `HourCostRow` (the structural subset of payroll's `HourCost`; a refusal arrives as `cost_per_hour: null` with its reason), synthesizes a refusal row for anyone the register skips (`offRosterWhy()`, or *"Not on the payroll register."*), and hands the list to `costHours()`. The `BookInput` / `docsFor` precedent — the page fetches, the library computes — applied to wages, so a library test can build cost rows by hand.
- **The month is the entry's own.** A row is priced at `periodOf(entry.date)`: September's hour at September's figure, so the register corrected in October cannot silently re-price last quarter's work.
- **A person is picked or a name is typed.** `employee_id` is optional; `''` means typed-only and is never costed. The picker is a native `select` (the lot-select precedent R2-29 set): *"Typed name only — never costed"* first, a stale option naming a person no longer on the register, the roster sorted by name.
- **A refusal is a row, not a zero.** A linked hour the register cannot price for its own month keeps a `null` cost and the register's own sentence in an amber finding (with a `/payroll` link); a typed-only hour is a grey finding — *"4 h carry a typed name only, so no cost is read for them — pick the person from the payroll register and those hours are costed too."* The two kinds count apart (`uncosted_hours` / `unlinked_hours`), and neither stands in for a rupee.
- **The reading sits beside the number it may not change.** `after_labour = contribution − labour_cost` prints as *after named hours* in the stat sub and the contribution card, wearing *partial* while any hour is unpriced, under the card's own sentence *"Read them together, do not add them."* — and `contribution` is byte-identical whether a row names a person or not.

**Found and fixed on the way:** the suite's first cut reached for `round2` from `projects.ts`, which does not export it — it lives in `costcentres.ts`, the reader the two registers share; and an unused `periodLabel` import came off the page. Driving notes: the picker and the preview only move React state through prototype-setter script clicks (the same as every native control in this app), and the entry form correctly refused a date before the probe project's start — the validator was right, the date was moved.

**Method:** 89 assertions in `/tmp/projectcost-test.mjs` (what one logged day costs through every `costHours()` branch, the two findings, `after_labour` and the untouched contribution, the summary, the additive `employee_id` in the stored envelope with the version staying 1, the CSV shapes, and the integration — a real payroll envelope through save/load and `costPerHourFor()`) — the **435 payroll, 212 attendance, 394 payslip, 247 daybook, 95 shopping and 73 serialbuild** suites re-ran green beside them. `tsc` 0; `eslint` 0 on the round's four touched files; `npm run build` ✓ **64 routes (61 pages + 2 api)**, no route moved. **Live in the dev session:** a probe project ("R2-36 cost check") with three entries — 6 h naming a person previewed *"October 2026: this person's hour cost ₹146.21 — this row would read ₹877.26 at cost."* and costed; 3 h naming Gopal refused in amber (*"No cost this month, so no hour to price."*); 4 h typed-only found in grey; the stat sub read *"₹0 billed · ₹0 spent · 1 live project · after named hours ₹-877.26, partial"* (6 of 13 h costed — *partial* earned); the picker rendered the roster live (*"Lakshmi N · E-11"*, *"Meera Raghavan · E-04"*, *"Sabia Khurana · E-30"*, *"Shabbir Ahmed · E-07"*); and the probe was removed through the app afterwards. `/vault` read back *"Projects and time | 122 B | 0 projects · 0 time entrys"* — which exposed the vault `plural()` polish ("time entrys") now recorded as deferred in 5-Project-Tasks, the small fix kept for a later phase rather than a round.

**Ledger:** **64 routes / 50 libraries / 69 glyphs / no new key** — the round added no route, no library and no glyph. Vault specs stay **31, 26 restorable** — only the projects spec's `what` line was re-worded, the list not lengthened. `projects.ts` stands at **1,310** and the projects page at **1,458**. Token audit: the projects page **0 hex**, **118 `var()`s over 23 tokens** all resolving against `:root`, the one shared scrim `rgba(15,23,42,.45)`; the payroll page re-audited alongside — **0 hex, 177 `var()`s over 18 tokens**, the same scrim and no notice-close (its modals close on their own buttons).

## Session — R2-37: the timesheet draws hours from the kept day book — propose, tick, write through the owner's door (2026-10-10)

**What was built:** two commits — `86cc1ec` (the engine half: `src/lib/projects.ts` 1,310 → **1,467** lines — the `BookWorkDay` / `DrawnDay` / `HoursDraw` vocabulary and `drawHours()`, which reads a person-month of structural work-days against the project's own register and returns proposals, refusals and `ticks: null`; and `src/lib/attendance.ts` 557 → **596** lines — `markWork()`, the one reader mapping a mark's spelling to `{fraction: 1 | 0.5 | 0, label, why}`, so a mark learned once is read the same by the month pull and the job draw) and `629be0b` (the page half: `src/app/(app)/projects/page.tsx` 1,458 → **1,705** lines — the draw modal reading `dayBook()` + `markWork()` into page-side `BookWorkDay[]` rows, the `.pj-draw-*` grammar in the page's own scoped CSS, an EmptyState action and a bulk-bar ghost button opening it) — taken from the recorded remainder of ADR-030's R2-36 amendment ("the attendance-to-job link"), not from the ranked list (every ranked gap is closed or blocked). No schema change, no new localStorage key, no new route, library or glyph; `PROJECTS_VERSION = 1` and `PAYROLL_VERSION = 1` both unchanged. **ADR-030 amended a second time**, and the round earned a new development rule — **Rule 37** in 3-Development-Rules.md (another register's evidence is drawn, never copied; only the tick writes).

**Why it took the shape it took:**
- **An hour can be drawn, not invented.** The payroll register's own `hours_per_day` prices a present day; the half mark proposes half; the draw invents hours no more than the book invents days — and nothing is ever proposed for a day the shop did not write.
- **The mark crosses as data, not as an import.** The *page* reads `dayBook(payroll, month, day)` + `markWork()` and hands structural `BookWorkDay[]` rows (`{date, label, hours, why}`) into the pure `drawHours()`; `projects.ts` imports neither `attendance.ts` nor `payroll.ts` — the structural-subset discipline (third application, after `BookInput`/`docsFor` and `HourCostRow`) keeps every register testable alone while the seam joins them.
- **A proposal is not a row.** `DrawState.ticks` is `string[] | null` — null means "the draw's own proposal" (present and half days, proposed only when nothing already sits on that date); any change of project, person or month re-proposes back to null; only a ticked day becomes a row, and `applyDraw()` writes through `upsertEntries()` with every row re-run through `validateEntry` — a draw cannot write an entry the form would have refused. An empty tick set refuses in words: *"Tick at least one day — a draw with no day on it writes nothing."*
- **A refusal is a row with the register's own sentence, not a silence.** A lost day, a paid-not-worked day, an unwritten day and an unreadable mark each arrive as a `DrawnDay` with zero hours and the register's own `why` — *"A lost day — the register reads it as no work, so nothing is drawn from it."*, *"Paid, not worked — a holiday, weekly off or leave holds no hours for a job."*, *"Unwritten — an unwritten day is not a day worked, and the draw will not invent one."* — and an empty book refuses at the person level with the deep link that fixes it (`/payroll?tab=daybook&month=…`): *"The day book holds no mark for … An unwritten book draws nothing — mark the days at /payroll, or log hours by hand."* A day whose hours already sit on another job that date stays tickable but is never proposed, and a day already on this project is un-tickable with the reason printed.
- **The drawn hour is already a costed hour.** Every drawn row carries its `employee_id` and lands with a note (*"Drawn from the day book — 2026-10-05 marked P."*), so R2-36's `costHours()` reading picks it up the moment it is written — the join and the draw compound without a second step.

**Found and fixed on the way:** the empty-book refusal's first cut implied a person when the *month* was the problem — the two cases now read apart (*"No day of this person's book falls in October 2026 — there is no page to draw from."* for the month, the *no mark* sentence for the book); the page's first render reached for a `d.fraction` that `DrawnDay` does not carry (the row's work tint reads `d.hours > 0 ? ' is-work' : ''`); an unused `periodLabel` import on the page fell to ESLint; and a case mismatch in the suite's sentence assertion was the suite's bug, not the register's.

**Method:** 99 assertions in `/tmp/drawhours-test.mjs` (markWork's spelling table incl. the neutral and unreadable cases, `drawHours()`'s proposal / half / refusal / future-day / before-project-start / already-on-this-project / elsewhere-on-that-date branches by whole sentence, `ticks: null` semantics and the reset on a picker change, the drawn row's `employee_id` and note text, and the integration — a real payroll envelope's day book read through `markWork()` + `drawHours()`) — with the **435 payroll, 212 attendance, 394 payslip, 247 daybook, 95 shopping, 89 projectcost and 73 serialbuild** suites re-run green beside them: **1,644 assertions across 8 suites, 0 failing**. `tsc` 0; `eslint` 0 on the round's three touched files; `npm run build` ✓ **64 routes (61 pages + 2 api)**, no route moved. **Live in the dev session:** a seeded day book read back *"2 proposed — 12 h"* (a full day at 8 h and a half mark at 4 h), an elsewhere-on-that-date day warned and left unticked, a manual tick raising the sum to *"Draw 3 days — 20 h"*, the apply flash *"3 days — 20 h — drawn onto … from the day book."*, the three rows landing linked and noted with their billable value and their R2-36 cost already beside them (₹12,000 and ₹6,000 at the project's ₹1,500/h, ₹2,283 and ₹1,141 at Farhan's own October figure of ₹285.375/h), a reopen showing the same days *Already on this project* and un-tickable, the empty-book refusal printing its `/payroll?tab=daybook` deep link, the off-roster Meera Raghavan (E-04, left 2026-08-31) refused above all other states, console clean, zero horizontal overflow at 355 and 1440 — and the probe removed through the app until the store read back *0 projects · 0 hour rows · Days written 0 of the 155*, exactly as found.

**Ledger:** **64 routes / 50 libraries / 69 glyphs / no new key** — the round added no route, no library and no glyph (the page's thirteen `icon=` values — the twelve R2-36 audited plus `book` — re-checked against `Icons.tsx` by script). Vault specs stay **31, 26 restorable** — only the projects spec's `what` line was re-worded, the list not lengthened. `projects.ts` stands at **1,467**, `attendance.ts` at **596** and the projects page at **1,705**. Token audit: the projects page **0 hex**, **131 `var()`s over 23 tokens** all resolving against `:root`, the one shared scrim `rgba(15,23,42,.45)`.

## Session — R3-1: the audit trail and the period seal — the log numbers itself, the window closes only once it ended, and the attempt is the record (2026-10-11)

**What was built:** one round, one new key, one new library, one new page, one write-path guard. `src/lib/audit.ts` (**611 lines**, new) and `/audit` (**716 lines**, new) together answer the two questions the PDF review opened Round 3 with — *who changed what* and *can a closed period still be edited* — as evidence and nothing else: a `seq`-numbered trail `verifyChain()` walks by number so a gap, a duplicate or a reorder is named; a `month` or `FY` window `sealPeriod()` refuses to close until its last day has passed and refuses to overlap a seal already standing; `releaseSeal()` stamps `released_at` and demands a `release_reason` rather than deleting the row; and `sealGuard()` + `recordRefusal()` are the two calls the invoice form makes before and after it stops a save. Recorded as the **new ADR-035** and the **new Rule 38**. One new localStorage key, `taxflow:audit:<orgId>` at `AUDIT_VERSION = 1` (ADR-013), joining the vault the same round it was created (Rule 31): the `REGISTERS` list now walks **32 specs (27 restorable)**, up from 31/26.

**Why it took the shape it took:**
- **The trail has one writer and every row is numbered.** `append()` is the only function that touches `events`, and every event gains a monotonic `seq` counting from 1 through it — the shape a hand-edit leaves behind. `verifyChain()` reads by number, not order, so re-sorting the array is not a fix; the register says *"the trail is unbroken"* and never *"verified"*, because a checksum shows a row was touched but cannot say who. A signature is what the browser cannot offer and the copy refuses to imply.
- **A seal is only over a window that has ended.** `sealPeriod()` reads a month (`YYYY-MM` → first/last day) or a whole financial year (April → March, `FY 2025-26` labelled), and refuses the range whose last day is today or later (*"A period is sealed only once it has ended — <label> runs to <to>, and today is <today>. The last day has not passed."*). A month you are still inside cannot be sealed and called history. `monthSealability()` gives the picker the same split, so a shop is never offered a checkbox that would fail.
- **Releasing is stamping, not deleting.** `releaseSeal()` never removes the row — it stamps `released_at` and requires a reason; a silent unlock is exactly what an auditor looks for, so no reason means no release (*"Releasing a seal without a reason is a silent unlock — say why the period is being reopened."*), and a released seal stays visible forever on the seals tab wearing its own release date.
- **The lock is a door a save path asks, not a wall around the store.** `sealGuard(orgId, date)` is the one question `/invoices/new` calls before anything writes, and returns the register's own sentence so the page never invents one. When the answer is yes the form shows a red inline banner with a *View seals* deep link, refuses the save, and calls `recordRefusal()` so the *attempt* itself lands on the trail as a `refused` event with its seq continuing — a save that quietly did nothing still leaves evidence the shop tried. v1 wires the guard into **only `/invoices/new`**, and the page names that boundary on the seals tab rather than implying a lock that quietly covers some doors and not others; widening it is a wiring task on each remaining write path, not a change to the engine.
- **Evidence holds no rupee.** A seal is a date range and an event is a sentence, so the register carries no money — it is the fifth register under Rule 31 / ADR-013's stricter reading, beside the cost centre (a share), the project (measurements), the recipe (quantities and minutes) and the salary register (structure and exceptions). The four `/audit` stat cards render their counts as `String()` values so `StatCard`'s default `formatCompactINR` cannot turn a `0` into `₹0.00`; the modal's preview line reads *"a locked window"* where a rupee-shaped placeholder had crept in.

**Found and fixed by driving the page — the three user-facing truthfulness fixes the audit trail was too credibility-sensitive to carry forward:** the four `StatCard`s on `/audit` printed *"₹0.00 Standing seals / ₹0.00 Released / ₹0.00 Trail events / ₹0.00 Writes refused"* because they passed a raw `number` and the card formatted it as INR — the fix is `String(stats.active)` etc., a count that bypasses the card's own default. The seal modal's preview rail listed *"₹0 held"* as the third bullet where it should have named the lock itself — replaced with *"a locked window"*. And `recordRefusal`'s note read *"Refused a invoice write dated …"* — a template with a hole — until the engine grew an article-aware helper (`/^[aeiou]/i.test(target) ? 'an' : 'a'`) so the trail reads as prose. The engine test's own assertion had baked in the buggy string, so both were updated together — the test suite guarding a bug is a suite blind to what the page actually says.

**Method:** 122 assertions in `/tmp/audit-test.mjs` (empty / damaged / wrong-version read; `parseAudit`'s seal and event parsers and the seq-sorted read; every `sealPeriod` refusal — the period still running, the overlap with a standing seal, the unparseable shape, the unreadable today — and both seal kinds landing their own correct windows, incl. the FY-to-date case and the whole-year `FY 2025-26` range; `monthSealability`'s split; the release branch with its already-released and no-seal refusals and the empty-reason block; the `sealGuard` sentence naming actor and date; the `refused` event that lands with the seq continuing and the article-aware wording; the `recordNote` trim branch; and every shape `verifyChain` catches — a clean run, a gap, a duplicate, a reorder). `tsc` 0; `eslint` 0 on the round's touched files; `npm run build` ✓ **65 routes (62 pages + 2 api + the manifest)** — one new page, one new library, three new glyphs. **Live in the dev session** (the Supabase browser session persisted; no re-login was needed): a September 2026 month sealed on the owner's own register and seen tick the stats to *Standing seals 1 · Released 0 · Trail events 1 · Writes refused 0*; the seal row and its trail row read back out of `taxflow:audit:<orgId>`; the invoice form's date set inside the window and the red inline banner seen with its *View seals* deep link to `/audit?tab=seals`; the save refused with the register's own sentence (*"2026-09-15 falls inside a sealed period — September 2026, closed by <org> on 2026-10-11. Release the seal at /audit before editing a document dated there."*) and the *Write refused* event seen landing with the seq continuing (2 events, 1 refused); the reason-gated release seen keeping the seal as *Released* with the reason on its row rather than deleting it (3 events, 1 released, seal still in the file); the `/audit?tab=trail` and `/audit?tab=seals` deep-links opening the right tab on load; the two CSVs exported through a Blob capture (the 8-column trail sheet with its refused row and its article-aware note, the 11-column seal sheet with its release date and reason both present); the phone layout re-measured in a pinned 355px iframe with `documentElement.scrollWidth == clientWidth` at both widths; and every probe seal and event removed afterwards so the register came back to the state the shop left it in — the org's own audit key wiped to `{version,updatedAt,orgId,seals:[],events:[]}`.

**Ledger:** **65 routes / 51 libraries / 72 glyphs / one new key** — the round added one route (`/audit`), one library (`audit.ts`), three glyphs (`lock`, `unlock`, `chevron-left` — the *shape the app had not drawn*: none of `shield` / `file-check` / `calendar` / `clock` means *this window is closed to writes*), and one localStorage key. The registry line at `4-Design-System.md` §16 was re-counted by the same regex it names (`/^  '?([a-z0-9-]+)'?:\s/gm` → 73 lines, one of them the component's own `name: string` prop → **72 real glyphs**). Vault specs now stand at **32, 27 restorable** — one new spec in the *workpapers* group, `id: 'audit'`, with `write:` → `saveAudit` and `verify:` → `loadAudit` counting both seals and events. `audit.ts` at **611** and the `/audit` page at **716**; token audit on the page still **0 hex**, every `var()` resolving against `:root` with the one shared `rgba(15,23,42,.45)` scrim.

**Rule earned:** **Rule 38** in 3-Development-Rules.md — *A Closed Period Is Sealed, an Attempted Edit Is Logged, and the Trail Proves Itself*. Its six conventions: one writer, monotonic `seq`; sealing only after the window ends; releasing stamping, never deleting; the guard as a door the path asks; refusal reading as prose (article-aware); and evidence riding the vault, holding no rupee and filing nothing.

**Standing boundary the round deliberately did not cross:** the R3 order is set by the PDF review — **(1)** the audit trail + period lock, **(2)** business setup and accounting depth (configurable document series, branches, opening balances that feed `ledger.ts`), **(3)** the P0 backend (durable data, tenant isolation, membership RBAC — which needs the user's hand-managed Supabase schema and stays a boundary until that schema work is authorised). R3-1 closes step **(1)** and the R2 competitor-gap series stays paused until all three land. The vault's `plural()` bug — *"3 time entrys"* on the projects row — is deliberately left for a later phase per the standing "keep the small changes for later" directive.

## Session — R3-2: the branch & series register — a number's shape is the shop's, its counter is the register's, and a branch is a registration that stamps its name (2026-10-11)

**What was built:** one round, one new key, one new library, one new page, six write paths dressed, and a grown ledger. `src/lib/setup.ts` (**547 lines**, new) and `/business` (**663 lines**, new — two tabs, branches and the five document series) answer the review's second backlog step — *multiple GST registrations, branches, financial years and configurable document series* — plus the *opening balances that feed the ledger* its "proper double-entry" line implies. Recorded as the **new ADR-036** and the **new Rule 39**. One new localStorage key, `taxflow:setup:<orgId>` at `SETUP_VERSION = 1` (ADR-013), joining the vault the same round it was created (Rule 31): the `REGISTERS` list now walks **33 specs (28 restorable)**, up from 32/27, with `id: 'setup'` in the *settings* group.

**Why it took the shape it took:**
- **The register dresses; it does not count.** `composeNumber(fmt, n, fy)` puts an already-counted integer into a shape while the invoice counter stays the Supabase `invoice_sequences` row per org per FY and each order kind keeps its own high-water mark, so a mid-year shape change re-issues nothing and papers keep the shape they were born with. Because a kind can change prefix, `seriesHistory()` hands the read walk every shape it has worn and `usedNumber()`/`usedNumberAnyYear()` parse each — a running (year-less) series inherits the largest count its year-shaped family reached rather than restarting at 1. A *second* counter would be the drift every rupee-less register already refuses; dressing the printed shape is the reversible half.
- **One prefix, one series.** `validateSeries()` refuses a prefix another kind already wears — the counters are per kind, so a shared prefix would double-issue `TAX-2026-27-0001` and the shop would only learn when a customer held both papers.
- **A branch is a registration.** Its own 15-character GSTIN (the same shape rule `einvoice.ts` uses; the checksum digits honestly unverified without a GSP) and its state code must agree — *"a Tamil registration cannot carry a <state> branch. Place of supply and the CGST/SGST split read this pair."* — codes and GSTINs unique, the GSTIN pre-filling its state, the first branch the head by construction.
- **The head is held, not owned, and history does not go out of business.** `setHead()` demotes every other row in one write; the head cannot close or delete until handed over, and a branch still stamped on any invoice can only be **deactivated** — the `referenced` answer read from the `invoices` rows themselves (an `ilike` on `[branch CODE]`) and passed into `deleteBranch()` as an argument, so the refusal comes from the books and not the modal.
- **The stamp rides the notes, not the schema** (Rule 21 unbroken): `[branch CODE]` at the front of `notes`, replace-never-stack, the form's *Issuing branch* picker swapping the printed seller header, the print page reading it back with a **named fallback** when the register can no longer answer — while the statutory pages still sign with the primary registration, because this is what the paper prints, not what the government holds.
- **`/business` holds no rupee, and the opening stands on a stated day.** `ledger.ts`'s `OpeningBalances` grew additively (`bank`, `receivables`, `payables`, `as_of`) under `openingStood = !as_of || as_of <= asOf`, so a balance sheet drawn before the books opened shows the documents it has, not an opening it was not yet standing on; the honest limit is printed — a payment grows `cash` but cannot reduce an opening receivable.

**Found and settled by driving the page:** the glyph registry was re-run and **added none** — `/business` reuses `building`, so the count stays **72 real glyphs** (the regex catches 73 lines because the component's own `name: string` prop matches; the trap `4-Design-System.md` §16 documents). The Button primitive has **no `className` prop** — met on the Delete button and settled by wrapping it in a styled span, now recorded in the design doc's Button row. The page-scoped `<style>` convention (`.bu-*` beside the audit page's `.au-*`) means `globals.css` stays untouched; the token audit on `/business` is **0 hex** with only the two shared rgba recipes (the scrim and the notice-close) and 58 `var()` over 19 tokens.

**Method:** **131 assertions in `/tmp/setup-test.mjs`** (store parse and its version/empty/damaged guards; every `validateBranch` refusal — blank code, bad shape, duplicate code, the GSTIN/state disagreement, the state-length rule, the duplicate GSTIN, the 15-char shape, blank name, and the all-valid empty list; head-handover demotion, close-the-head and delete-the-head refusals; the referenced-branch delete refusal; `setHead`/`setBranchActive` on a stranger; every `validateSeries` rule with the prefix collision; `composeNumber` in both the with-FY and year-less shapes; `usedNumber`/`usedNumberAnyYear`/`seriesHistory` reads across worn shapes; the CSV row builders) with the audit suite's **122 re-run green** against the grown `ledger.ts` / `orderflow.ts` surfaces. `tsc` 0; `eslint` 0 new errors (HEAD-baseline copies proved every remaining quotation/invoice-page error pre-existing); `npm run build` ✓ **66 routes** (63 pages + 2 api + the manifest) with `/business` prerendered. **Live in the dev session:** branch CRUD with the GSTIN→state auto-fill and the auto-head seen; the series editor's live preview seen recompose before Save; the `TAX` and challan overrides seen dress `TAX-2026-27-0012` and `TST-2026-27-0001` while old papers kept their born shapes (the form preview read the dressed count, the invoice list still read `INV-…`, and reverting gave `INV-2026-27-0012` with the count never restarted); the collision refusal seen verbatim; the invoice form's seller swap to the Karnataka branch seen print its GSTIN/state/address with *(head)* named in the picker; the accounting opening round-trip seen tie out — **15,00,452 assets = 5,46,111 liabilities + 9,13,821 retained + 40,520 derived capital** — with the new sub-lines printed; the head-delete refusal seen open the editor with the engine's sentence; the invoice-list regression seen clean (no banner, primary GSTIN, no probe leak); the 355/1440 width pass with `scrollWidth == clientWidth`; console clean; and every probe reverted, the branch unheaded and deleted, the opening register back to zeros.

**Ledger:** **66 routes / 52 libraries / 72 glyphs / one new key** — the round added one route (`/business`), one library (`setup.ts`), **no** glyphs (the desk reuses `building`), and one localStorage key (`taxflow:setup:<orgId>`). The dressed shape was threaded into six call sites — `create-invoice.ts`, `orderflow.ts` (`peekNumber`/`takeNumber`/`appendDraft`/`raiseDrafts` now take an optional `SeriesFormat`), the challans page, the shared `OrderRegister`, the quotations page (its own numbers *and* the invoice its convert path raises) and the recipes materials board — with every counter left in place. Vault specs now stand at **33, 28 restorable** — one new spec in the *settings* group, `id: 'setup'`, shape-guarded `accepts:`, `write:` → `saveSetup`, `verify:` counting branches + overrides. `setup.ts` at **547** and the `/business` page at **663**.

**Rule earned:** **Rule 39** in 3-Development-Rules.md — *The Shape of a Number Is the Shop's; the Counter Belongs to the Register, and a Branch Stamps Its Name*. Its six conventions: the counter stays and only the dress changes; one prefix, one series; a branch is a registration with an agreeing state; the head is held not owned and a referenced branch deactivates rather than deletes; the stamp rides `notes` not the schema with a named fallback and statutory pages still signing primary; and no rupee on `/business` with the opening gated by `as_of`.

**Standing boundary the round deliberately did not cross:** R3-2 closes step **(2)** of the PDF review's order; step **(3)** — the P0 backend (durable data, tenant isolation, membership RBAC) — needs the user's hand-managed Supabase schema and stays a boundary until that schema work is authorised, so the R2 competitor-gap series stays paused. Two honest v1 limits, both printed on the page: the **stamp → read-back → delete-refusal chain is engine-tested (131 assertions), not lived on a stamped invoice** — the UI offers no invoice delete and no shop's books were dirtied with un-deletable probe paper; and credit/debit notes and purchase bills stay **typed by hand**, because the portal and the supplier number those, so they are deliberately outside `SERIES_KINDS`. The vault's `plural()` bug — *"3 time entrys"* on the projects row — remains deliberately deferred per the standing "keep the small changes for later" directive.

## Session — R3-2b: the manual journal — judgement written by hand, only where it balances, answered rather than erased, and asking the seal (2026-10-11)

**What was built:** one round, one new key, one new library, one new page, and a grown statements engine. `src/lib/journal.ts` (**1,135 lines**, new) and `/journal` (**1,335 lines**, new — two tabs, the register and the heads) answer the review's "proper double-entry" line by letting the shop state a movement no document raised, under three gates: it must **balance to the paisa** before it is posted, it is **answered by a mirror rather than edited**, and it **asks R3-1's seal before its legs are read**. `src/lib/ledger.ts` grew **296 → 450** to fold the postings into both derivations; `/accounting` **500 → 592**, `/audit` **716 → 725**, `src/lib/ask.ts` **1,227 → 1,261**, and `/ai/ask` ±7. Recorded as the **new ADR-037** and the **new Rule 40**. One new localStorage key, `taxflow:journal:<orgId>` at `JOURNAL_VERSION = 1` (ADR-013), joining the vault the same round (Rule 31): `REGISTERS` now walks **34 specs (29 restorable, 5 evidence-only)**, up from 33/28, with `id: 'journal'` in the *workpapers* group beside the audit trail and a `accepts:` that shape-guards heads and legs. Thirteen files, **+2,864 / −47** in one commit (`571e7bc`, pushed).

**Why it took the shape it took:**
- **The sign convention lives in exactly one table.** Each of the fifteen `StatementSlot`s carries one `SLOT_META` record with its `natural` side, its group, and which derivation reads it (`where: 'pl' | 'bs'`, `onto:` the ledger's own field name). A head declares its line, so a fold cannot get an income leg backwards and there is no second copy of the convention anywhere to drift — the classic double-entry failure mode.
- **Balance before posting, in paise.** `totals()` sums through `paise()` so a float tail cannot decide whether an entry balances, and `validateLegs(legs, store, posting)` carries every refusal as a sentence: fewer than two legs, a leg with no head, a leg naming a head that is gone, a leg on a **closed** head (only at posting — a draft may remember a head that has since closed), a ₹0 leg, a leg that rounds to nothing, a side that is neither debit nor credit, **one head on both sides of the same entry**, and the balance itself naming all three figures.
- **A posted entry is answered, not erased.** `writeEntry` refuses a non-draft, `deleteEntry` refuses a posted row (*"A delete would leave a number with no entry"*), and `reverseEntry()` re-summonses **every leg from the original with only its side flipped** — so a correction cannot drift from what it answers — with a required reason, a readable non-future date, and one answer per entry. `foldableEntries()` filters drafts only, so a reversed pair **still folds**, nets to nothing, and stays visible; `livePostings()` is the separate reading the stats use (`posted && !reverses`).
- **A balanced journal renames money and provably cannot move the derived plug.** Δplug = Σdebits − Σcredits, and a capital-named head gives the room back by name through `journalCapital`, so the unexplained remainder stays unexplained. The claim is **read off the rows the sheet is holding** rather than asserted: `plugProof()` walks the folded rows back to their sides through `SLOT_META[r.slot].natural` and prints debits / credits / residual on `/accounting`, with the same caveat in `/ai/ask` (Rule 33's *beside, never merged*).
- **The seal is the second door, and it is asked first.** `stopForSeal()` calls `sealGuard()` before the legs are validated — a sealed window is a statement about the date, not about the row being typed — and logs the attempt through `recordRefusal(target: 'journal')`, so an edit that never happened is still evidence. A *draft* dated inside a standing seal is refused on write too, because a draft dated there becomes a posting dated there. **(R3-5 collapsed those two calls into one `askSeal()` — `journal.ts` 1,135 → 1,139 — so the read and the trail entry are the same call, and this register is now labelled `its own engine` on `/audit`'s door list rather than counted as a page door.)**
- **The number is dressed here and counted nowhere else** (Rule 39 unbroken): `JR-<FY>-NNNN` comes from `/business`'s sixth series over this register's own high-water `seq` map, the walk reading every shape the kind has worn through `seriesHistory()`; `peekNumber()` allocates nothing so the composer can show a number without spending it; and a draft takes no number at all, so deleting one cannot punch a hole in a series an auditor reads.

**Found and settled by driving the page:** the glyph registry was re-run and **added one** — `journal`, a page with a T-account ruled on it (the vertical stroke meeting a crossbar so the leaf splits debit-left / credit-right), taking the registry from 72 → **73 real glyphs** (74 regex lines, the component's own `name: string` prop being the documented trap in `4-Design-System.md` §16); its three wearers were grepped rather than assumed (`nav.ts:91`, `commands.ts:682`, `accounting/page.tsx:258`), and the page's own ten `icon=` values were each re-checked against the registry. `/business`'s flash copy was about to print *"Journal entrys now wear JR…"* — the naive-plural bug the vault still carries — and was switched to `SERIES_KIND_LABEL[kind].plural`, which fixes that page's sentence without touching the deferred vault one. The `--mono` sweep found the register's figures using a token **defined nowhere** in `globals.css` (the app declares a mono stack once, inside `.tf-cmd-kbd` at line 755) so it rides its own fallback; recorded as a follow-up rather than silently reported as a clean 24-token page. And two claims carried from the build session were **falsified by reading the device store** at this docs pass (the entries and totals I had written down did not match `taxflow:journal:<orgId>`), which is what the re-derivation rule is for — the docs now quote the rows that are actually there.

**Method:** `tsc` 0; `eslint` 0 new (HEAD-baseline copies proved every remaining `no-explicit-any` error in `ask.ts` and `/ai/ask/page.tsx` pre-existing); `npm run build` ✓ **67 routes** (64 pages + 2 api + the manifest) with `/journal` prerendered. **393 assertions in `/tmp/journal-test.mjs`** (the store's parse/version/damaged guards, `validateHead`/`writeHead`/`addStarterHeads`/`setHeadActive`/`deleteHead` with its usage refusal, `headUsage`, every `validateLegs` and `validateEntry` refusal, `totals` in paise, `unresolvableLegs`, the draft → posted → reversed lifecycle through `writeEntry`/`postEntry`/`reverseEntry`/`deleteEntry` including the seal refusal and its trail event, the number walk across worn shapes and `peekNumber` allocating nothing, both CSV builders, `movements`/`expenseLines`/`slotTotal`/`headBalance`, and `plugProof`) and **250 in `/tmp/fold-test.mjs`** (the fold through both derivations, walking **every pair of the fifteen statement lines** so the plug invariant is proven per pair rather than asserted once), with the audit suite's **122** and the setup suite's **131** re-run green against the grown `ledger.ts` — **896 across the four suites that exist in `/tmp`, 0 failing**. **Live in the dev session** on the owner's org (`ABC traders`, actor `nizam`, FY 2026-27): a draft dated inside the standing September 2026 seal seen refused with its `refused` event landing on the trail; **JR-2026-27-0001** (₹40,000 *Depreciation* debit / ₹40,000 *Accumulated depreciation* credit) seen posted and then answered by its mirror **0002** — narration *"Reversal of JR-2026-27-0001 — The rate was wrong — the shop uses 15%, not the figure first typed."* — and **0003** (₹2,500 *Bank charges* debit / ₹2,500 *Loan received* credit — *"Till cash banked, and the loan instalment set aside."*) seen standing, all three read back from `taxflow:journal:<orgId>` with `seq {"journal:2026-27": 3}` and eight open heads; the three foldable postings sum to ₹82,500 on each side, which is the arithmetic `plugProof()` prints and why the derived capital line did not move. **The phone pass could not be measured** — the in-app surface reported `window.innerWidth === 0` and the session had signed out, so no width was read and `/accounting`'s live figures could not be re-measured; what stands in for it is a static CSS audit (0 hex; 145 `var()` over 24 token names, **23 resolving against `:root`** and `--mono` defined nowhere; 2 `rgba`, both shared recipes; 89 distinct `.jn-*` class names; breakpoints 1100/980/768, where the modal becomes the house bottom sheet — `align-items: flex-end`, top-rounded 16px, `max-height: 94vh`, full-width stacked save buttons) plus an `evaluate_script` DOM drive through React's own handlers. A static check is not a measured pass and is not recorded as one.

**Ledger:** **67 routes / 53 libraries / 73 glyphs / one new key** — the round added one route (`/journal`), one library (`journal.ts`), **one** glyph, and one localStorage key (`taxflow:journal:<orgId>`). Vault specs stand at **34, 29 restorable, 5 evidence-only** (`outbox`, `ewb`, `tally`, `palette-recents`, `compliance-profile`). Surfaces touched: `ledger.ts` 296 → 450, `journal.ts` 1,135 (new), `/journal/page.tsx` 1,335 (new), `/accounting/page.tsx` 500 → 592, `/audit/page.tsx` 716 → 725, `ask.ts` 1,227 → 1,261, `vault.ts` 1,374 → 1,397, `setup.ts` ±6, `business/page.tsx` ±6, `Icons.tsx` 373 → 380, `nav.ts` +1, `commands.ts` +20, `/ai/ask/page.tsx` ±7.

**Rule earned:** **Rule 40** in 3-Development-Rules.md — *Judgement May Be Written By Hand Only Where It Balances, Is Answered Rather Than Erased, and Asks the Seal* — with its three conditions (balances to the paisa before a statement reads it, a mistake answered by a mirror rather than an edit, the seal asked before the legs are read), its paise-only arithmetic, its one-table sign convention, and its boundary: a register that holds rupees as *stated movements on named lines* is allowed to exist precisely because it files nothing, settles nothing and reaches no bank.

**Standing boundary the round deliberately did not cross:** the journal **files nothing** — no return, portal or GST workpaper reads a journal row — it **settles no bill**, it **does not reach the bank**, and it grows **no chart of accounts, no account codes and no trial balance**; those four facts are printed on the page rather than implied by a form that looks like accounting software. It is also the app's **one deliberate exception** to "money records are never stored here", stated in `2-System-Architecture.md` §12 rather than smuggled past it. Two limits named rather than fixed: **the round's rows are left behind on purpose** — a posted entry is answered not deleted and the trail only grows, so September 2026 still stands sealed (1 sealed, 2 refused) and the three postings above are still on the register; this is the first round whose demo data is permanent by design, not a probe that was forgotten. And the seal still guards **two doors** (`/invoices/new`, `/journal`) — bills, notes, payments and expenses save on their own paths, and `/audit` now derives its own coverage sentence from `GUARDED` so the copy can never outrun the wiring. **R3-5 closed this line** (ADR-039): the question moved into `create-invoice.ts`, `create-bill.ts` and `creditnote.ts`, so twelve paths inherit it, six more are carried by their page, and `/audit` prints where each of its twenty-one rows asks. Follow-ups: define `--mono` or reuse the app's single mono recipe; the vault's `plural()` *"3 time entrys"* stays deferred. **Both were closed at R3-5** — `--mono` is a `:root` token now and `.jn-mono` reads it without its fallback, and `plural()` learned `ies`, so the projects row reads *"3 time entries"*. Next is **R3-3 — the P0 backend** (durable data, tenant isolation, membership RBAC), which needs the user's hand-managed Supabase schema and stays a boundary until that work is authorised.

## Session — R3-4: the filings register — a due date copied then audited, a late fee read off its slab, a correction dated against the window that still allows it, and a seal read rather than asked (2026-10-11)

**What was built:** one round, one new key, one new library, one new page, and two corrections in an engine that already existed. `src/lib/filings.ts` (**2,279 lines**, 118 exports, new) and `/gst/filings` (**2,079 lines**, new — three tabs: the returns register, the amendment board, the declared-vs-books variance) answer the review's *"filing-status history, amendment tracking"* half — the two things a compliance calendar and a GSTR-9 workpaper could not say between them: *when did each period actually go in, was it late, what did it state*, and *what is still open about the paper that missed its window*. Six forms, three cadences, three filing statuses, five amendment kinds, nine row states, one key (`taxflow:filings:<orgId>` at `FILINGS_VERSION = 1`). **A row is a transcript, not a posting** — the rupees on it are what the shop says the return stated, they are stored rather than recomputed, and `ledger.ts` reads none of them.

**Why it took the shape it took:**
- **Copied, then audited, then printed where the two disagree.** `due` rides the row from `compliance.ts` so a filed history cannot re-date itself when the calendar's rule text is later corrected, and `statutoryDue()` re-derives the day from the rule (20th under 61(1)(i); 22nd/24th under 61(1)(ii) by the principal place of business's state code; 18th under 62(1)(i); 30 June under the proviso to 62(1)(ii) from FY 2024-25; 31 December for CMP-08 and for the annual return). `expectedRows()` prints **only** the disagreements: an agreeing cited rule is silent, a cited rule with no derivable date says the calendar cannot be checked yet, and an uncited rule names both dates. Rule 29's *finding, not correction* — the register never overwrites the date it was shown.
- **The QRMP fold is done and then disclosed.** The calendar emits a *monthly* summary row even for a quarterly filer, so twelve become the four quarters rule 61(1)(ii) actually speaks of, and `calendarObjections()` prints the fold rather than presenting four rows as if the calendar had made them. Folding is what exposed two bugs the suite then pinned: a quarter key must name the **financial year**, not the calendar year its last month falls in (Q4 of FY 2026-27 is Jan–Mar 2027 — `quarterLabel()` was reading *"Q4 2027-28"*), and GSTR-4's annual return moved to **30 June** under the proviso to rule 62(1)(ii) from FY 2024-25 while 30 April stays correct for an earlier year.
- **A slab, not a headline.** `estimateLateFee()` picks the ceiling from the shop's own preceding-year turnover band (₹2,000 / ₹5,000 / ₹10,000 under Notifications 19/20/21/2021-Central Tax dated 01.06.2021, ₹500 on a nil return) or GSTR-9's own table (₹50/₹100/₹200 a day capped at 0.04% / 0.04% / 0.50% of the turnover *in that State* under Notification 07/2023 dated 31.03.2023), and returns the `basis` sentence **with the notification inside it**, so `/gst/calendar`'s ₹5,000 card can print its headline *beside* the slab instead of swallowing it. The composition ceilings are labelled *"this register's reading"* — they could not be verified from a dated source this round, and the register says so rather than citing a notification it has not read.
- **Three clocks, and a fourth that is not a clock but a bar.** `windowsFor()` computes the proviso-to-59(1) door (shut by that period's own 3B, so the date printed is the 3B's), Table 9A (shut by GSTR-9 actually being filed, reading the row's *stated* due date when the shop replaced it), and section 16(4)'s credit deadline as reproduced in Circular 237/31/2024-GST dated 15.10.2024; `amendmentBoard()` reads each item against its kind's clock and prints **lapsed** when every window has shut. Separately, `consequencesFor()` quotes the bars the rules themselves enforce — 59(6)(a), 59(6)(b) for a quarterly filer's IFF (with the 2024 amendment recorded where the two readings differ), 59(6)(d) against an unpaid or unanswered 88C(1) intimation — and names the row that has to go in first. The suite made an edge explicit that the first draft got wrong: **an empty register is not a clean one**; with no April 3B on file, the next May GSTR-1 is honestly barred.
- **₱1 is the same ₹1 the 9C workpaper uses.** `varianceFor()` compares the six heads a filed return states against the period's outward documents and names only heads past `RECON_TOLERANCE`, as **books minus stated**, with a nil row compared *as nothing* (so the year's books become the gap instead of the row being skipped), an unfiled period not compared, and an unrecorded row `unset` rather than zero. The tolerance is shared with `gstr9.ts` deliberately — the two pages read the same two sides and must not disagree about what a rupee means.
