- **The seal is read and handed, never asked.** The engine's own row builder reads `/audit`'s store through `sealForPeriod()` (R3-5 folded this register's second spelling, `sealOf()`, into it) and hangs `{ sealed, label, id }` on the row, so the page shows a sealed period as closed paper — a *sealed · <label>* line and a link to `/audit` instead of an edit — without ever calling the door; `sealFilingsPeriod()` hands the close to `/audit`'s own `sealPeriod()` and keeps its rules (a quarter is refused by naming the three months it would need, and the answer points at `/audit`); a status change appends one sentence to that same trail; `deleteFiling()` refuses while an **open** amendment still points at a filed period and journals an allowed removal in the trail's own words. Unlike `/journal` there is no door here, and the reason is on the row: a transcript moves no rupee on the statements.

**Found and settled at this pass:** the glyph registry was re-run and **added one** — `filing`, a return sheet with the day stamped across it — taking the registry from 73 → **74 real glyphs** (**75** regex lines; the component's own `name: string` prop is the documented trap). Its four wearers were grepped rather than assumed (`nav.ts:80`, `gst/page.tsx:517`, `commands.ts:702` and `:712`), and the page's **twenty** distinct `icon=` values across 43 uses were each re-checked against the registry — the first draft of the docs sentence said *nineteen* and had dropped `wallet`, which the at-risk card and its section title both wear. `compliance.ts`'s two date corrections were found by folding, not by guessing. The style audit caught a real deviation the build session shipped with: the page wore a **third** scrim recipe (`rgba(15, 23, 32, 0.42)`) and a modal shadow no sibling has (`0 24px 60px rgba(15, 23, 32, 0.28)`); both now use the house `rgba(15, 23, 42, 0.45)` (25 other pages) and `var(--shadow-lg)` (27 others), which is the reason a zero-hex count is not the same audit as a literal *checked against the shared ones*. The page's remaining colour is that one scrim and nothing else — it carries **no** notice-close hover literal, unlike most registers. Two more findings the docs pass made by reading rather than re-asserting: **the PMT-06 copy pointed at a page that does not hold it** — both the `NOT_TRACKED` row and the QRMP fold objection told a quarterly filer that the 25th-of-the-month deposit "lives on /gst/calendar", and `grep -n "PMT-06" src/lib/compliance.ts` returns **nothing**, the calendar emits no such row (the objection's own title already said *"the calendar does not carry that day"*, so the sentence contradicted the heading it sat under); both strings now state that the calendar does not carry it, which is the reason the register prints the warning itself. **And `sealOf()` is live only in the suite** — it is exported at `filings.ts:2112`, carries three assertions, and is never imported by the page, which gets its badge from `sealForDate()` inside the engine's own row builder at `:2045`. The engine therefore answers *"is this period sealed?"* in two places and the app calls one; recorded as a follow-up rather than smoothed over, because the honest description of the wiring is not the one a reader would guess from an exported helper. **R3-5 took that follow-up up**: `sealOf()` is gone, `sealForPeriod(audit, period)` stands on `audit.ts`'s `sealForDate()` as the one path from a period label to a seal, and `filings.ts` moved 2,279 → 2,282. Both fixes are copy and surface, not arithmetic: the suite was re-run after them and still reads **446, 0 failing**, with the other four suites beside it (1,342 total).

**Method:** `tsc` 0; `eslint` 0 new — the ten `no-explicit-any` errors this run reports are all in `/gst/gstr9/page.tsx`, at the **same count as HEAD** (10 before, 10 after; this round's diff there is JSX and CSS only), and the three `react-hooks/exhaustive-deps` warnings are the same pre-existing pair in `gst/page.tsx` and `gst/calendar/page.tsx`; `npm run build` ✓ compiled with `/gst/filings` prerendered, **75** route entries listed (**re-counted at the R3-5 pass: the build lists 68 rows — 64 page routes + 2 `ƒ /api/*` + `/manifest.webmanifest` + `/_not-found`, the root `page.tsx` being a `redirect('/login')` that earns no row — *that last clause was wrong; the R3-9 pass re-counted this round's log at **69 rows**, the root earning its own `○ /` row, and R3-6's correction stands*; no line of this build's output contains 75, so the figure this sentence carried was not a row count**), **67** route files on disk (65 `page.tsx` + 2 `api/route.ts`). **446 assertions in `/tmp/filings-test.mjs`** (the store's parse/version/damaged guards, `validateFiling` and every write refusal including a future `filed_on`, `statutoryDue` against each rule, the slabs against their notifications, the snapshot-then-audit drift rows, the QRMP fold and its year carry, `windowsFor()`'s three shutters, the 59(6) bars on a filled *and* an empty register, the board's urgencies and its `dangling` flag, `varianceFor()`'s tolerance and sign, the nine-state ladder read from different days, `deleteFiling`'s refusal and its journalled removal, the seal badge and the handed-over seal, `loadContext`, `NOT_TRACKED`/`BOUNDARY`, and all three CSV builders), with the four surviving suites re-run green at this pass — **393 + 250 + 122 + 131**, so **1,342 across the five suites in `/tmp`, 0 failing**. **Not verified live:** the browser session has no signed-in Supabase user (it redirects to `/login`, and the test account's password is deliberately not recorded anywhere), so no row was recorded, corrected, sealed or exported through the UI in a real org. The engine is suite-verified, the wiring is grep-verified, the phone pass is read off three `@media` queries (1180 / 1040 / 768 — the surface reports `innerWidth === 0`), and the live drive remains owed to the next session that has a signed-in browser.

**Ledger:** **67 route files / 54 libraries / 74 glyphs / one new key** — the round added one route (`/gst/filings`), one library (`filings.ts`), **one** glyph, and one localStorage key (`taxflow:filings:<orgId>`). Vault specs stand at **35, 30 restorable, 5 evidence-only** (`outbox`, `ewb`, `tally`, `palette-recents`, `compliance-profile`). Surfaces touched: `filings.ts` 2,279 (new), `/gst/filings/page.tsx` 2,079 (new), `compliance.ts` ±14 (the `quarterLabel(fyStart, index)` signature, the FY-correct CMP-08 and GSTR-1 quarter labels, and GSTR-4's 30 June from FY 2024-25), `vault.ts` +26 (the `filings` spec), `commands.ts` +20, `Icons.tsx` +10, `nav.ts` +1, and three cross-read notes — `/audit` (filings *reads* the seal, guards no door, and its status changes still land on the trail), `/gst/calendar` (the ₹5,000 headline points at the register's slab), `/gst/gstr9` (the 9C table's two sides are this variance's two sides) — plus a card and a link row on the GST hub.

**Rule earned:** **Rule 41** in 3-Development-Rules.md — *A Filing Register Transcribes What the Portal Says and Posts Nothing; It Reads the Seal, Never Guards a Door* — with its six conventions (snapshot the date then audit it and print the drift; a late fee is a slab that names its notification; an amendment lives inside a window and a shut window prints as lapsed; the variance names a head only past ₹1 and keeps the books-minus-stated sign; the seal is read, offered and handed but never asked as a door; transcribe, do not file, post or invent), and its boundary: a register allowed to hold figures it does not own, precisely because it owns none of them. Recorded alongside it as **ADR-038**.

**Standing boundary the round deliberately did not cross:** this register **is not the portal** — no GSP feed, no ARN verification (`reference` is stored exactly as written and nothing implies the portal agreed), **no filing and no payment**, and **no effect on the statements**; `NOT_TRACKED` names five things a shop might expect (the portal feed, the IFF, the PMT-06 deposit a QRMP filer owes by the 25th under rule 61(3), refunds and DRC notices/demands, the statement figures themselves) with the rule for each rather than a quiet omission. `compliance.ts` **still emitted** monthly rows for a quarterly filer and **no** PMT-06 row when this round shipped: the fold lived here, the emitter was unchanged, and teaching the calendar to emit quarters was the deferred half — **R3-6 closed that half** (ADR-040 / Rule 43: the emitter now writes four GSTR-1/GSTR-3B quarters, eight IFF windows on the thirteenth and eight PMT-06 deposits on the twenty-fifth for a QRMP filer, and the fold that remains is only of a period *key*). Also deferred and named: the seal still guards **two doors** (`/invoices/new`, `/journal`) and filings is deliberately not a third — **the first half closed at R3-5** (ADR-039 widened the door to twelve writers and six page paths); this register is still deliberately not a door, and that half stands; the 22nd/24th state-code table carries only the row-1 states this round could source (Karnataka, CGST circle Hyderabad) and prints *"cannot be checked yet"* where it has no derivation; and filings rows are not ⌘K record-search entities yet. Next is **R3-3 — the P0 backend** (durable data, tenant isolation, membership RBAC), which stays a boundary until the hand-managed Supabase schema is authorised.

## Session — R3-5: the seal moves onto the writers — one question in three forms, a refusal that cannot burn a number, and a coverage list the suite reads back from source (2026-10-12)

**What was built:** no new route, no new key, no new register, no new glyph — one round that moved an existing control to a lower altitude. `src/lib/audit.ts` (**611 → 673 lines**) turned the seal's single question into **three honest forms of it**: `sealGuard(orgId, date)` reads and returns the register's own sentence, `askSeal(orgId, {date, target, ref, actor})` reads **and** appends the attempt as a `refused` event, then hands back the guard or `null` so a caller's happy path stays one line, and `requireOpenSeal()` does both and **throws** `SealedWriteError` — a stable `name`, a `.sealed` flag, and `message` set to the guard's own `why`, because a shared writer has no screen of its own and must refuse upward. The throwing form went in as the **first statement** of the three shared money writers — `create-invoice.ts` (**118 → 132**), `create-bill.ts` (**69 → 81**), `creditnote.ts` (**116 → 132**) — above the sequence-number read, so **twelve paths inherit one door** (`/invoices/new`, `/pos`, `/recurring`, `/offline`, `/sales-orders`, `/purchase-orders`, `/challans`, `/tally`, `/ai/scan-bill`, `/bills`, `/credit-notes`, `/debit-notes`). Where no writer exists, the page that types straight into its table carries the door itself through `askSeal`: `/expenses`, `/payments`, `/quotations`, `/invoices/[id]` (the paid stamp and the cancel), `/bills` (the same two) and the scan page's *keep as an expense* half — six rows. `/journal`'s `stopForSeal()` moved from `sealGuard` + `recordRefusal` to one `askSeal` (`journal.ts` **1,135 → 1,139**) so its refusals reach the trail like everyone else's. `src/components/SealNotice.tsx` (**47 lines**, the tenth file in `src/components`) draws the banner from `sealGuard` **only**, on **eight pages**, over a new `.tf-notice` block in `globals.css`. `/audit` (**730 → 772**) became the ledger of the whole arrangement: `GUARDED` is now **21 rows each labelled by which hand writes** — `the writer` ×12, `its own engine` ×1, `this page` ×6, **2 open on purpose** — and every count in the printed coverage sentence is computed off that array. `filings.ts` (**2,279 → 2,282**) folded its dead second spelling, `sealOf()`, into `sealForPeriod()`. Codified as **Rule 42** and **ADR-039**.

**Why it took the shape it took:**
- **A control on a form governs only the forms thought of that day.** Before R3-5 the seal was asked on two of twenty-one dated paths and ten of the others saved through a writer that never been asked anything — an offline outbox replay and a POS sale both raised real invoices through `create-invoice.ts` while `/invoices/new` was the only page holding a door. Putting the question in the writer means the path decides, not the screen, and a page added next round is covered the moment it calls the same function.
- **The seal first, the sequence second.** The door sits above the read of `invoice_sequences` / the series counter, so a refused save cannot burn a number: a shop that tries to write into a closed month ends up with the same next-number it had before it tried. That ordering is the difference between a refusal and a hole in a statutory series.
- **One question, three forms, because three callers genuinely differ.** A banner is not an attempt, so it reads (`sealGuard`). A page that stops a save must leave evidence, so it reads-and-logs (`askSeal`). A library with no screen has nothing to render, so it throws (`requireOpenSeal`) and its caller decides whether that becomes a banner, an error slot or an `alert()`. **A disabled button is not one of the forms** — a greyed control is a UI hint, and the shop can still reach the same write through POS, outbox replay or an import.
- **The page that hosts the button keeps its own ask, deliberately.** `/invoices/new` and `/ai/scan-bill` now ask twice: once at the page so the banner says *sealed* before Save is pressed, once inside the writer so no path escapes. The double ask is not redundancy to be tidied — a form should warn before the press, and a writer must refuse after it.
- **The coverage list is checked, not typed.** `GUARDED` carries a `by` label per row and the audit suite **re-reads that array from source**, asserting each labelled row's page really mentions the seal, that every `href` resolves to a real `page.tsx`, and that at least one row stays open. The docs pass proved the arrangement was needed: the old list still pointed at a **`/bills/new` route that has never existed**, and `/journal` was labelled a page door when its engine has held the door since R3-2b.
- **Reading is not asking.** `SealNotice` never calls `askSeal`, and the write path is the only thing that appends. If a banner logged, the trail would fill with people who looked at a date — Rule 37's *the attempt is the evidence* only means anything while an attempt is the thing that produces a row.

**Found and settled at this pass:** every number here was re-measured rather than carried. The monospace cleanup the round opened is **bigger than the build notes claimed**: the docs first said *43 declarations*, while `grep -rn "font-family:[^;]*monospace" src` is **45 across 28 files**, plus the stacks written inline in a React `style={{ fontFamily }}` prop — this pass counted that half at **7** and said *52 spellings in 32 files*. **(R3-7's docs pass re-measured that second half: `grep -rn "fontFamily:[^,}]*monospace" src` returns **19** props across **11** files — 7 of them name `ui-monospace` and 12 are the bare `fontFamily: 'monospace'` an earlier grep did not match — so the honest figure is **64 spellings in 37 files**. The design system's §3 and the open-cleanup rows now carry the re-measured split.)** The *eight pages* that hang the banner is a `grep -rl SealNotice src/app` count, not an impression, and `/quotations` was found to be a **ninth door carried without a banner** — its convert path types an invoice straight into the table, asks `askSeal` before the number, and reports the stop in its own words. ADR-035's own Consequence line was found to be **false after this round** — it still said an outbox invoice and a POS sale bypass the seal, which stopped being true the moment the door moved into `create-invoice.ts` — and it now records the closure instead of the stale boundary; the same pass marked R3-2b's *the seal still guards two doors*, R3-4's `sealOf()` follow-up, and R3-2b's `--mono` / `plural()` follow-ups as closed rather than deleting them. The `*3 time entrys*` row R2-31 shipped finally reads *3 time entries* — `vault.ts`'s `plural()` learned that a consonant before the `y` turns it into `ies`.

**Method:** `tsc` **0** (re-run at this pass); `eslint` **measured against HEAD rather than asserted** — a throwaway worktree at `HEAD` with the same eighteen files linted on both sides returns **71 errors before and 71 after**, identical file-for-file (`quotations` 17, `bills` 10, `ai/scan-bill` / `credit-notes` / `debit-notes` 8 each, `invoices/new` and `invoices/[id]` 7 each, `create-invoice` 2, `creditnote` 3, `create-bill` 1 — all pre-existing `@typescript-eslint/no-explicit-any` from the repo's `as any[]` Supabase pattern), while the new `SealNotice.tsx` and the round's `audit.ts`, `journal.ts`, `filings.ts`, `vault.ts`, `/audit`, `/expenses` and `/payments` come back **0**. The worktree was removed after measuring. `npm run build` ✓ exit 0 with its **full log kept** so the route table could actually be counted: **68 rows** = 64 page routes + 2 `ƒ /api/*` + `/manifest.webmanifest` + `/_not-found`; the root `src/app/page.tsx` is a `redirect('/login')` and earns no row *(**disproved at the R3-9 pass**: this round's own build log shows the root earning an `○ /` row, and its table counting **69 rows** — 65 pages + 2 api + `manifest.ts` + `/_not-found` — which is why R3-6's "corrects the 68" note was the right call and this sentence is kept with its correction rather than deleted)*, so the honest figure is **67 route source files** (65 `page.tsx` + 2 `route.ts`, plus `manifest.ts`) read off disk — a build pipe'd through `tail` cannot produce either number, which is how R3-5's first draft came to quote one it had not measured. **1,392 assertions across the five suites in `/tmp`, 0 failing** — `/tmp/audit-test.mjs` **122 → 172** (+50: `sealGuard`'s sentence, `askSeal`'s logged refusal and its `null` on an open date, `requireOpenSeal` throwing for each of the three writers, `SealedWriteError`'s `message`/`name`/`.sealed`, a non-date passing so validation keeps its own error, and the new section that reads `/audit`'s `GUARDED` array **out of the page source** and checks it against the tree), `/tmp/filings-test.mjs` **446**, `/tmp/journal-test.mjs` **393**, `/tmp/fold-test.mjs` **250**, `/tmp/setup-test.mjs` **131** — the five re-run together at this pass. **NOT VERIFIED LIVE:** the browser session at `localhost:3000` has no signed-in Supabase user (it redirects to `/login`, and the test account's password is deliberately recorded nowhere), so **no banner, refusal or `refused` trail event was produced through the UI this round**. The doors are grep-verified against source, the suite reads the page's own array, and the live drive — seal a month, try to save an invoice from `/pos` and a bill from `/bills`, watch the banner and the trail row, then a payment on a page-carried door — is owed **together with R3-4's** to the next session that has a signed-in browser. This round added no breakpoint and no layout, so it claims nothing about the phone beyond what R3-4's three `@media` queries already claimed.

**Ledger:** **65 page files / 67 route files, 54 libraries, 10 component files, 74 glyphs, 35 vault specs (30 restorable, 5 evidence-only), one `localStorage` key fewer than a round usually adds — none.** `audit.ts` 673, `/audit` 772, `SealNotice.tsx` 47, `create-invoice.ts` 132, `create-bill.ts` 81, `creditnote.ts` 132, `journal.ts` 1,139, `filings.ts` 2,282, `globals.css` 1,008 (`:root` gained `--mono`, `.tf-notice` + `.tf-notice-link` beside it). Eight banner hosts (`invoices/new`, `invoices/[id]`, `bills`, `credit-notes`, `debit-notes`, `expenses`, `payments`, `ai/scan-bill`) and one banner-less page door (`quotations`). A wider door is a rule change, not a new register, which is why the counts that usually move after a build round did not move at all.

**Rule earned:** **Rule 42** in 3-Development-Rules.md — *A Seal Is a Door on the Writer, Not on the Page; the Page That Hosts the Button Inherits It* — with its conventions (put the question in the writer that raises the paper; ask it before the sequence number is spent; three forms and no fourth that is only a disabled button; let the page that hosts the button ask too, so the shop is warned before the press; keep the coverage list as data and let a suite read it back; one banner, one sentence, one red, drawn from the read and never from the log), recorded alongside it as **ADR-039**.

**Standing boundary the round deliberately did not cross:** **the door is in this app's writers**, so a row changed through the Supabase table directly, or by another client that never imports `audit.ts`, is not stopped — the same honesty the trail keeps about its checksum, and the reason **R3-3's durable backend** is where that boundary could actually move; no schema, no migration and no RLS policy was added (Rule 21 unbroken). Masters and GSTR-1 filing status stay open **on purpose** and print as open, because a record with no date and no rupee has no window to close. A seal never rewrites or erases paper already issued, and releasing one still keeps the row and demands a reason. Left open and named: **52 monospace stacks in 32 files** that the new `--mono` token does not yet reach; filings rows are still not ⌘K record-search entities; `compliance.ts` still emits monthly rows for a quarterly filer and no PMT-06 row; and the live drive for **both** R3-4 and R3-5 remains owed.

*(Two of those closed at R3-6: `compliance.ts`'s emitter now writes the four quarters, eight IFF windows and eight PMT-06 days for a QRMP filer — see ADR-040 / the R3-6 session below. The monospace stacks and the filings-rows-in-⌘K gap were left open, and the live drive for R3-4, R3-5 **and** R3-6 is still owed.)*

## Session — R3-6: the calendar learns its cadence — the emitter prints the period the rule names, one state table dates two screens, and every date, sum and citation is read from a primary text or struck out (2026-10-12)

**What was built:** no new route, no new key, no new register, no new glyph — one round that fixed what the calendar *prints* and then audited what the app had already *written* about the QRMP scheme. `src/lib/compliance.ts` (**313 → 407 lines**) changed a quarterly filer's year from twelve monthly summary rows to the **four** GSTR-1/GSTR-3B quarters rule 61(1)(ii) actually speaks of, and added the paper and money a quarter leaves behind — **eight** Invoice Furnishing Facility windows (the thirteenth, rule 59(2)) and **eight** FORM GST PMT-06 deposits (the twenty-fifth, rule 61(3)) per year; `profile.filing === 'monthly'` is now the only gate on the twentieth-day row. A `Profile.stateCode` arrived with them, and `qrmpCategory()` — `QRMP_TWENTY_SECOND` / `QRMP_TWENTY_FOURTH` keyed by state *name* against `INDIAN_STATES` — became the **one** function that places a shop in rule 61(1)(ii)'s two rows: `filings.ts` (**2,282 → 2,296 lines**, 117 exports) **deleted its own half-read copy** of that table and re-exports this one, so the code that dates a quarter and the register that audits the date cannot disagree. `/gst/calendar` (**520 → 593 lines**) gained the state selector that sets the day — QRMP-only, prefilled from the shop's primary `gst_registrations` row, filling a blank and never overwriting a chosen state — and a second line on the cost-of-slipping card stating that a deposit is not a return. `globals.css` gained one token, `--brand-line: #bfdbfe`. Codified as **Rule 43** and **ADR-040**.

**Why it took the shape it took:**
- **Fix the emitter, not the reader.** For two rounds the honest-looking answer lived downstream: the calendar printed a date the rule under which the shop files cannot produce, and `filings.ts` folded twelve into four and complained in print. Rule 29's *find-then-show* is a contract between two texts; here the app was simply stating the wrong period on the screen a shop reads first. Folding stays only for a period **key** (`eventPeriod()`), because a register keeps periods and a calendar prints dates.
- **One table, two readers.** Two copies of rule 61(1)(ii)'s two rows would drift into a register disputing its own calendar over a state neither had finished reading, so the emitter owns the table and the register imports the function.
- **A day a rule states is derived; a day a notification sets is labelled the app's reading.** The twentieth, twenty-second/twenty-fourth, eighteenth, twenty-fifth and the IFF thirteenth come from rule text; GSTR-1's eleventh and a quarter's thirteenth come from a notification table the app carries and models no shift for, and `statutoryDue()` now returns `cited: false` for a date it cannot name an instrument for.
- **A citation the app cannot produce is deleted, not kept in plausible shape.** This round's sweep read rule 61, rule 59, Notification No. 85/2020-Central Tax and Circular No. 143/13/2020-GST (both dated 10.11.2020) from primary texts and struck six figures the app had printed as authority — a *"six per cent"* deposit, notifications cited as **13/2025 and 14/2025 dated 17.02.2025** (that range's real pair is 17.09.2025 and covers other subjects), a **Circular dated 23.02.2020** with a *"two days prior to the due date for filing of GSTR-1"* reading nowhere in it, a **12% interest cap under "the second proviso to rule 61(3)"** (no such proviso; no 12% in the consolidated rules), the claim that rule 61(4) bars a top-up, and the **35% PMT-06 fixed sum cited to Circular 143 alone** when the notification that prescribes it is **No. 85/2020-Central Tax dated 10.11.2020** under section 148 read with the proviso to section 39(7). The IFF moved from the eleventh to the **thirteenth**, and the ₹50 lakh ceiling now reads as inter-State **and** intra-State B2B (rule 59(5)(a) names both). The suite pins a guard that **fails if any struck citation returns** (`/13\/2025|14\/2025|23\.02\.2020/`).
- **State the day you are sure of and the amount you do not have.** Rule 61(3) asks for *"the tax due under proviso to sub-section (7) of section 39"* by the twenty-fifth and states **no sum** — the notified fixed pre-fill is thirty-five per cent of the cash tax in the last 3B of the preceding quarter, prescribed by **Notification No. 85/2020-Central Tax dated 10.11.2020** under section 148 read with section 39(7), explained at para 6.1 of Circular No. 143/13/2020-GST of the same date; the alternative is self-assessment. So the calendar prints the day with confidence, the register prints which half is missing and why, and no rupee is guessed on either screen. Pre-computing the challan — folding the register's filed rows and the cash ledger into the amount — is named as the next round's work.
- **An unstated fact prints as a gap, never as a pass.** A QRMP shop whose state is not named gets the **earlier** of the two days (never later than the rule allows, a day early at worst), the row says so, and `/gst/filings` reports those periods as *cannot be checked* rather than confirming them.

**Method:** `tsc` **0**; `eslint` at the **HEAD baseline** on the touched files — the calendar's single `react-hooks/exhaustive-deps` warning was re-measured against a stashed HEAD and is present identically before the round, so 0 new; `npm run build` ✓ with `○ /gst/calendar` prerendered, and the route table re-counted row by row at **69 entries** over **67 route source files** (65 `page.tsx` + 2 `route.ts`), which **corrects the "68"** the R3-4 and R3-5 notes carried. **511 assertions in `/tmp/filings-test.mjs`** (446 → 511) now pin the sixteen monthly rows and the election in an untracked QRMP year, the rule-quoted strings, the thirteenth, the eighteen-per-annum interest figure, the 35% cited to **Notification No. 85/2020-Central Tax** rather than the Circular alone, and the citation guard, with the four other suites re-run unchanged — **511 + 393 + 250 + 172 + 131 = 1,457 across the five `/tmp` suites, 0 failing**. **NOT VERIFIED LIVE:** the browser session still has no signed-in Supabase user, so the state selector, the quarter rows and the deposit rows have not been driven through the UI; this round's live drive joins R3-4's and R3-5's as owed, and the responsive claims are read off the page's own CSS rather than measured.

**Ledger:** **65 page files / 67 route files, 54 libraries, 10 component files, 74 glyphs, 35 vault specs (30 restorable, 5 evidence-only)** — no new register, key, glyph or route, so Rule 31 stayed untriggered and the counts that usually move after a build round did not move. `compliance.ts` 407, `filings.ts` 2,296, `/gst/calendar` 593, `globals.css` +1 (`--brand-line`).

**Rule earned:** **Rule 43** in 3-Development-Rules.md — *A Calendar Emits the Period the Rule Names, and Every Day, Sum and Citation on It Is Read From a Primary Text or Labelled as the Shop's Own* — recorded alongside it as **ADR-040**.

**Standing boundary the round deliberately did not cross:** the calendar carries the twenty-fifth but **not its amount** — rule 61(3) leaves the sum to be notified, so a pre-computed challan needs the quarter's *cash* tax folded from the register's filed rows and the cash ledger, and that fold is the next round's work rather than a figure this one guesses. The register still keeps **no row** for the eight deposits and eight invoice windows the calendar now prints — neither is a return. Next is **R3-3 — the P0 backend** (durable data, tenant isolation, membership RBAC), which stays a boundary until the hand-managed Supabase schema is authorised; the live browser drive for R3-4, R3-5 and R3-6 is owed to the next signed-in session.

*(The first sentence closed at R3-7 — `depositFor()` folds the deposit's rupee on the calendar card itself, as a labelled ceiling or a ready challan; see **ADR-041** and the R3-7 session below. The register still keeps no row for the deposits and invoice windows, by the same reasoning, and the live drive for R3-4 through R3-7 is still the one owed thing.)*

---

## Session — R3-7: the calendar's deposit gets its rupee — one fold, two sources, three states, on the envelope the screen already owns (2026-10-14)

**What was built:** no new route, key, register, glyph or component — one round that finished the arithmetic R3-6 named and left. `src/lib/filings.ts` (**2,296 → 2,368 lines**) grows `DepositFigures` and `depositFor(store, statuses, supplyMonth)` with the notified share as data (`DEPOSIT_PCT = 35`): a May 2025 deposit reads `2024-Q4`, a January 2026 one reads `2025-Q3`, and anything that is not a month returns `null`. The fold has **two sources and three states** — `ready` (35% of the cash tax the shop stated for that quarter), `ceiling` (35% of what that quarter's filed GSTR-3B row *stated in total*), `none` (neither exists, so no rupee prints and the card quotes rule 61(3) and Notification No. 85/2020-Central Tax instead). `src/lib/compliance.ts` (**407 → 435**) widens `StatusMap` with an optional `cashTax` and puts the shop's figure on the calendar's **existing** `taxflow:compliance:<orgId>` envelope under `cash:<quarter>` — `cashTaxKey()` / `enteredCashTax()` / `withEnteredCashTax()` as the three helpers, `isCashTaxKey()` as the switch that keeps a sum out of the vault's filing count. `src/lib/vault.ts` (1,433 lines) changes three fields on the `compliance` spec (`what` names the stated cash tax, `unit` becomes *"kept row"*, `detail` splits the envelope's two kinds of key). `/gst/calendar` (**593 → 675**) loads the filings store **read-only** (`loadFilings(oid)`, nothing on this page saves to it), folds each of its own `PMT-06` rows through a `depositByEvent` memo keyed off the `pmt06-<YYYY-MM>` tail of the event id, prints the three states in the row's own card (`up to ₹…` in `--amber` beside the stated total for a ceiling), and puts one `type="number"` box on the same card that writes the shop's figure on `onBlur` with a `clear` beside it; `amountHint` is suppressed exactly when a deposit fold exists. Codified as **Rule 44** and **ADR-041**.

**Why it took the shape it took:**
- **The register does not hold the number the statute asks for, so it prints a ceiling.** Notification No. 85/2020-Central Tax dated 10.11.2020 (section 148 read with the proviso to section 39(7)) fixes the pre-fill at thirty-five per cent of the tax **paid in cash** in the last 3B of the preceding quarter. `FilingRecord.heads.tax` is what a return *stated in total* — the credit ledger is not in it — so 35% of that is only exact when none of the quarter's tax was met from input credit. R3-6's Rule 43 says a sum is read from a primary text or labelled as the shop's own; relabelling a stated total as a cash leg would break it from the other side, so the card says *up to*, *a ceiling*, and names the line it read.
- **The upgrade comes from the shop, not from a guess.** The figure that is genuinely the shop's own is their cash tax, entered once per quarter and stored on the calendar's envelope — keyed by **quarter**, not by deposit, because a quarter's two PMT-06 rows have the same base. Same arithmetic, different source, and the card states which produced it.
- **Fold the period that owns the money.** A deposit answers for one month and its base is the quarter *before* the quarter that month sits in: `prevPeriod(quarterOfMonth(...))`. That is also where the year-carrying R3-4 already learned bites — Q4 of FY 2024-25 labels `Q4 FY 2024-25` and a January deposit has to reach back into it, so the suite pins both.
- **One envelope, no new key — and a count that can be confused by a sum.** Storing under `cash:<quarter>` inside `taxflow:compliance:` kept Rule 31 untriggered (the vault is still 35 specs / 30 restorable), but that envelope is the *filings-tick* register the vault counts and restores, so `isCashTaxKey()` splits its two kinds of row: the spec's line now reads *"2 returns marked filed · 1 quarter with a stated cash tax"* rather than reporting a rupee figure as a filing. `count` and `verify` still see every key, so they stay consistent with each other.
- **Copy that deferred had to be re-read.** R3-6 left two sentences pointing at a future round — `UNTRACKED_CALENDAR_FORMS['PMT-06']` and the register's disclosure card. Both now describe the screen as it behaves: the card's title is *"The deposit the calendar now prints states a day it is sure of, and its amount is a ceiling until the cash tax is named"*, and no string in `src/` says "next round" any more. A disclosure that describes last round's app is a discrepancy the app has not found yet.
- **A bug this round's own test caught.** `withEnteredCashTax()` returned a new map but `delete`d `cashTax` off the *entry* it shared with the caller's map — clearing a figure rewrote the previous React state, and a `ready` deposit read back as a `ceiling` on the very next render. The fix builds a fresh entry (`{ doneOn, reference }`) and the suite pins the immutability directly.

**Method:** `tsc` **0**; `eslint` at the **HEAD baseline** on the touched files — this page's single pre-existing `react-hooks/exhaustive-deps` warning (on its load effect) re-measured against a stashed HEAD, 0 new; `npm run build` ✓ with `○ /gst/calendar` prerendered and the route table at **69 entries over 67 route source files** (65 `page.tsx` + 2 `route.ts`), unchanged from R3-6's correction. **547 assertions in `/tmp/filings-test.mjs`** (511 → 547) pin both sources and all three states, the year-carrying base quarter (`2024-Q4` → statedTax 72,000 → ceiling 25,200), Jan-2026 → `2025-Q3` → `none`, the `cash:2024-Q4` keying and the ready flip (₹14,000 from ₹40,000), clearing and immutability, `isCashTaxKey()` on both kinds of key, a nil quarter that still reads as a `ceiling` of 0, non-month refusals, `DEPOSIT_PCT === 35`, and the two rewritten register sentences — with the four other suites re-run unchanged: **547 + 393 + 250 + 172 + 131 = 1,493 across the five `/tmp` suites, 0 failing.** **NOT VERIFIED LIVE:** the browser session still has no signed-in Supabase user, so the deposit card, the number box and the ceiling/ready switch have not been driven through the UI; this round's drive joins R3-4's, R3-5's and R3-6's as owed, and the CSS reads are off the page's own `<style>` block rather than measured.

**Ledger:** **65 page files / 67 route files, 54 libraries, 10 component files, 74 glyphs, 35 vault specs (30 restorable, 5 evidence-only)** — re-counted at this pass, nothing moved. `compliance.ts` 435, `filings.ts` 2,368, `/gst/calendar` 675, `vault.ts` 1,433 with three fields changed on one spec. The deposit card added **no hex literal**: it is built from `--mono`, `--amber` and `--brand-line`, and it suppresses the row's own `amountHint` rather than printing a second rupee against the fold. The hex audit in this file's design section was two counts out (`ledger` listed 7 against 8 in the file, `gst/calendar` 4 against 3 — the fourth went to `--brand-line` at R3-6); corrected at this pass, with the rest of the listed counts re-measured and confirmed.

**Rule earned:** **Rule 44** in 3-Development-Rules.md — *A Sum on a Calendar Is Either the Shop's Own or a Labelled Ceiling, and a Fold Names the Line It Read* — with its conventions (two sources, three states, one card; fold the period that owns the money; one envelope and no new key, with a vault count that can tell a sum from a filing; copy that deferred must be re-read; never mutate the map you were handed; a ceiling is not a warning), recorded alongside it as **ADR-041**.

**Standing boundary the round deliberately did not cross:** **the app never reads the cash ledger** — there is no electronic-cash-leg row in this codebase, so a `ceiling` stays a ceiling until the shop states the number, and the card says *state*, never *this is what you owe*; the portal's own challan remains the record. No register row was added for the eight deposits or the eight IFF windows (neither is a return), no schema, migration or RLS policy was touched (Rule 21), and no new `localStorage` key was created (Rule 31 untriggered). Next is **R3-3 — the P0 backend** (durable data, tenant isolation, membership RBAC), still a boundary until the hand-managed Supabase schema is authorised; the live browser drive for R3-4, R3-5, R3-6 and R3-7 is owed to the next signed-in session. Named open cleanup, re-measured at this pass: **64 monospace spellings in 37 files** (45 class rules over 28 files, 19 inline `fontFamily` props over 11 — the docs had said *52 in 32* because the inline half had been grepped by the stack that names `ui-monospace` and not by the bare `fontFamily: 'monospace'`), the one-off tints listed above, and filings rows still absent from ⌘K record search.

## Session — R3-8: the Tally bridge learns two more vouchers — an import may raise a draft, never a posting, and it borrows the door of the register it lands in (2026-10-14)

**What was built:** no new route, key, register, glyph or component — one round that moved ADR-029's skip list by exactly one word. `src/lib/tally.ts` (**3,211 → 3,513 lines**) grows two `PlanTarget`s — `journal_draft` (*Journal draft*) and `challan_draft` (*Challan draft*), write order slots 7 and 8 — with `journalItem()` and `challanItem()` planning the two voucher kinds the bridge has listed as skipped since R2-22. The engine reads a file strictly one-way: a journal leg lands only on a **head that already stands on the journal register** (case-folded match, a closed head still lands with a flag, the import creates nothing), and the whole voucher must **balance across the legs the file writes** — a ₹0 line is dropped with a flag naming it, and a genuinely unbalanced voucher **blocks rather than being squared**; a **cash or bank leg blocks** with money's own sentence, because a journal is not a writer. A challan lands only against a **party and items the books already know** — an unknown name blocks with the missing word printed and the row becomes draftable only when the shop registers it — its lines are **untaxed** (tax inside the voucher is reported in `flags`, *the lines win*, and the rupees stay the lines' sum), the place of supply is derived exactly as an invoice's from the party's GSTIN, a party with no state is placed in the shop's own **only when its primary GSTIN names one** (flagged), and blocks when there is none to read; an unknown movement reason defaults to `other` with a read-before-confirm flag; a negative-quantity line — a *reverse* challan — blocks, because that is the other register's semantics. The draft row's own date counts into the plan's date range like any document's. `/tally` (**1,608 → 1,735 lines**, + one ⌘K keyword line) grows the two groups under the `journal`/`truck` glyphs, a three-column draft detail card (desktop and phone grids), the two write branches, the two log chips, and a header paragraph saying why a draft keeps all three of the bridge's rules at once. The writes are the registers' own calls, not the bridge's: `writeEntry` on the journal store — which asks the **period seal even for a draft** and burns **no number** (the JR number is taken at posting, `postEntry` reading the entry's own date) — and `appendDraft` on the challan store, so `DC-…` comes from that series' high-water mark in the shop's own series shape (R3-2's `resolveSeries` inherited whole). The import log grew two counters (`journal_drafts`, `challan_drafts`) **on the existing `taxflow:tally:<orgId>` envelope** — no new key, Rule 31 untriggered, the vault still 35 specs / 30 restorable. The sample dump gained the two vouchers it lacked — `J-51` (a rent journal that **blocks** on an untouched chart, the round's rule demoed on the demo file) and `DC-77` (5 × ₹1,200, **ready** at ₹6,000 into May 2025) — and now plans **ready 9 / blocked 2 / skipped 2 / needs-your-eye 2** over thirteen rows. Codified as **Rule 45** and **ADR-042**.

**Why it took the shape it took:**
- **One word, not an eraser.** The options were to make the import stronger — create the head, create the party, post the entry — or leave the two most-used Tally voucher kinds outside the app. Posting a journal from a file is a judgement the shop never made; skipping it forever was the honest-but-narrow answer. So the boundary moved exactly one word: **an import may raise a draft, never a posting**. A draft burns no number in a statutory series, reaches no return, cannot be read as money, and is refused by the seal like any other write.
- **R3-5's door came free.** Because the round called the register's own `writeEntry` instead of touching storage, the seal question the writers have asked since R3-5 is asked here too — verified in the suite: `sealPeriod(oid, {kind:'month', month:'2025-04'}, '2026-10-01')` then an imported draft dated 2025-04-30 is **refused with `sealed: true`**, and the register keeps exactly one posted entry. A parallel write path would have been a second door to forget.
- **Numbering is the register's, in the shop's shape.** The JR number arrives at posting and the DC number at raising, both off the high-water walk R3-2 built — the suite pins that a draft leaves `seq` untouched, that `peekNumber` equals the number posting actually mints (`JR-2025-26-0001`, `DC-2025-26-0001` → `0002`), and that a shop that dressed its challan series differently gets its own shape, not the bridge's.
- **What the row cannot carry is said.** A challan has no tax columns, so tax in the voucher prints in `flags` and the untaxed lines win; a movement reason the rule set does not know becomes `other` with the read-before-confirm sentence; a reverse (negative-quantity) challan blocks rather than silently restocking. Each of these was a temptation to average, spread, or flip — all refused.
- **Dedupe across import generations, one sentence per row.** A draft whose `Tally Journal #J-…` / `Tally Delivery Challan #DC-…` line already sits in a stored row's notes reads **already**, exactly as the note rows' reference match does since R2-27; and `skippedDocumentItem` still gives each skipped row **exactly one** sentence — generic, money, or order — which the suite pins (`reasons.length === 1`), the generic one now naming journals and delivery challans among the kinds the bridge *does* read.
- **The demo file caught the harness lying.** The Read-tool snapshot of `tally.ts` rendered the challan `tallyRef` **without** its `#`, sending two "already" fixtures chasing a needle the engine never prints; `awk` on the file proved the `#` is there. When Read output and runtime behaviour conflict, verify the file through the shell.

**Method:** **99 assertions in `/tmp/tally3-test.mjs`, 0 failing** — six sections: journal planning (empty-chart block naming the ledger, case-folded head match → ready legs, rupees = the debit, `#J-` in the narration, the draft sentences, one-leg/cash-leg/unbalanced/no-date blocks, ₹0 drop with flag, closed-head flag, already-match, lookalike same-day flag that stays ready); challan planning (ready at 6,000 untaxed, POS `07` derived, reason `other` with its flag, `appendDraft` derivation, unknown party/item blocks then the registered product satisfies, `expandSelection` carrying the requirement, no-state party → org state flagged / empty org state blocked, tax-reported-lines-win, negative-qty block, already); what stays skipped (order / money / generic — one sentence each); the sample dump (J-51 blocked, DC-77 ready, P-301 money sentence, draft dates in the range, the block printed in the plan CSV); the journal **write path** against a stubbed `window.localStorage` (heads written, draft ok with `number === ''`, high-water unchanged, replan reads `already`, `postEntry` mints the peeked `JR-2025-26-0001`, sealed April refused with `sealed: true`, exactly one posted entry, legs round-trip); and the challan **write path** (`appendDraft` → `DC-2025-26-0001` = peek → `0002`, stored as `draft`, replan `already`, fields keep `other` and `#DC-90`). `tsc` **0**; `eslint` **0 problems on the round's three changed files** (`tally.ts`, the page, `commands.ts`); `npm run build` ✓ with the route table unchanged at **69 build entries over 67 source files** (67 + `/_not-found` + `/manifest.webmanifest`). **The `/tmp` harness was rebuilt this round after the 2026-10-14 wipe:** `/tmp/jr-register.mjs` is a registrar that calls `module.register()` on `/tmp/jr-hooks.mjs`, an ESM resolve hook mapping `@/…` into `src/…` with `.ts/.tsx/index.ts` and extension-less relative fallbacks; importing the hook from the `--import` file does *not* install it — the split is mandatory. The **five** suites that last ran at R3-7 (filings 547, journal 393, fold 250, audit 172, setup 131) went unrecoverable with the wipe and were **not** re-run; their counts stand as recorded in the R3-7 entry, and this round's figure is stated alone.

**Ledger:** **65 page files / 67 route files, 54 libraries, 10 component files, 74 glyphs, 35 vault specs (30 restorable, 5 evidence-only)** — re-counted at this pass (`find src/app -name page.tsx | wc -l` = 65, `route.ts` = 2, `ls src/lib/*.ts` = 54), all exactly where R3-7 left them: no new route, key, glyph, component or register. The build's route listing re-counted at **69 entries over 67 source files**, unchanged.

**Rule earned:** **Rule 45** in 3-Development-Rules.md — *An Import May Raise a Draft, Never a Posting, and a Deepening Borrows the Door of the Register It Lands In* — recorded alongside as **ADR-042** in this file.

**Standing boundary the round deliberately did not cross:** **receipts and payments stay outside, and nothing confirms for the shop.** Money has one writer, `/payments`, and an import does not make one — the money skip and the cash/bank leg block are the same rule read from both sides. A Tally *order* stays on the order registers until it is billed. And a draft is a proposal in a register, not a posting: the JR number, the DC confirmation, the stock movement, the return — every one of those still needs the human who owns the books. No schema, migration or RLS policy was touched (Rule 21), no new `localStorage` key was created (Rule 31 untriggered). **NOT VERIFIED LIVE**: the browser session is still signed out, so dropping a file with a journal and a challan and watching the drafts land on `/journal` and `/challans` has not been driven through the UI — this round's drive joins R3-4's through R3-7's as owed.

---

## Session — R3-9: the vault knows which books moved — a backup's promise is measured on the device, by checksum, never by timestamp and never from the file (2026-10-14)

**What was built:** matrix row #17 came back for its second pass, on the one question ADR-033's file could not answer. `src/lib/vault.ts` (**1,433 → 1,597 lines**) grew `VaultFingerprint {rows, checksum}` and an optional `fingerprints?: Record<string, VaultFingerprint>` on `VaultEvent`; `recordBackup(orgId, vault)` now builds that map from the `VaultFile` it was handed — the rows each register contributed and the very FNV-1a checksum its block was packed with — so the memory is of the bytes that left this browser. `registerFreshness(orgId, now)` walks the same 35 `REGISTERS` the packer walks, re-reads each key's raw value, recomputes `checksumOf()` and compares it **checksum-against-checksum** to the last recorded backup's fingerprint, returning a `FreshnessReport` of seven states — `fresh` *In your file*, `changed` *Changed since*, `new-since` *New since*, `cleared` *Only in your file*, `nothing` *Nothing to back up*, `unrecorded` *Cannot tell*, `never` *In no file yet* — each with its tone and its *why* in `FRESHNESS_META`, and `atRiskRows()` naming the three that a wipe would take. `loadVaultLog()` sanitises the map on read: a fingerprints object whose entries are not clean `{rows, checksum}` pairs is dropped whole, so a hand-edited log can only ever *compare*, never declare a book "In your file". `/vault` (**1,071 → 1,226 lines**) prints the answer where the question already lives — a **Registers**-tab card *"What moved since your last backup"* (`compare` glyph) with the three honest summary variants in `freshLine` (no backup ever / that backup predates fingerprints / the counted line), counted chips per state, the *"What a wipe would take with it"* list naming each book, its rows, and the page that owns it, and a seventh table column *Since your file*; the *Last backup taken* `StatCard` (`clock`) now sub-lines `${atRisk.length} books hold work your newest file does not`. Codified as **Rule 46** and **ADR-043**.

**Decisions the round had to make explicitly:** the comparison had to be **content, not touch**. A register's stored envelope carries its own `updatedAt`, and it moves when a page re-saves the same rows — so a timestamp answers *was this key touched*, not *did the books change*, which is the only question a shop standing in front of a backup screen is asking. A checksum answers content, and the checksum the packer already writes on every block was lying right there in the file being packed, which is why `recordBackup()` reads its fingerprints out of the `VaultFile` argument rather than recomputing them from the device — the memory is of the bytes that left. Two **ignorance states** were added rather than papered over: `never` (no backup has ever been recorded in this browser) and `unrecorded` (the last backup predates fingerprints) — the page prints "take a new backup and this table starts answering the question" instead of an empty grid or a guess. And `cleared` was deliberately **kept out of the at-risk list**: a book the device no longer holds but the file does is the one case where the file is the surviving copy, so a wipe takes nothing; it still prints *Only in your file* in red, because it is news the shop should see.

**Method:** **120 assertions in `/tmp/vault3-test.mjs`, 0 failing** — no-backup, first backup, an unchanged book, an edited book, a cleared book, a book the file never carried, an evidence-only register, the `unrecorded` degradation, the sanitiser (a forged `{rows, checksum:'abc'}` map reads `changed`, never `fresh`), restore-then-re-backup, cross-org isolation, and the page's own wiring (0 hex literals, both `registerFreshness(oid)` call sites, the 7th column, the three summary variants); `/tmp/tally3-test.mjs` re-run **99 / 0**, `tsc` **0**, `eslint` **0 problems on the round's two changed files**, `npm run build` ✓ exit 0 with `/vault` in the route table. **The route-table count was settled at this pass**: this round's own log re-counted row by row prints **69 rows** — 65 `page.tsx` (the root included: `redirect('/login')` still earns its `○ /` row), 2 `route.ts`, `src/app/manifest.ts` as `/manifest.webmanifest`, and the framework's `/_not-found` — over **67 route source files**, which vindicates R3-6's "corrects the 68" note and disproves the R3-5 sentence that had claimed the root earns no row; both older entries now carry their correction in place. Width behaviour of the new column and cards checked by **static CSS audit** — the `.tfv-inv` grid's seventh track folds under the kit's 768px `.responsive-table` rule, and `.tfv-risk-row` collapses to one column at 560px.

**Ledger:** **65 `page.tsx` + 2 `route.ts` = 67 route files, 54 libraries, 10 component files, 74 glyphs, 35 vault specs (30 restorable, 5 evidence-only)** — re-counted at this pass, all exactly where R3-8 left them. R3-9 added **no register, no `localStorage` key, no route, no glyph and no component**: the fingerprints ride the log key the vault already owns (`taxflow:vault:<orgId>`, which is never itself a spec), so **Rule 31 stayed untriggered** and the vault is still 35/30. Glyph registry re-audited: `/vault` uses **52 glyph calls over 20 distinct keys**, all resolving to registered glyphs, none falling through to the silent `dashboard` fallback — a page that reads a checksum needs no new shape.

**Rule earned:** **Rule 46** in 3-Development-Rules.md — *A Backup's Promise Is Measured on the Device, and Which Books Moved Is Answered by Checksum, Never by Timestamp and Never from the File* — recorded alongside as **ADR-043** in this file.

**Standing boundary the round deliberately did not cross:** **the file is still not read, and the vault still does not sync.** Nothing here talks to a server, checks a file in a folder, or reconciles two devices — a second laptop that restores this file inherits the books and starts its own record, because a claim about what one browser handed out is not data to travel inside what it handed out. And the answer is device-side by construction: this page can say *this browser is newer than the backup this browser took*, which is the honest half of "are my books safe"; the other half — where the file sits, whether it still exists — belongs to the shop. No schema, migration or RLS policy was touched (Rule 21), no new key was created (Rule 31 untriggered). **NOT VERIFIED LIVE**: the browser session is still signed out and `/vault` redirects to `/login`, so taking a backup, adding a row to a register, and reading the Registers tab has not been driven through the UI — this round's drive joins R3-4's through R3-8's as owed.
