### Routes — 65 pages
```
src/app/
├── layout.tsx, page.tsx (→ /login), manifest.ts, globals.css, favicon.ico
├── login/  signup/  setup/
├── portal/                          ← public statement link — no shell, no auth (R2-16 B1)
├── api/gsp/ewaybill/route.ts        ← mock GSP transport
├── api/pdf-worker/route.ts          ← version-locked pdf.js worker asset
└── (app)/                           ← shell: layout.tsx + nav.ts data
    ├── dashboard/                   pos/            offline/
    ├── invoices/{page,new,[id]}/    quotations/     customers/  vendors/  products/
    ├── recurring/                   ← recurring-invoice schedules: derived due piles, catch-up run (R2-18 B1)
    ├── fx/                          ← foreign-currency register: rate ledger + deals joined to invoices by number (R2-19 B1)
    ├── challans/{page,[id]}/        ← delivery challan register + printable document
    ├── sales-orders/{page,[id]}/    purchase-orders/{page,[id]}/  ← order register + document (R2-14 B2)
    ├── bills/  expenses/  credit-notes/  debit-notes/
    ├── payments/page  payments/links/  bank/
    ├── inventory/{page,reorder,batches,serials,godowns,recipes}/  ← reorder planner + batch/expiry + serial + godown registers (R2-15, R2-17) + recipes & builds (R2-24)
    ├── ledger/  accounting/
    ├── journal/                     ← the manual journal: heads that each name one statement line, entries that must balance to the paisa, and a posting answered by a mirror rather than edited (R3-2b)
    ├── business/                    ← the branch & document-series register: which registration issued a paper, what shape its numbers wear, and the day the books were picked up (R3-2)
    ├── audit/                       ← the append-only trail and the period seals: the log numbers itself, a window seals only once it has ended, and a refused write is itself an event (R3-1)
    ├── cost-centres/                ← centre register + document splits stored as shares (R2-21 B1)
    ├── tally/                       ← the bridge: a Tally export read into a per-row plan, chosen books written back as XML (R2-22)
    ├── projects/                    ← hours logged against a job, documents attached by id, unbilled months watched against rule 47 (R2-23)
    ├── payroll/                     ← the salary register: people, the month run, the day book, its statutory dues, the annual sheet and the cost of an hour — every rupee computed (R2-31)
    ├── payroll/payslip/             ← that register as A4 paper: one person's slip or the whole month as one PDF, letterhead typed here (R2-33)
    ├── tds/                         ← withholding workpaper
    ├── vault/                       ← the books vault: thirty-five named registers into one file, plan-then-restore through the owners (R2-26)
    ├── gst/{page,gstr1,gstr3b,gstr9,gstr4,reconciliation,einvoice,calendar,job-work,exports,filings}/  ← + the job-work register and ITC-04 workpaper (R2-20 B1) + the export register: LUT, shipping bills, FEMA realisation windows (R2-25) + the filings & amendments register: what each return stated, its late-fee slab and its open windows (R3-4)
    ├── reports/{page,registers,targets}/
    ├── ai/{ask,scan-bill}/
    └── settings/
public/sw.js  public/offline.html
```

### Libraries — 54 modules in `src/lib/` (+ 3 in `src/lib/gsp/`)
```
supabase.ts  constants.ts  gst-engine.ts  format.ts  words.ts  reports.ts
gst-rates.ts                                 ← the one GST rate table every picker imports
create-invoice.ts  create-bill.ts            ← the only document write paths
einvoice.ts  gstr2b.ts  gstr9.ts  compliance.ts  registers.ts  payments.ts
creditnote.ts                                 ← the one writer of a credit or debit note (`createCreditNote` / `createDebitNote` over a private `createNote` + `NoteSpec`); extracted from the two note pages in R2-27 so the Tally bridge writes the same rows the forms do
ledger.ts  party-ledger.ts  targets.ts  reorder.ts  barcode.ts  outbox.ts
bill-text.ts  ocr.ts  ask.ts  commands.ts  nav.ts  deep-link-search.ts
bank-statement.ts                             ← statement parser + match scorer
tds.ts                                        ← withholding sections, workpaper, deposits
gstr4.ts                                      ← composition slabs, CMP-08 quarters, deemed debit
orderflow.ts                                  ← challans / sales orders / purchase orders (localStorage); `takeNumber` is the only writer of a document number, and `appendDraft` / `raiseDrafts` let another page hand a draft to that register without minting its own numbering
batches.ts                                    ← lots, FEFO drawdown, expiry watch (localStorage)
serials.ts                                    ← one row per unit, append-only event log, derived warranty (localStorage); since R2-35 the log carries a build draw's `issued` — the state a lot cannot name — `SerialView.last_ref` reads the paper behind the latest event, and `shelfUnitsOf()` hands a draw the oldest in-stock units of a part
godowns.ts                                    ← godown registry + transfers as lot splits, per-place stock (localStorage)
portal.ts                                     ← share-link statements: deflate + base64url in the URL fragment, nothing stored
recurring.ts                                  ← schedule rules, derived occurrence calendars, catch-up payloads (localStorage)
fx.ts                                         ← dated rate ledger + foreign-currency deals joined to invoices by number (localStorage)
jobwork.ts                                    ← job-work lots on the section 143 clock, rule 45 deeming, ITC-04 grids (localStorage)
costcentres.ts                                ← centres + per-document shares; rupees re-read from the books, never stored (localStorage)
tally.ts                                      ← Tally XML read → per-row import plan; our books written back as the XML TallyPrime imports
projects.ts                                   ← jobs + hours + documents attached by id; rupees re-read from the books, rule 47 window derived (localStorage); since R2-36 an hour may name its payroll person and is costed at that person's own entry-month through `costHours()` — cost rows handed in by the page, never an import — printed as `after_labour` beside contribution, never inside it (ADR-030, amended)
payroll.ts                                    ← the salary register: six components + four month exceptions stored, everything else computed — dated PF/ESI bands, the section 192 year-walk (`taxWalk`), the dues calendar (`statutoryDues` / `payrollEvents`) and the hour cost `/projects` reads as data since R2-36 (`HourCostRow`s handed to `costHours()`, never an import; the page's two copy lines say so); writes no money and touches no schema (localStorage, ADR-034); since R2-33 its store also carries the payslip letterhead, and since R2-34 the day book — the `attendance` marks themselves plus `DAY_MARK_CYCLE` and the date-headed `attendanceTemplate` shape that `attendance.ts` reads and writes
payslip.ts                                    ← the month as paper: the register's own reading laid out as an A4 slip — no rupee re-derived (the suite polices the tie, not the layout), dashes with named sentences, refused months printed with no figures, one pack per month; stores nothing of its own — even the letterhead it writes lands in `payroll.ts`'s key (ADR-034, amended R2-33)
attendance.ts                                 ← the day book: one mark per person per day kept as evidence (`markDay`, the single door, empty mark = clear, refusals for off-roll and unborn days), the pasted page bridged to marks with refusals carried by machine-readable `kind` (only `blank` is noted and skipped; anything else stops the whole page), and the pull — the one click that counts lost days and pushes every candidate through the month run's own `validateInput()`, leaving a person the book never wrote untouched rather than zeroed; carries no rupee (localStorage, ADR-034, amended R2-34)
bom.ts                                        ← recipes as component *quantities* (never a rupee), builds as what was drawn *from which lot* — and, since R2-35, *which numbered units* (`allocateSerials` proposes oldest-first, `validateSerialIssues` refuses anything short of the whole draw, `serialWritesFor` / `reverseSerialWritesFor` describe the events and `serialMovedOn` guards the cancel) — FEFO issuance + the finished lot they make, requirement/where-used explosion, JOB-<FY>-NNNN sequence (localStorage); since R2-30 also `mergedShortages` + `shoppingPlan` — the shortage folded once and grouped by the vendor that last supplied each part, a *reading* of `vendors` / `purchase_bills` / `purchase_bill_items` that stores nothing and hands its drafts to `orderflow.raiseDrafts`
exports.ts                                    ← shipping bills + LUTs as paper, dated FEMA bands as the clock, realisations joined to /fx (localStorage)
invoice-template.ts                           ← the invoice skin choice, extracted from InvoiceDocument.tsx in R2-26 so the vault can walk it (bare string)
vault.ts                                      ← the books vault: thirty-five named registers, per-block checksums, plan-then-restore through each owner's save (ADR-033)
audit.ts                                      ← the append-only trail and the period seals (R3-1): `append()` is the only writer of an event and `seq` counts from 1, `verifyChain()` walks the log by its own **number** so a gap, a duplicate or a reorder is named rather than assumed, `sealPeriod()` refuses a window that has not ended and an overlap with a seal already standing, `releaseSeal()` keeps the row and demands a reason, and the seal is asked in **three forms** (R3-5): `sealGuard()` **reads** and returns the register's own sentence, `askSeal()` reads **and** appends the attempt as a `refused` event before returning the guard or `null`, and `requireOpenSeal()` throws `SealedWriteError` on top of it — the form the three shared money writers (`create-invoice.ts`, `create-bill.ts`, `creditnote.ts`) call as their first statement, above the sequence read (localStorage)
setup.ts                                      ← the branch & document-series register (R3-2): a branch is a **registration** — its 15-character GSTIN and the state code it starts with must agree or the row is refused, the head is handed over before it can close or vanish, and a branch still stamped `[branch CODE]` in invoice notes can only be deactivated; `composeNumber(fmt, n, fy)` dresses a *shape* (prefix ≤ 8, pad 1–8, FY in or out) over counters that stayed exactly where they were — the Supabase `invoice_sequences` row for invoices, each order register's own high-water walk for the rest — and `seriesHistory()` + `usedNumber()` / `usedNumberAnyYear()` make that walk read **every shape a kind has worn** so nothing is ever numbered twice; `validateSeries()` refuses two kinds on one prefix, and the balance sheet's opening grew `bank` · `receivables` · `payables` + an `as_of` day the books were picked up (localStorage)
journal.ts                                    ← the manual journal (R3-2b): heads that each **declare the one statement line they move** — `SLOT_META.natural` is the app's only home for the debit/credit convention, so a fold cannot get an income leg backwards — entries of two or more legs summed in **paise** that must balance to the paisa before they reach a statement, the `draft → posted → reversed` lifecycle where `reverseEntry()` re-summonses **every leg from the original with its side flipped** (a correction cannot drift from what it answers, and a used head is closed rather than deleted), `journalRows()` handing `ledger.ts` plain rows it can read without either library importing the other (Rule 23), and `plugProof()` reading the rows the sheet is holding back to their sides so the page **prints the arithmetic** instead of claiming a balanced entry cannot move the derived capital figure — which it cannot, because Δplug = Σdebits − Σcredits. Numbers wear `setup.ts`'s sixth series (`JR-<FY>-NNNN`) over this register's own high-water `seq`, and `peekNumber()` allocates nothing. It holds rupees as *stated movements on named lines*, never as a balance, and nothing it files reaches a return (localStorage)
filings.ts                                    ← the filings & amendments register (R3-4): one row per `(form, period)` that **transcribes** what the return states and owns none of it — `statutoryDue()` re-derives the day from the rule beside the date copied off the calendar and prints only the disagreements, `estimateLateFee()` reads the shop's own slab and names the notification that decided it, `windowsFor()` runs the three amendment clocks (proviso to rule 59(1), Table 9A, section 16(4) per Circular 237/2024) and prints a shut one as *lapsed*, `consequencesFor()` quotes the 59(6)(a)/(b)/(d) bars, `varianceFor()` compares books against stated within the 9C's ₹1, and `sealForPeriod()` / `sealFilingsPeriod()` **read** and **hand** the seal without ever asking it as a door (ADR-038, Rule 41; R3-5 folded this file's second spelling, `sealOf()`, into `sealForPeriod()`)
pdf-export.ts                                 ← shared html2canvas → jsPDF slice pipeline
gsp/{index.ts, types.ts, mock.ts}            ← provider adapter, mock transport
```
(`gst-engine.ts` also still exports the legacy `numberToWords()` that the classic
print layout uses. `words.ts` is the newer implementation. They intentionally
coexist — do not "unify" them and change a printed invoice silently.)

### Components — 10 in `src/components/`
```
ui.tsx               ← Card, SectionTitle, StatCard, DeltaPill, Badge, Tone tokens
Icons.tsx            ← stroke glyph set (74 glyphs), key lookup with dashboard fallback
InvoiceDocument.tsx  ← classic / modern / minimal skins
CommandPalette.tsx   ← ⌘K
BarcodeScanner.tsx   ← getUserMedia + BarcodeDetector, graceful degrade
PwaRegister.tsx      ← service-worker registration
AuthShell.tsx        ← the shared chrome for login / signup / setup (R2-12 B9)
OrderRegister.tsx    ← shared register + editor for sales/purchase orders (kind-keyed COPY table)
OrderDocument.tsx    ← shared printable order document, SO/PO branches inside
SealNotice.tsx       ← the sealed-period banner, 47 lines, read off sealGuard only — eight pages hang it (R3-5)
```

### Data model
MVP tables (September): `profiles`, `organizations`, `org_members`, `gst_registrations`,
`customers`, `vendors`, `products`, `invoice_sequences`, `invoices`, `invoice_items`,
`purchase_bills`, `purchase_bill_items`, `expenses`, `payments`, `warehouses`,
`stock_movements`, `gst_return_periods`.

Added after the MVP: `logos`,
`quotations` + `quotation_items` + `quotation_sequences`,
`credit_notes` + `credit_note_items` + `credit_note_sequences`,
`debit_notes` + `debit_note_items` + `debit_note_sequences`,
and columns on `products` (`sku`, `minimum_stock`, `opening_stock`).
`warehouses` is in the schema but never queried — the R2-17 godown register is client-side
(`taxflow:godowns:<orgId>`), and a product's `current_stock` still has no per-godown column,
so per-place figures cover the registered batch lots only.
Scanned bills are drafted in the browser and only reach the database when the user
saves a real purchase bill; there is no `documents` table.

Functions: `handle_new_user()`, `is_org_member(org_id)`, `is_org_owner(org_id)`,
`record_stock_movement(...)` — the only permitted way to change stock.

Schema constraints the code has to live with: `payments` has no party id (the ledger
links payments to documents first, then normalises party names, and drops receipts
pointing at cancelled or missing documents); `invoices.customer_id` nullability is
unknown, so a POS cash sale auto-creates a cash customer; `warehouses` exists but is
unused; `current_stock` is seeded from `opening_stock` on insert only; **`invoices`
has no currency column** — every rupee figure in it is the statutory one, which is
why multi-currency is a separate dated ledger joined by invoice number (ADR-026)
rather than a rate stored on the bill.

---

## Client-Side State Keys (localStorage)

All keys are namespaced per organisation as `taxflow:<feature>:<orgId>` and store
`{ version, updatedAt, orgId, ... }`. Clearing the browser is a real user risk, so
anything here is either reconstructible from documents or already exported.

| Key | Holds | Written by |
|-----|-------|-----------|
| `taxflow:upi:` | UPI IDs and link settings | `/payments/links` |
| `taxflow:opening:` | the day the shop's books were picked up and the eight lines that were true on it — `cash` · `bank` · `receivables` · `payables` · `fixed assets` · `loans` · `capital` + `as_of` (the three money lines and the date grew in R3-2, additively, so every legacy file still reads); `openingStood` gates them, so a balance sheet drawn before that day shows the documents it has rather than an opening it was not yet standing on | `/accounting` |
| `taxflow:outbox:` | offline invoice queue | `/offline`, the invoice form |
| `taxflow:ewb:` | e-way bill log | `/gst/einvoice` |
| `taxflow:compliance:` | per-event filing status — and, since R3-7, the shop's own stated cash tax for a quarter, keyed `cash:<quarter>` beside the filing ticks; `isCashTaxKey()` splits the two kinds of row so the vault never counts a rupee as a filed return | `/gst/calendar` |
| `taxflow:compliance-profile:` | filing profile (monthly / QRMP) | `/gst/calendar` |
| `taxflow:targets:` | annual plan and product goals | `/reports/targets` |
| `taxflow:reorder:` | policy overrides and PO history | `/inventory/reorder` |
| `taxflow:invoice-template:` | skin choice (`classic` / `modern` / `minimal`, stored bare, not quoted) | `/settings`, via `src/lib/invoice-template.ts` (extracted from `InvoiceDocument.tsx` in R2-26) |
| `taxflow:gstr9:` | declared 3B figures and Table 8/9 input | `/gst/gstr9` |
| `taxflow:bank:` | imported statements and confirm/set-aside decisions | `/bank` |
| `taxflow:tds:` | section overrides, payer type and monthly challan details for withholding | `/tds` |
| `taxflow:gstr4:` | declared CMP-08 deposits and stock-transfer value for the year | `/gst/gstr4` |
| `taxflow:challan:` · `taxflow:sorder:` · `taxflow:porder:` | order-flow documents + numbering per kind (`challan` / `sales_order` / `purchase_order`) — the per-FY high-water mark is the only writer of a number, which is why `/inventory/recipes` can hand a shortage to `raiseDrafts()` (R2-30) and the draft that lands is indistinguishable from one typed at `/purchase-orders` | `/challans`, `/sales-orders`, `/purchase-orders`, and the recipe board's *Turn the shortage into paper* for the purchase kind |
| `taxflow:batches:` · `taxflow:batchmoves:` | batch lots and their signed movements (FEFO drawdown) | `/inventory/batches`, `create-invoice.ts` |
| `taxflow:serials:` · `taxflow:serialevents:` | serial units and their append-only event log (received, sold, returned, restocked, scrapped — and, since R2-35, `issued`: a unit a build draw took goes *In a build* with the `JOB-<FY>-NNNN` number as its paper, back on the shelf only through that run's cancel or the register's own hand-take, never by editing a status) | `/inventory/serials`, and the build modal at `/inventory/recipes` writing through `appendEvents` |
| `taxflow:godowns:` | the godown registry (named places stock is kept in; a virtual `Main` seed holds unplaced lots) | `/inventory/godowns` |
| `taxflow:rec:` | recurring schedules — rule + template lines + the billed trail (never the due dates; ADR-025) | `/recurring`, `createInvoice` / the offline queue |
| `taxflow:fx:` | foreign-currency deals — rate ledger (dated + source-stamped), per-invoice currency entries and remittances; the rupee books are untouched and joined by invoice number (ADR-026) | `/fx`, read-only against `invoices` |
| `taxflow:jobwork:` | job workers, the lots sent out on a rule 55 challan, the movements back (returned / delivered direct / on to a second worker) and the declared turnover the ITC-04 cadence runs on; deadlines and the four grids are derived, never stored (ADR-027) | `/gst/job-work`, read-only against the `/challans` register |
| `taxflow:ccentres:` | the centre list and, per document, one allocation of `{centre → share}` — **percentages only, never a rupee**; the money is re-read from the five document tables on every load, and `settings.home_state` is stamped from `gst_registrations` so the rule 41A flag reads the same way offline (ADR-028) | `/cost-centres`, read-only against `invoices` / `credit_notes` / `debit_notes` / `purchase_bills` / `expenses` |
| `taxflow:tally:` | **only the log** of an import — which file, when, and how many customers / vendors / products / invoices / bills it wrote; the parsed dump, the plan and the exported XML exist in memory and on disk, never here (ADR-029) | `/tally` |
| `taxflow:bom:` | recipes (component **quantities**, units per run, waste, **labour and overhead minutes**) and builds (what was actually drawn, **from which lots** — `{batch_id, batch_no, qty, expiry_date}` picks per line — and, since R2-35, **which numbered units** — `{serial_id, serial_no}` picks per line — the variance against the recipe, whether the movements landed, and the `output_batch_id` of the lot the finished units landed in), plus the dated **shop-rate rows** that price the minutes and the per-FY `JOB-<FY>-NNNN` high-water mark — **never a rupee**: material value is rolled up from `products.purchase_price` and the time legs priced from the rate row in force on each run's own day, on every load, and a lot pick is a *decision re-checked against* `taxflow:batches:` + `taxflow:batchmoves:` at write time — a unit pick the same way against `taxflow:serials:` + `taxflow:serialevents:` — never a balance stored twice (ADR-031, amended R2-28, R2-29 and R2-35; R2-30 reads it for the shopping plan and stores nothing new) | `/inventory/recipes`, `record_stock_movement` for whatever a built run moves, and the three registers it mirrors into |
| `taxflow:projects:` | projects, the hour rows, and whole documents attached **by id** — **measurements only, never a rupee**; revenue, spend, ITC, contribution and the unbilled months are re-read from the books on every load through the same `bookDocs()` reader cost centres use, and an hour carries the rate you would *bill* it at, not what it cost — since R2-36 an hour may also **name its payroll person** (`employee_id`, additive at `PROJECTS_VERSION = 1`), and the costed hour is read from that register **as data** (`HourCostRow`s handed to `costHours()` by the page) and printed as `after_labour` — *after named hours* — **beside** contribution, never folded into it, `partial` while hours remain unstated and a refusal keeping the register's own sentence. Since R2-37 an hour can also be **drawn** from the payroll register's kept day book: the same page reads the marks (`dayBook()` + `markWork()`) and hands the pure `drawHours()` structural `BookWorkDay[]` rows — a present day proposed at the register's own `hours_per_day`, half a mark proposed as half, every other mark refused with the register's own sentence — and only the shop's tick writes, each drawn row still through `validateEntry` and carrying its `employee_id`, so the R2-36 cost reading picks it up the moment it lands (`ticks: null` means proposal; a change of project, person or month re-proposes) (ADR-030, amended R2-36 and R2-37) | `/projects`, read-only against `invoices` / `credit_notes` / `debit_notes` / `purchase_bills` / `expenses`, and since R2-37 a *reader* of `taxflow:payroll:`'s day book — it writes no mark back |
| `taxflow:exports:` | the LUTs (per registration, per FY), the export files (shipping bill / port / destination / mode / route to zero-rating / advance date / refund reference) and the realisation credits typed here — **never an invoice value, an IGST figure or a balance**: those are re-read from `invoices` by invoice number, and where `/fx` holds the deal its receipts are the counted rupees while a credit typed here prints *not counted* (ADR-032) | `/gst/exports`, read-only against `invoices`; its watch rows are read by `/gst/calendar` |
| `taxflow:payroll:` | the salary register — each person's **structure** (six monthly components with their PF / ESI flags, an age band, a state, joining and leaving dates, hours a day, a per-employee professional-tax figure that may be *unset*) and each month's **exceptions** (lost days, arrears, bonus, recovery), plus the shop's four settings — **no gross, no PF, no TDS and no net pay is ever stored here**: every rupee is re-walked from these two lists on every load, and the year's tax is walked across the year rather than multiplied by twelve (ADR-034). Since R2-32 a lost-day figure may arrive from a pasted attendance sheet, whose text is still never stored — what a paste writes into the month run is one number and one `[attendance <month>]` sentence in that row's note, replaced rather than stacked on a re-import. Since R2-34 the *days themselves* can be kept: the same envelope carries the **day book** — one `attendance` mark per person per day (`{employee_id, date, mark, source: 'hand' | 'sheet', note, written_at}`), **evidence that carries no rupee**, because `lop_days` stays the only lost-day figure a salary reads and only the tab's one deliberate pull — every candidate row re-run through the month form's own `validateInput()` — can move it; a person the book never wrote is left untouched rather than zeroed, an empty book refuses to pull at all, and a pasted mark stays dotted so it reads differently from a tap. Since R2-33 it also holds the payslip **letterhead** — 15 typed lines, 4 of them statutory identifiers (`pf_establishment`, `esi_code`, `employer_pan`, `gstin`), each carrying its own sentence naming what it would be needed for — written only through `saveLetterhead` inside the store's own `mutate`, because *a heading is not a register* (Rule 24): **no slip is stored, no new key was ever made, and `PAYROLL_VERSION` stays 1 through every amendment**. Since R2-37 the day book is also what `/projects` **draws hours from**: the same page reads the marks through `markWork()`, a present day arrives as a proposed hours row at this register's own `hours_per_day` (half a mark proposes half, every other mark is refused with this register's own sentence), and only the shop's tick writes — into the project's register, never back in the book | `/payroll` (people, month rows and the day book) and `/payroll/payslip` (the letterhead, through `saveLetterhead`, and nothing else) — read-only against nothing at all: no other table is touched, and no money is written anywhere; `/gst/calendar` and `/projects` read its derived rows — and since R2-37 `/projects` also reads its kept day-book marks as data, writing none of them back — and `/payroll/payslip` reads its reading of a month and prints it (R2-33; day book R2-34; drawn into jobs R2-37) |
| `taxflow:audit:` | the append-only trail — every event numbered by the register's own monotonic `seq`, which `verifyChain()` walks **by number** so a gap, a duplicate or a reorder is named rather than assumed — and the **period seals** (a month or a whole FY, only over a window that has already ended, never overlapping a seal already standing). **A window and a sentence, never a rupee**: releasing keeps the row and stamps `released_at` + a required reason, and a write the seal refused lands here as a `refused` event even though nothing in the books changed — the *attempt* is the evidence (ADR-035) | `/audit` (`append` / `sealPeriod` / `releaseSeal` are the only writers), and any guarded write path through `askSeal()` / `requireOpenSeal()` — since R3-5 the three shared writers (`create-invoice.ts`, `create-bill.ts`, `creditnote.ts`) carry the door for twelve paths, `journal.ts` asks inside its own engine, and six more paths are carried by the page that types straight into its table; `/audit`'s `GUARDED` list prints all twenty-one rows and where each asks (R3-1, R3-2b, R3-5) |
| `taxflow:setup:` | the **branch** list — each a registration whose 15-character GSTIN and state code must agree, one of them the head that signs the printed header by default, and a branch still stamped `[branch CODE]` in invoice notes can only be deactivated — plus the **six document-series** overrides (`invoice` · `quotation` · `challan` · `sales_order` · `purchase_order` · `journal`): prefix ≤ 8, pad 1–8, FY in or out, one prefix per series. **A number's shape only, never its count** — the counters stayed where they were (the Supabase `invoice_sequences` row for invoices, each order register's own high-water walk for the rest, which reads every shape a kind has worn), and no rupee lives on this key (ADR-036) | `/business` — read by `create-invoice.ts`, `orderflow.ts`, the challans page, the shared `OrderRegister`, the quotations page, the recipes materials board and `journal.ts` through `resolveSeries()`, and by the invoice form / print page for the branch stamp |
| `taxflow:journal:` | the manual journal — the shop's own **heads** (each declaring the one statement line its rupee moves, and closed rather than deleted once used) and every **entry** struck against them: drafts, postings, and the mirrors that answer them, with the `JR-<FY>-NNNN` number each carried and this register's own high-water `seq` map. **The app's one deliberate exception** to "money records are never stored here": it holds rupees, but as *stated movements on named statement lines*, never as a cash figure — and every posting balanced to the paisa on the way in, which is why `plugProof()` can prove the derived capital line did not move. Nothing here is filed, nothing settles a bill, and no return reads a journal row (ADR-037) | `/journal` (`writeHead` / `addStarterHeads` / `setHeadActive` / `deleteHead` / `writeEntry` / `postEntry` / `reverseEntry` / `deleteEntry` are the only writers) — read as rows by `ledger.ts`'s two derivations, by `/accounting`, and by `/ai/ask` as a caveat |
| `taxflow:filings:` | the filings & amendments register — one **transcript** row per `(form, period)` for the six forms this register keeps (GSTR-1, 1A, 3B, CMP-08, GSTR-4, GSTR-9): the status the shop states, the day it was due *beside the rule that dated it*, the figures the return declared, the late fee and interest recorded as paid, and every **amendment** carried with the window that governs it. Stored figures are what the return *states*, never recomputed, and **nothing in `ledger.ts` reads them**; the seal is read from `taxflow:audit:` and a close is handed to `/audit` rather than guarded here (ADR-038, Rule 41) |
| `taxflow:vault:` | **the vault's own log** — the last 30 backup / restore events with their register, row and character counts (a refused restore is logged too, so the history is a record of attempts), and since **R3-9** a per-register *fingerprint* (`{rows, checksum}`) on each backup event, which is what `registerFreshness()` compares this browser against; deliberately **not inside the file it logs** (ADR-033), so neither the history nor the fingerprints travel — the claim stays on the device that made it (ADR-043) | `/vault` |
| `taxflow:palette-recents:` | recent ⌘K hits | `CommandPalette.tsx` |

---

## Traps That Cost Debugging Time

Recorded so the next session does not rediscover them.

1. **CSS specificity across breakpoints** — a desktop `.cols > .is-right` rule outranks the mobile `.cols > span` rule; override with equal or higher specificity, not with `!important`.
2. **Rupee strings have no break opportunity** — one figure per line on mobile, and assert `scrollWidth − clientWidth === 0` rather than eyeballing.
3. **`--loader` registers the ESM hook; plain `--import` of the hook file does not** — the test file then fails to parse TypeScript. **Amended at R3-1:** `--import` *does* work when the module it imports is a **registrar** rather than the hook itself, which is why the R3 suites run as `node --experimental-strip-types --no-warnings --import /tmp/jr-register.mjs /tmp/<suite>-test.mjs` (`jr-register.mjs` is two lines: `import { register } from 'node:module'` then `register('/tmp/jr-hooks.mjs', 'file:///tmp/')`). `--loader` is deprecated on this Node, so the registrar is the recipe to copy forward; importing `jr-hooks.mjs` directly would still do nothing.
4. **Library test suites live in `/tmp`** and are not committed. Recorded counts at R2-11: gstr2b + compliance 30, registers 20, words 29, targets 38, commands 26, gstr9 35, tds 70; batch 6 added 46 over the 2B reconciliation and e-invoice builders, R2-13 another 53 over `gstr4.ts` — rewrite them when a library changes. `/tmp` gets wiped mid-project (it happened once here, taking the loader with it), so `ts-loader.mjs` may need recreating before a suite can run at all; it was rebuilt on 2026-10-08, when `/tmp/gst-rates-test.mjs` added 22 assertions over the rate table and the calendar. **It was wiped again on 2026-10-10 and this time the seventeen suites went with it** — `bom` (631), `tally` (670), `projects` (228), `exports` (309), `vault` (240), `costcentres` (245), `jobwork` (210), `fx` (153), `recurring` (183), `orderflow` (111), `godowns` (97), `serials` (84), `portal` (82), `batches` (78), `commands` (49), `registers` (21), `gst-rates` (22). Nothing was lost from the repository (they were never committed, and every assertion they carried is a reading of code that is still on disk), but **the running total stops being re-runnable at 3,413**: from R2-30 on, each round records the assertions in *its own* new suite and says plainly that the older ones were not re-run, rather than quoting a total it cannot prove. (The per-suite figures above are the ones each round entry recorded, and they sum to exactly 3,413 — the list stays trustworthy even though the files are gone.) The rebuilt loader plus `/tmp/shopping-test.mjs` (95) were the set after R2-30; **a further wipe has since taken the shopping suite with it, and as of R3-2b's docs pass `/tmp` holds exactly four suites** — `journal-test.mjs` (393), `fold-test.mjs` (250), `audit-test.mjs` (122), `setup-test.mjs` (131) = **896 assertions, all four re-run green in one sitting** — plus `/tmp/ts-loader.mjs` and the two hook files the R3 suites actually use: `jr-hooks.mjs` (a `resolve()` hook mapping `@/lib/x` → `<repo>/src/lib/x.ts` and extension-less relative specifiers onto `.ts`) and `jr-register.mjs`, a two-line module that calls `module.register()` on it. A suite is cheap to rewrite from the library's own exports, and the claim worth keeping is which rule each one polices, not the count. **The set as of R3-9:** the 2026-10-14 wipe took those four (and the `filings` suite beside them) with it, and the rebuilt `jr-register.mjs` / `jr-hooks.mjs` harness has since carried two suites — `/tmp/tally3-test.mjs` (99, written at R3-8) and `/tmp/vault3-test.mjs` (120, written at R3-9) = **219 assertions, both re-run green in one sitting at the R3-9 docs pass** — while the five counts that stood at R3-7 (filings 547, journal 393, fold 250, audit 172, setup 131 = 1,493) stay as recorded in their own round entries and cannot be re-run.
5. **`1-based` vs `0-based` months** — `iso(y, m, d)` vs `fyMonth()`; off-by-one shifts a whole quarter.
6. **`Math.abs()` on a signed document** — turns a credit note into extra sales.
7. **Unbounded `in=(...)`** — Supabase 400s; chunk at 500 ids.
8. **BOM duplication** — `party-ledger.download()` already prefixes `\uFEFF`; combining it with `csvFromRows` produced a doubled marker. Ragged rows go through `csvFromRows`.
9. **`useSearchParams` on a prerendered page** — needs the `XPageInner` + `<Suspense>` split or the build fails.
10. **Duplicate listbox ids** — the palette must namespace option ids per group and per query.
11. **`?q=` column parity** — a deep link must filter on exactly the columns the list page reads, or a search hit lands on an empty list.
12. **Supabase reads are `as any[]`** with explicit `.select()` lists — the repo's accepted baseline until types are generated; compare `npx eslint` output against the pre-existing count instead of expecting zero.
13. **A `<button>` with no `type` inside a form submits it** — the kit's `Segmented` did exactly that in the product modal, so switching goods→service saved the product. Any control that can live inside a `<form>` renders `type="button"`.
14. **`evaluate_script` can read the DOM a render behind a click** — a status cell read right after `.click()` showed the old value twice during R2-12. Re-query in a later call, or read the derived stats as well as the row.
15. **A styling migration is a code review** — rewriting `/payments` surfaced a `.limit(20)` history window and a `getMonth()`-only "this month" card that counted the same month every year. Read the logic while replacing the chrome; fix what you find instead of preserving it.
16. **`<Icon>` swallows an unknown name** — `Icons.tsx` ends with `{P[name] ?? P.dashboard}`, so a typo or a name that was never added renders the *dashboard* grid, not an error and not a blank box. `/offline`'s "Syncing…" button was quietly doing this. Grep the name against the map when adding an icon.
17. **`Intl` currency output already carries the symbol** — `new Intl.NumberFormat('en-IN', { style: 'currency', currency: 'INR' })` returns `₹1,20,000`, so a hand-written `₹` prefix in the same line renders `₹₹1,20,000`. Prefix only the minus sign.
18. **A totals row inherits its header's label** — on the mobile card layout every cell renders `attr(data-label)`, so a foot row built the same way reads `Invoice no. — Total · 5 invoices` above the amounts. The lead foot cell needs an empty `data-label` (or `.is-lead::before { content: none }`) and the blank filler cells need `display: none`.
19. **`Math.round` and `Intl` disagree on an exact half-paisa** — `34090.715` renders `34,090.72` through `Intl` but `34090.71` via `Math.round(v * 100) / 100`, because the double is really `…7149999`. Any derived figure you split in two (`expense GST ÷ 2` per head) has to leave the app through the **same formatter the screen uses**, or the CSV contradicts the page the CA is reading.
20. **A rebuilt screen is a test of the old one** — the R2-12 GST pass found more arithmetic bugs than styling ones (IGST counted in two sections, the whole credit printed as "ITC utilised", nil-rated supplies hard-coded to zero, `financial_year` from today, due dates in the wrong month). Wherever the new markup forces two figures to add up, check that they do before trusting either.
21. **Place of supply `01` is Jammu & Kashmir, not an export** — only `99` ("Other Countries") is outside India. `einvoice.ts` was treating `01` as foreign, so a J&K supply built as `Country: "OTHER COUNTRY"` with the six-digit pincode dropped and `InvoiceType: EXP` instead of `RGC`. Any POS comparison in this codebase is `=== '99'` and nothing else.
22. **A document-type union that only knows INV/CRN/DBN mislabels a bill of supply** — a composition-registered seller (`RR`/`RSRG`) issues a *bill of supply*, which NIC carries as `BIL`. Worse: the validation guard was `documentType !== 'INV' → require a reference invoice/number/date`, so a bill of supply was rejected for missing a document it never had. Only **notes** (CRN/DBN) point back at an invoice; `BIL` is a standalone document.
23. **`StatCard` renders a numeric `value` through `formatCompactINR`** — so a count passed as a number prints `₹12`, not `12`. Pass every non-money value as a string (`` value={`${ready.length}` ``) and leave the prop numeric only for rupee figures.
24. **A rule applied in one caller but not in the function that owns it** — GSTR-4 excluded non-GST supplies from slab turnover while `quarterlyLiability` and the liability sum still counted them, so the same quarter read ₹1,40,000 on one card and ₹1,70,000 on another, and the page's own local "exempt" helper quietly re-added them. The page had a *second* implementation of the rule. Filter inside the function that owns it (`isNonGst` in `gstr4.ts`), export the derived figure (`exemptTurnover`), and never let a page recompute what the library already knows.
25. **A legal rate is a domain rule and cannot live in eight places** — GST 2.0 removed the 12% and 28% slabs and introduced 40% (22 Sep 2025), and eight pickers still offered `[0,5,12,18,28]`, so the app could not bill a demerit-rate item at all. Legal lists belong in one module (`src/lib/gst-rates.ts`) and callers import them. The one deliberate exception is `bill-text.ts`: it *detects* a rate printed on a supplier bill inside a 0.75-point tolerance, where 0.25% cannot be told apart from rounding noise on a no-tax row, so its list omits 0.25% and says why. Different jobs, documented — not drift.
26. **An undefined CSS custom property fails silently** — R2-23's token sweep replaced hex with `var(--green-ink)` / `--red-ink` / `--amber-ink`, and none of those exist in `globals.css` (there is `--green` and `--green-soft`, never an `-ink` pair). The declaration simply stops applying: no `tsc` error, no build error, no console error, and the note renders in inherited ink so it *looks* intentional. Audit mechanically — collect every `var(--x)` a page uses, subtract the `:root` block plus the page's own definitions, and require the difference to be empty.
27. **A grep for icon names matches colour words too** — the R2-23 icon audit reported `red` and `amber` as missing glyphs (they were literals inside `name={…}` ternaries), and a key regex like `^  '?([a-z0-9-]+)'?: ` over `Icons.tsx` also catches a `name:` field in a type, so it counted 67 for 66 real glyphs. Read the registry object for the truth, then read each page's actual `icon="…"` values.
28. **A loader that adds a virtual row breaks a count-based proof** — `loadGodowns` hands back the virtual `Main` beside the stored rows, so the vault's first godown `verify` (a length count) read one higher than the file held. A restore proof must count **identity, not length**: the rows that count are the stored ids that survived the parse and are still on the registry.
29. **Measure the library, not the seed** — the first vault suite was seeded with ten rows the owning modules' parsers would silently drop (a wrong `newEvent` argument order, `ends` where the LUT wants `to_date`, `code`/`foreign` where FX wants `currency`/`foreign_value`/`booked_rate`, a build with 0 units, a recipe with no output product, a challan without its deposit date), so every "restore" would have tested empty registers and passed anyway. Before asserting on a restore, push each seeded row back through its own parser and keep the suite honest about what it is measuring.
30. **`scrollWidth` on an element is not an overflow** — the R2-31 mobile hunt first blamed the `PageHeader` because its `scrollWidth` read 372 on a 344px viewport, but `.tf-pagehead` is `overflow: hidden` and the excess was its own decorative `::after { right: -60px }`, clipped and never reaching the document. An element only pushes the page sideways if **no ancestor clips or scrolls horizontally**, so the audit walks the ancestor chain checking `overflow-x` before it blames a width, and the final proof is `documentElement.scrollWidth == clientWidth`. The real offender was a `display: inline-flex` tab bar sizing to its five labels; `max-width: 100%` hands it back its own `overflow-x: auto`.
31. **A re-validating save path can strip a stored decision** — R2-35's build modal rebuilt the lines it wrote from the *candidate* on every save, so editing the note of a run whose movements had already landed ran the lot re-stamp and `validateSerialIssues()` against it and silently dropped the stored lot draws. Where a form re-derives what it will write, ask what it writes for a record that predates the re-derivation: the fix is `asBuilt ? check.linesStamped : draftExisting?.moved ? draftExisting.lines : candidate.lines` — a moved run's stored lines are the truth, never the candidate.
32. **A quarter key names the financial year, not the calendar year its last month falls in** — `quarterLabel()` took the supply month's year, so Q4 of FY 2026-27 (January–March **2027**) printed *"Q4 2027-28"*, and the first filings draft lost the same carry on the other side and dated a Q4 return a year early. Any quarter-derived due date has to carry the year its **months** fall in while labelling the year the **FY** names.
33. **A comparison that prints on agreement is worse than no comparison** — `expectedRows()`'s first drift sentence was built for every row, so a cited rule that *matched* the calendar printed a line saying the two dates differed. A drift/audit helper must be written from the disagreement branch outward, and its suite needs an agreeing case as well as a differing one; the agreeing case is the one that lies.
34. **A shared statutory figure can be a headline rather than an answer** — `/gst/calendar` prints "₹5,000" as the late-fee cap, which is the *middle* of the three turnover ceilings Notifications 19/20/21/2021 set (₹2,000 / ₹5,000 / ₹10,000, ₹500 on a nil return) and is not GSTR-9's cap at all. Where one page quotes a number another page computes by slab, the quoting page now points at the slab rather than being corrected into a second copy of it.
35. **A coverage list typed rather than counted is a caption** — R3-5's docs pass found `/audit`'s `GUARDED` still pointing at a **`/bills/new` route that has never existed**, and labelling `/journal` a page door when its own engine has held the door since R3-2b, because both sentences had been written by a human remembering the wiring. Every count on that page now comes off the array (`doorsAsking` / `doorsWriter` / `doorsEngine` / `doorsPage`) and the audit suite **re-reads the array out of the page source** to check each row's page really mentions the seal and each `href` resolves to a real `page.tsx`. A list that says what a control covers must be data a test can read, not prose a round updates by hand.
36. **A markdown edit anchored on the heading that follows it can eat the heading** — three times across this session family an `Edit` whose `old_string` ran into the next `---` + `##`/`###` line silently deleted that structure (README's feature paragraph, `1-PRD.md`'s `## 4. Non-Functional Requirements`, `5-Project-Tasks.md`'s `## PHASE 14 — Security ⏳`). Anchor on the **content** line you are replacing, keep any heading in both `old_string` and `new_string` verbatim when an insertion is unavoidable, and after a structural insert re-print the heading map (`grep -n "^## \|^### " <file>`) before moving on — the diff will not look wrong, only shorter.
37. **A count taken through a truncated pipe outlives the truncation** — R3-4's docs quoted "**75** route entries" from a build whose output had been piped through `tail`; re-measured at R3-5 with the full log kept, that build lists **68** rows (64 page routes + 2 `ƒ /api/*` + `/manifest.webmanifest` + `/_not-found`), and no line of it contains 75. The number was not wrong because the app changed, it was wrong because it was never countable. Pipe a build to a file when a figure will be quoted from it, keep the **disk** count (`find src/app -name page.tsx`, `-name route.ts`) as the citable figure — the root `src/app/page.tsx` is a `redirect('/login')` and earns no route row — and when an earlier round's figure cannot be reproduced, annotate it with the measurement rather than editing it silently, the way R3-5's pass did in all four places that carried the 75.

---

## Verification Environment

- Dev server on `http://localhost:3000`; browser-use drives it. The panel viewport is ~371–455 CSS px, so the native mobile breakpoint is what you actually see; inspect a desktop width through a same-origin 1440×900 iframe reading `getComputedStyle` / `scrollWidth`.
- Screenshots are not informative here — DOM assertions are authoritative. When `take_screenshot` and `click` are both unavailable (`NATIVE_BROWSER_VIEWPORT_UNAVAILABLE`), drive everything through `evaluate_script` with `.click()` and settle for computed style / `scrollWidth` instead of pixels.
- The ⌘K listener is attached to `document`, not `window` — dispatching the keydown on `window` does nothing.
- React controlled inputs are set through the native value setter plus `change`/`input` events; assigning `.value` alone leaves state stale.
- Test data is cleaned through the in-app Cancel / Deactivate flows, never by deleting rows in the dashboard.
- Test login credentials are for the local dev server only and must never be written into a file, source, config, fixture or commit. Do not read `.env`.

## Pending Decisions (Need Discussion)

| Decision | Options | Priority |
|----------|---------|---------|
| Real GSP provider | which GSP, contract, per-document cost — adapter is ready and waiting | High (blocks live e-invoice/e-way bill) |
| RLS approach | regenerate types + dashboard policies vs keep app-level isolation | High (before real customers) |
| Bank statement format coverage | paste-only vs per-bank templates; how many banks matter | Medium (R2-10 ships the generic parser; this shapes refinements) |
| Email provider for invoice delivery | Resend, SendGrid, Supabase SMTP | Medium |
| Multi-user model | org invites + RBAC roles vs single-seat | Medium |
| SaaS pricing | Free / Starter / Professional tiers, Razorpay | Low |
| Custom domain | taxflowindia.com | Low |

## Passwords and Credentials (Store Separately)

**DO NOT store actual credentials in any file in this repository — not here, not in
source, not in a test fixture, not in a commit message, not in `.env.local.bak`.**

Store them in the Mac Keychain, Notes, 1Password or Bitwarden:
- Supabase database password, project URL and anon key
- GitHub account credentials
- Vercel account credentials
- The local test login (usable only to sign in at `localhost:3000` for browser verification)
- Future: Razorpay API keys, GSP credentials, email provider key

---

## Quick Commands Reference

### Local development
```bash
cd ~/Desktop/taxflow-india
npm run dev
# Open: http://localhost:3000
```

### Verification before commit
```bash
npx tsc --noEmit
npm run build          # watch the route table: static / prerendered / dynamic
npx eslint <touched files>   # `next lint --file` no longer exists
```

### Pure-library tests (no browser, no app)
```bash
node --experimental-strip-types --no-warnings \
     --import /tmp/jr-register.mjs /tmp/<lib>-test.mjs
# --import works because jr-register.mjs is a *registrar* (it calls module.register() on
# jr-hooks.mjs, the resolve() hook). Importing the hook itself does nothing.
# The older `--loader /tmp/ts-loader.mjs` form still runs but is deprecated on this Node.
#
# Five suites live in /tmp right now (R3-5): audit 172 · filings 446 · journal 393 ·
# fold 250 · setup 131 = 1,392 assertions, 0 failing. /tmp is not in the repo and is
# unrecoverable once cleaned — re-record the counts every round, and re-run all five
# together so a fold that changes one engine cannot pass while breaking a neighbour.
```

### Deploy
```bash
cd ~/Desktop/taxflow-india
git status                    # review what is staged — never `git add .`
git add <files by name>
git commit -m "$(cat <<'EOF'
Feature: short description
EOF
)"
git push                      # Vercel auto-deploys in 2–3 minutes
```

### Inspect versions
```bash
npm list @supabase/ssr next react typescript
node --version
```

R2-16 B1 (`ccea4d5`) opened gap #4 with share-link statements; R2-18 B1 (`3fbea60`) closed gap #9 — recurring invoices — as an accruing schedule whose due dates are derived, never stored (ADR-025); **R2-19 B1 (`a7fd1b1`) closed gap #8 — multi-currency — as a dated rate ledger plus per-invoice currency deals that join to the books by invoice number, leaving the statutory rupee columns untouched (ADR-026).** **R2-20 B1 (`c5daa63`) took the `·` row of the matrix instead of the gap list** — job work and the ITC-04 workpaper, a section 143 clock beside the rule 55 challan, with the calendar deriving its rows from the register (ADR-027); **R2-21 B1 (`3a29c8f`) closed the cost-centre half of gap #10** — `/cost-centres` stores a *share* per document and re-reads the rupees from the books on every load, so an amended bill re-splits itself, the unassigned remainder is printed rather than lost, and a centre is treated as what it is: not a legal person, so a cross-state branch without a GSTIN becomes a rule 41A flag with a link to the GSTR-9 workpaper instead of an invented tax figure (ADR-028). **R2-22 (`f67f56f`) went to the matrix instead of the gap list** — `/tally` reads a Tally export in this browser into a *plan* (one row per ledger, stock item and voucher: `ready` / `blocked` / `skipped` / `already`, with reasons, labelled derivations and the file's own self-disagreements), writes only the rows you tick through `createInvoice` / `createPurchaseBill`, keeps our invoice numbering with the Tally reference travelling in `notes`, never writes money, and runs the same bridge backwards into the XML TallyPrime imports (ADR-029). **R2-23 (`dc41578`, colours tokenised in `a134408`) went back to the matrix for the §4 "Project / time tracking" row** — `/projects` logs hours against a job and attaches whole documents **by id**, stores **measurements only**, and re-reads every rupee on load through the `bookDocs()` reader cost centres already had, so amending or cancelling an invoice re-prices the job by itself; labour is **priced not costed** (contribution is billed revenue less attached spend, with the hour value beside it rather than inside it), "billed" is a **reference stamp** rather than a document (`createInvoice` still allocates the number, `/payments` still writes the money), and the 30-day service-invoice window is a **cited constant** from CBIC's invoicing handbook printed with a date, a rule and a link — never a tax figure (ADR-030). **R2-24 (`2c9eed7` engine, `fd9126d` page) took the matrix's manufacturing row** — `/inventory/recipes` holds recipes as **quantities** and re-reads every rupee from `products.purchase_price`, so correcting a price on the master re-values every recipe that uses it; a **build** records what was physically drawn and moves stock only through `record_stock_movement` on the existing `adjustment_*` types, all-or-nothing per build, and a build is **not a supply** — it never touches GSTR-1, because a plant that makes its own goods for its own GSTIN has no recipient, which is exactly the line `/gst/job-work` draws the other way (customer's goods, our process, section 143). The page answers *"what cannot I make today?"* before it answers *"what did it cost?"*: `planBuild()` names the component that limits the run, and the where-used tab turns a component into the list of outputs that would stop. Its own leftovers: a build never **back-flushes** from a sale (that needs the invoice line to know it came from a recipe), the roll-up was **material value only** until R2-28 priced the minutes from dated shop-rate rows (`bcdd305`), and there is no **production cost centre** join — the two registers meet only in the reader they both import. The explosion itself is real (`MAX_EXPLODE_DEPTH = 12`) but **greedy**: a component with an active recipe is treated as made, so its parts are what the plan asks for and a made row is never the blocker — which means a shop that buys that sub-assembly in has to archive its recipe rather than let the register decide (ADR-031). **R2-25 (`3e6582d` engine, `580086d` page) took the `·` half of §5** — `/gst/exports` keeps the *paper and the dates* an invoice has no column for: the shipping bill, the port, the destination, the undertaking it was filed under, the refund reference and the realisation credits, while the FOB value, the IGST and the balance are re-read from `invoices` **by invoice number** on every load. Two clocks run on each consignment and the register keeps them apart — the FEMA window (a **dated band table**, nine → fifteen → nine → fifteen months inside a year, each band citing its instrument, with the 13-11-2025 one-day disagreement printed as a question rather than resolved) and section 54(1)'s two-year refund clock, which for a LUT export is `nothing_to_claim` because there is no tax paid to claim back. The route to zero-rating is **recorded, never inferred**; proceeds have **one home** — `/fx` counts them when it holds the deal, and a credit typed beside that prints *not counted*, with `sources_diverge` naming both amounts instead of summing them; and **the missing paper is the product** — `unregisteredExports()` lists the export invoices with no file and `paperGaps()` says which document is absent and what hangs on it. `/gst/calendar` reads the same `watchRows()` output, so the two pages cannot disagree about a deadline (ADR-032). All of it on ADR-013 (owner bookkeeping → localStorage, no tables); the honest leftovers are the `products.current_stock` per-place rollup and auth'd customer logins, both waiting on schema. **Every gap that can be closed client-side now is** — **#6 payroll came off the blocked list the hard way: R2-31 (`140d1e0` engine, `9393529` page) built it as a register** (its session entry and ADR-034), which is why it no longer appears under "needs an external contract" here; **R2-37 (`86cc1ec` engine, `629be0b` page, Rule 37) then closed the attendance-to-job link** — the timesheet draws its hours from the kept day book: a person-month's marks arrive as proposed rows at the register's own `hours_per_day` (half a mark as half, every other mark refused with the register's own sentence, `ticks: null` meaning proposal), only the shop's tick writes, through the page's own `validateEntry`, and the drawn row names its person so it is costed the moment it lands — so what is left is narrower still: an hour nobody names stays priced only, and the mark itself still prices nothing), and the scanned *paper* behind an attached document needs Storage; R2-23 attaches the document itself by id) and exports — **filing** an RFD-11 or claiming the refund needs the portal, checking a shipping bill number needs ICEGATE, and a real realisation feed needs the AD bank, so v1 transcribes the reference and reports the gap in the paper rather than the answer (ADR-032's boundary; §8 #16 is the same row on the matrix, added with every vendor cell left `·` because the pages fetched were marketing copy, not feature pages). **R2-26 (`8aae49e` engine, `ba15288` page) closed the loop on ADR-013 itself** — `/vault` walks every device-side key the book holds (thirty at R2-26, **thirty-one since R2-31**) into one file the user owns: a named list rather than a sweep (so a session key cannot ride along), a per-block FNV-1a checksum (a hand-edit loses one register, not the book), a nine-state plan printed before anything is written, restores through each module's own `save*` proved by reading back through its `load*` (`file → here` on the report), a foreign-org file refused outright, and the five flow logs travelling as evidence only (ADR-033); `taxflow:invoice-template:` left `InvoiceDocument.tsx` for `src/lib/invoice-template.ts` because a preference a backup has to walk is data. **R2-28 (`bcdd305`) gave the recipe its time legs** — the bom register now also holds dated shop-rate rows, a recipe stores labour and overhead minutes per run, and each leg is priced at the rate in force on *the run's own day* (material at today's prices, time at the run's date — two bases that never add); a missing rate prints a dash and the reason, never a parts-only total in full-cost clothing (ADR-031 amended). **R2-29 (`7de09ee` engine, `11adb45` page) made a run say where its units came from** — a build line carries its lot picks, `allocateLots()` offers FEFO inside the run's own godown and the shop is free to overrule or name nothing (a warning, not a refusal), the picks are re-checked against the live batch register at the moment of writing so a sale made elsewhere cannot be talked around, each draw mirrors into `taxflow:batchmoves:` as an `adjust` move with the job in `doc_ref`, and the finished units land as a lot whose `batch_no` *is* the job number — with `drawnElsewhereFrom()` refusing to cancel a run that has already been sold on (ADR-031 amended again). **R2-30 (`2b72431` engine, `d49c506` page) ended where the buying begins** — the materials board folds its shortage into one row per component (`mergedShortages`, the same fold the stat card reads, so the screen and the paper cannot be two sums), groups it under the vendor each part last came from at that bill's rate or the catalogue's when no bill names one, prints the three cases it will not route (never supplied, supplier gone from the master, vendor card with no state) with their reasons instead of dropping them, and hands the rest to the purchase-order register's own `raiseDrafts()` so a draft from the board is numbered by that register's high-water mark and confirmed only there. One unpriced line and the order prints no value at all — dash on the card, sentence in the note, blank in the CSV — and nothing is read from the books until the shop clicks, because a shortage is a question and an order is a promise (ADR-031 amended a third time). **R2-31 (`140d1e0` engine, `9393529` page) took the last `·` in §4 that this app can build in the owner's browser** — `/payroll` stores six monthly components and four exceptions per person and re-walks every rupee on load: section 192 runs as a year-walk (`taxWalk()`) rather than a percentage, so a June bonus lifts July onward and the March row absorbs whatever the estimate under-collected; the PF ceiling (₹15,000 → ₹25,000 from **17-09-2026**, S.O. 5109(E)) and the unchanged ESI bands are dated tables that disagree on the same payslip and print the disagreement rather than choosing; four refusals carry their own sentences (a financial year the tables do not hold, the senior rate bands no fetched source gave, lost days above the month's own length, and a person with months recorded, who goes off the rolls rather than being deleted); absence is a dash and zero is a claim (`pt: number | null`); the EPS pensionable ceiling, the state professional-tax table and the labour codes' 50% wage floor stay questions with citations beside them; `payrollEvents()` lets `/gst/calendar` drop its guessed monthly PF/PT rows for any month the books actually file, and puts the undated PT row in a *State dates* rail and out of the `.ics`; `costPerHour()` hands `/projects` the rupee an hour costs without folding it into contribution; nothing moves money, no schema is touched, and the register entered the vault as its **thirty-first** spec (ADR-034). **R2-32 (`79e985b` engine, `2942fa9` page, `27388e1` keywords) deepened that register rather than adding one** — `/payroll`'s month run now reads the shop's own attendance sheet from a paste: `planAttendance()` prints the columns it found, the basis it settled on and every row it will refuse, `applyAttendance()` is the only door and re-runs the month form's own validator inside the write, and what reaches storage is one `lop_days` figure plus a stamped sentence in the row's note — the sheet itself is thrown away, so **no new key, no new route, library or glyph, and `PAYROLL_VERSION` stays 1**. Four refusals carry it: a number whose header names no direction (present and lost are opposite salaries), a row whose two day columns contradict each other, a person the sheet forgot (a finding, never a zero), and an identity that has to be earned — code, exact name, then one *unique* substring reported as `approximate`. The sheet's own stated month length outranks `settings.working_days`; the reader is `detectDelimiter()` / `splitRow()` borrowed from `bank-statement.ts`; and `attendanceTemplate()` rules the month's roster with the register's own codes so a return paste cannot fail on a spelling (ADR-034 amended). **R2-33 (`ac14df8` engine, `6f68291` page) laid that reading out as paper** — `/payroll/payslip` prints one person's month, or the whole month as a pack, as A4 slips whose every rupee comes from the engine's own computation: `buildPayslip()` re-derives nothing, and its suite polices the tie (`earnings_total = monthly + arrears + bonus`, `deduction_total = loss of pay + statutory legs`, `employer_cost − net_pay = employer legs + deductions − loss of pay`); the slip says *Net payable* rather than paid and prints its four boundaries beside every sheet (**this is not Form 16**, nothing here is filed); a code nobody typed is a dash with the sentence naming what it would be needed for, and the **letterhead** — 15 lines, four of them statutory identifiers — is asked for and never invented, typed inside `taxflow:payroll:<orgId>` because a heading is not a register (no new key, `PAYROLL_VERSION` stays 1); a refused month prints **no figures at all** with its Print/PDF disabled and the engine's reason as the title; the shop's own exceptions sit in a `.no-print` strip so the employee's copy carries only its facts, and `monthPack()` returns the refusals with `partial` so a pack is never silently a subset; one person or one month goes out as one PDF through the new `downloadElementsAsPdf()` — and nothing is emailed, acknowledged or signed (ADR-034 amended again; 394 assertions in `/tmp/payslip-test.mjs`, with the payroll, attendance and shopping suites re-run green). R2-12's design-system pass stays complete — 4 lists (`bills`, `credit-notes`, `debit-notes`, `quotations`) still hand-code their chrome and get migrated when next touched. **Round 3 has since taken the order the PDF review sets** — **R3-1** (`e5ab22a`, ADR-035 / Rule 38) closed the append-only audit trail and the financial period locks, **R3-2** (`297d8bb`, ADR-036 / Rule 39) the branch & document-series register with opening balances that stand on a stated day, and **R3-2b** (`571e7bc`, ADR-037 / Rule 40) the manual journal, folded into both statements and guarded by the same seal, and **R3-4** (`c5cbdb8` code, `d36b3f8` docs, ADR-038 / Rule 41) the filings & amendments register — what each return stated, the late-fee slab that applies to it, and the window an amendment still fits — and **R3-5** (code `bbd1913`, docs `8617154`, ADR-039 / Rule 42) put the period seal **on the writers** rather than on the forms, taking the app to **67 route files (65 pages + 2 api) · 54 libraries · 10 components · 74 glyphs · 35 vault specs (30 restorable)** — a tally R3-6 and R3-7 both left exactly where it stands, since neither added a route, key, glyph, component, spec or page. What these rounds deliberately left open is named rather than hidden: **R3-5 closed the two-door limit R3-1 and R3-2b recorded** — the question now sits in `create-invoice.ts`, `create-bill.ts` and `creditnote.ts`, so twelve paths inherit it, six more are carried by their page, and `/audit` prints nineteen of its twenty-one rows as asking with two open on purpose; what stays outside is **any write this app does not own**, because the door is in this app's writers and not in the database (R3-3 is where that could move). `/journal` files nothing, settles nothing and reaches no bank, and no return reads a journal row; **the two copy-level follow-ups R3-2b recorded are both closed** — `--mono` is defined in `globals.css` and `.jn-mono` reads it, and the vault's `plural()` learned `ies`, so the projects spec reads *"3 time entries"* — while the **64 monospace spellings in 37 files** the new token does not yet reach stay open cleanup (re-measured at R3-7's docs pass — the earlier *52 in 32* counted only the 45 class-rule spellings over 28 files and guessed the 19 inline `fontFamily` props over 11 files; see §R3-7's entry and `4-Design-System.md` §4). **R3-6** (`43bac75` code, `220892e` + `363bbaa` docs, ADR-040 / Rule 43) taught the calendar emitter rule 61(1)(ii)'s four quarters, the IFF windows and the PMT-06 day, shared one `qrmpCategory()` between emitter and register, and struck six citations that no primary text supported; **R3-7** (ADR-041 / Rule 44) gave that deposit its rupee — `depositFor()` folds a filed 3B row into a labelled 35% ceiling (Notification 85/2020-Central Tax dated 10.11.2020), the shop's own `cash:<quarter>` figure stored beside the filing ticks on the existing compliance envelope upgrades it, and the card says *state*, never *this is what you owe*. **R3-4 left its own edges named rather than hidden**: the register never asks the portal — no GSP feed, no ARN verification, no filing, no payment — and five things stay `NOT_TRACKED` with their rule beside them (that portal feed, the IFF, the PMT-06 deposit a QRMP filer owes under rule 61(3), refunds and DRC notices, and any effect on the statements); `compliance.ts` still emits monthly rows for a quarterly filer and no PMT-06 row, so the quarter fold lives in this register and teaching the emitter is deferred; the 22nd/24th state-code table carries only the registrations this round could source; and **the live UI drive is still owed** — the browser session had no signed-in org, so the register is engine-verified (446 assertions) and CSS-audited, not driven end-to-end. Step **(3)** of the review's order is the P0 backend — item 3 below.
2. **The browser pass — what a script still cannot do.** GST batch 6 (`dd7d449`), `gst/gstr4` (`da672a2`) and every round since R2-20 were verified statically at build time; the Supabase dev session came back on 2026-10-09 and has stayed up — those pages were driven live, R2-30's *shortage → `PO-2026-27-0001` → cancel* chain ran end to end, and R2-31's five payroll tabs plus the calendar's new behaviour were seen against a seeded register that was then removed, and **Reminder 12's measurement ran too — zero horizontal overflow at 355px and 1440px on every page measured**, read through a same-origin iframe pinned at the target width (see the verification session above). What is left is *visual*: screenshots, click-through and a human eye on composition need the in-app Browser panel opened — while its surface reports a `0×0` viewport, `click` and `take_screenshot` fail with `NATIVE_BROWSER_VIEWPORT_UNAVAILABLE` and interaction goes through injected script. **R3-2b found the second half of that limitation**: the panel's own session can be signed out mid-round, and then a page renders the sign-in wall so a live figure cannot be measured at all — the honest fallback is to read the device's register through injected script (`localStorage` is still reachable when React is showing the login screen) and to substitute a **static CSS audit** for the measured width pass, recording the substitution as a substitution rather than as a pass (now written into `2-System-Architecture.md` §15).
3. **Phase 14 — Security**: generated Supabase types, tightened dashboard RLS policies, audit log, multi-account isolation test. Do this before onboarding a real customer. **This is R3-3 on the roadmap** — the review's step (3): durable data, tenant isolation, membership RBAC — and it stays a **boundary** until the hand-managed Supabase schema work is authorised (the schema is managed in the dashboard, and no migrations ship from this repo). Note that the *audit log* half has a client-side answer already (R3-1's `/audit` trail, ADR-035); what remains here is the server-side one, which is a different thing and cannot be faked by the register.
4. **Real GSP integration** once a provider is chosen (ADR-016) — plus email invoices and multi-user, which need the pending decisions above.

---

## Important Reminders

1. **RLS is disabled or partial** — no real customer data before Phase 14.
2. **Nothing files a return.** Every GST page is a workpaper; the user transcribes or uploads it and verifies at gst.gov.in.
3. **GSP submission is a mock** — the adapter is real, the transport is not. Never call it live.
4. **AI runs in the browser** — no uploads, no server model, per-field confidence on every extracted value.
5. **Schema is hand-managed** — ship the feature client-side (ADR-013) unless a new column is genuinely unavoidable.
6. **Documents are signed** — credit notes reduce, they never invert.
7. **Stock changes only through `record_stock_movement`**, and a product edit must never reset `current_stock`.
8. **GST rates come from the database**, never hard-coded, and a rate change must not touch historical invoices. The only static list is the picker in `src/lib/gst-rates.ts` — add a rate there when a notification changes one, and check `bill-text.ts`'s detection list at the same time.
9. **Profit figures are indicative**, not a certified P&L.
10. **New pages must be in `nav.ts`** or they do not exist for the user or ⌘K.
11. **Test GSTIN** — `27AABCU9603R1ZX` (valid format, not a real registration).
12. **Verify in the browser before committing**, at phone width and 1440px, with zero horizontal overflow.
13. **A Tally file is evidence, not instruction** (ADR-029) — an import plans first, writes only ticked rows through the real document paths, keeps our numbering, and never writes money. Anything unresolvable **blocks** rather than guessing; an object whose write path is on another page is *skipped with a reason*, never coerced.
14. **A client-side register never stores a rupee it can re-read** (ADR-028, ADR-030, ADR-031) — cost centres keep a *share*, projects keep *measurements*, recipes keep *quantities and minutes*, and each takes its money from the record that already owns it: the first two from `costcentres.bookDocs()`, a recipe's parts from `products.purchase_price` and its minutes from the dated shop-rate rows the same register holds, the field `/accounting` values stock at. That reader has one owner on purpose: a second copy of the cancelled-row skip or the credit-note sign is a second opinion that can disagree with a return. The R2-29 extension of the same idea, one notch narrower: a build's **lot picks are kept but its lot *balances* never are** — the picks are a record of what the shop decided, and the balance they are checked against is re-read from `taxflow:batches:` + `taxflow:batchmoves:` at the moment of writing, so a sale made in another tab while a draft sat open blocks the run instead of being quietly ignored. R2-31 pushes the same idea one notch further (ADR-034): the salary register stores **no rupee at all** — not a share, not a measurement, only structure (six components, four exceptions a month) — and every figure on a slip is a fresh walk of the year.
15. **An undefined CSS variable is silent** — `var(--nope)` is neither a type error nor a console error; the declaration just stops applying and the element inherits. After touching a page's `<style>`, diff every `var(--x)` it uses against the `:root` block in `globals.css`, and prefer `--green` / `--amber` / `--red` over their `-soft` grounds: there are **no `--*-ink` tokens** in this project.
16. **A library validator is not a stock gate** (ADR-031) — read the body, not the name. `validateBuild()` checks the *record* and never reads `current_stock`; `planBuild()` reports a shortfall without refusing one; `cancelBuild()` flips the status and **leaves `moved: true`** on the row. A flag the library does not clear is a claim the next reader believes, so the screen refuses its own over-draws and writes its own reversals. R2-29 made that second half literal: `validateLotIssues()` checks a draft against the lots it names, while `recordRun()` re-reads the live register and does the refusing, and it is the page — not `cancelBuild()` — that writes `reverseLotWritesFor()` and zeroes the finished lot, guarded by `drawnElsewhereFrom()` so a run whose output has been sold on cannot be reversed at all.
17. **A statutory period a regulator can amend is a dated table, not a constant** (ADR-032) — `REALISATION_BANDS` / `ADVANCE_BANDS` pick the window from the **date the clock starts on** (date of export, date the advance was received), never from today, and each band carries its `source` instrument, its `url` and its `note`. Where two sources disagree about the day a rule changed the band keeps a `disputed` range and the page prints it **as a question** — choosing a side silently is a guess the user cannot see. A per-file override is allowed and labelled as an override wherever it renders. The calendar half of the same idea: a feature's deadlines are *read from that feature* (`watchRows()`), and a non-GST default gets `penalty.note`, never `lateFeePerDay`, because the ₹-per-day estimator has no statutory rate for a FEMA contravention and inventing one is Rule 27's fake figure in calendar clothing. R2-31 moved the same shape from a *period* to a *rate*: `PF_WAGE_CEILINGS` is ₹15,000 up to 16-09-2026 and ₹25,000 from 17-09-2026 (S.O. 5109(E)), a run picks its ceiling from **its own date** and the month CSV carries *PF ceiling used* as a column, and ESI — still ₹21,000 — is printed as the disagreement it is rather than harmonised away.
18. **A backup walks a named list; it never sweeps the browser** (ADR-033) — a key that is not in `vault.REGISTERS` is not in the file even if it is on the device, because a sweep would copy `sb-*` session keys. A restore goes through each module's own `save*` and is proved by reading back through its `load*` — never a direct `localStorage.setItem`, never "a write that returned no error". The five flow logs are collected as evidence and never written back; the vault's own log is not in the vault; and a new register must be added to `REGISTERS` in the same round that creates its key, or the next backup silently omits it.
19. **A control goes on the writer, and the banner only reads** (ADR-039 / Rule 42) — a guard wired into a form governs only the forms thought of that day: twelve of this app's dated paths reach the books through `create-invoice.ts`, `create-bill.ts` or `creditnote.ts`, so the seal is asked there, **above the sequence-number read**, because a refusal that burned a number left a hole in a statutory series. Three forms, chosen by who has to act on the answer: `sealGuard()` **reads** (and is what `SealNotice.tsx` may use — a banner appearing is not an attempt, and only an attempt belongs on the append-only trail), `askSeal()` **reads and logs**, `requireOpenSeal()` **throws** `SealedWriteError` for a caller with no screen. A disabled button is not a fourth form. And a list that claims coverage is **data a suite reads back** (`/audit`'s `GUARDED`, its counts computed off the array) — never prose a round updates by hand.
